# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1987

## Śrīla Prabhupādadeva-lokopadhyāyāṣṭaka by Kuśakratha dāsa

### Text 1

*āmoditah pāda-lasat-saroja-*  
*yugena kṛṣṇasya pareṇa lokaḥ*  
*yad-yatnato gopapati-priyasya*  
*namāmi tam śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*āmoditaḥ*-fragrant; *pāda*-feet; *lasat*-splendid; *saroja*-lotus; *yugena*-by the pair; *kṛṣṇasya*-of Lord Kṛṣṇa; *pareṇa*-transcendental; *lokaḥ*-the world; *yad-*of whom; *yatnataḥ*-by the efforts; *gopapati-priyasya*-of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the beloved son of the king of the *gopas*; *namṁi*-I offer my respectful obeisances; *tam*-to him; *śrī-prabhupādadevam*-His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, by whose efforts this world is now fragrant with the remembrance of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.**

## Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Svāti dāsī and Ṣaṣthī dāsī

Since time immemorial, pilgrims who go to the River Ganges for purification stand half submerged in her waters. In a mood of devotion they take palmfuls of the Ganga’s own water and, with supplication and prayers, offer it back to her. Similarly, those who have received the mercy of *guru*, for their own purification, gather that mercy, which is the be all and end-all of their life, and offer it back to him, feeling that they have no other possession to give him.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has written, *śrī guru caraṇakamala bhaja man, guru-kṛpā vinā nahi koi sādhana bal, bhaja man bhaja anukṣan:* “O mind, just worship the lotus feet of Śrī Guru! Without the mercy of the *guru* there is no power to execute the regulative practices of *bhakti.* Therefore, O mind, just worship him at every moment.”

Due to the distraction of the illusory energy, a devotee may find himself bereft of the opportunity of such constant service and remembrance; therefore once a year, on the anniversary of the *guru’s* appearance, the disciples gather together and offer worship, prayers, and obeisances. This function is known as Vyāsa-pūjā.

Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the literary incarnation of God, composed the Vedic scriptures and specifically the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the essence of all truths, and through his disciplic succession that knowledge has been transmitted. The bona fide *guru* who is thus empowered to receive knowledge in this line is offered this ceremony of Vyāsa-pūjā and may also sit on the *vyāsāsana.*

In the sixteenth century, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu churned the ocean of pure devotional service and brought forth the means of deliverance for the conditioned souls of the Kali-yuga: Their divine *saṅkīrtana*-chanting the holy names of the Lord.

It was the desire of Caitanya Mahāprabhu that the *saṅkīrtana-yajña* should be propagated throughout the whole world. He empowered His devotees to assist Him in various ways. To teach His philosophy of *bhakti,* Śrī Nitāi went to Bengal; in Purī there were Rāmānanda Rāya and Svarūpa Dāmodara, and the Gosvāmīs were sent to Vṛndāvana and deputed to write books on *bhakti-yoga.* Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja, Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura, Lokanātha Gosvāmī, and other contemporaries wrote about Mahāprabhu’s life and precepts. Following them, Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, and others throughout the centuries continued the tradition. But al these great *ācāryas* stayed within the confines of Bhārata-varṣa (India), and thus the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu lay bound up within the lotus bud of Vraja-Gauramaṇdalabhūmi, not reaching the far shores of the *pāścātya-deśap* (Western countries).

At the dawning of the modern world, Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda conceived of furthering the teachings of Caitanya Mahāprabhu by writing some treatises on *bhakti-yoga* in English. This was further nurtured by his son Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī, who became the most bold and erudite Vaiṣṇava scholar of his time. And then it was that Crīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī gave Śrīla Prabhupāda the order to preach and print books in English, and thus through Crīla Prabhupāda’s lifetime of intense devotion and dedication, the lotus bud of Mahāprabhu’s mercy unfurled, and only now is it complete, as the holy name of the Lord is being spread to every town and village. For this reason Śrīla Prabhupāda is the most unique and beloved *guru* of the entire *sampradāya* in the line of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

The divine characteristics of such a person as Śrīla Prabhupāda are revealed by Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura in his song *Śrī guru-caraṇ kamal bhaja mana:*

*kṛṣṇa-kṛpā ki ānanda mṛti dīnanan kauna nidhān*  
*jñāna-bhakti prema tina prakāśata śrī-guru patita-pavana*  
*śruti smṛti aurapurāna mahi kinho spaṣta pramāṇa*  
*tana mana jīvana pade arpane sadā harināma ratan*

**“Śrī Gurudeva is the personification of the bliss of Kṛṣṇā’s mercy. He is the storehouse of compassion for the poor and suffering souls. He reveals divine knowledge, pure devotion, and ecstatic love of God, and he is the savior of the fallen souls. Therefore offer your body, mind, and very life at the feet of Śrī Guru, and always chant the divine name of Śrī Hari.”**

## Introduction by Kīrtirāja dāsa

According to material vision, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda passed away on November 14, 1977. However, according to spiritual vision, a higher vision, our most beloved father, friend, and master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, lives on.

The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, says in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* “For the soul there is neither birth nor death at any time. He has not come into being, does not come into being, and will not come into being. He is unborn, eternal, ever-existing, and primeval. He is not slain when the body is slain.” (Bg. 2.20)

According to this statement and others by Lord Kṛṣṇa, we can understand that Śrīla Prabhupāda is still living somewhere, in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness, executing pure loving service to his most worshipable Lord and his eternal spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda.

But, we can understand something even more about the continuing existence of Śrīla Prabhupāda. That is, that Śrīla Prabhupāda is still living with us, actively inspiring and engaging us in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He is ever present by his divine instructions and will remain so, provided we maintain and expand upon our loving relationship with him. “The spiritual master lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.”

This continuance of association with Śrīla Prabhupāda is, however, dependent upon us. It is dependent upon the fact that we strictly follow the teachings and instructions that Śrīla Prabhupāda has given us and that we work to push on his mission according to our ability. Just as in the case of Hanumān and the spider: Hanumān was throwing big boulders, and the spider was pushing tiny grains of sand. Each was serving his Lord according to his ability, and the Supreme Personality of Godhead recognized this. Śrīla Prabhupāda used to say that “Kṛṣṇa does not see how much you are giving, but He is seeing how much you are holding back.” While performing our service, however large or small it may be, we must never forget this most important fact that we are actually serving the Supreme Personality of Godhead by serving His most intimate and confidential servant, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

This yearly Vyāsa-pūjā book is not simply a yearbook of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. It is an offering of love and devotion made to the most important person in our lives, our eternal spiritual master. It is also an opportunity for all of us to reaffirm the commitment that we made at the time of our first initiation, that under Śrīla Prabhupāda most expert guidance, inspiration, and devotion to the Supreme Lord, we are dedicating our lives to the service of our spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa.

It is inevitable that so many difficulties and obstacles, both external and internal, will confront us while on this path back home, back to Godhead. Such obstacles were there at the time of Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya as well. Why should we think that we will not face these problems? Śrīla Prabhupāda often said that there is danger at every step. As the years go on, more and more of us can testify to the truth in this statement. And, just as in the case of the cowherd boys, who had the faith that if they surrendered to and depended upon Kṛṣṇa then everything would be all right. He would protect them; then this should also be our attitude. If we, disciples and granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda alike, surrender to the instructions and desires of Śrīla Prabhupāda, then we are sure to please him and make progress toward reaching the ultimate goal of becoming fully Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Śrīla Prabhupāda often stressed that the real association with one’s spiritual master is not through the spiritual master’s personal association but through his instructions. Śrīla Prabhupāda left us volumes and volumes of instructions, probably more instructions than any other teacher, material or spiritual, has left for his students. Now we have to find out what those instructions and teachings are by reading and studying Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, and we have to absorb those teachings into our lives. In all respects, they have to become the very basis of our existence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us everything. He didn’t just make the plans for building a house in which the whole world would be able to live, but he actually constructed that house. Now we have to maintain that same house. Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted that we at least be able to maintain what he had given us. This is not an insignificant or simple task. Śrīla Prabhupāda established an organization and institution that he expected to last for the next ten thousand years – and not to just exist for that period of time, but to vigorously spread the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every corner of the globe. We have a task to perform that is certainly beyond the scope of our imagination. But in order to come anywhere near realizing the goal of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission, we must cooperate. This was Śrīla Prabhupāda’s final statement to us, that our love for him would be shown by how much we cooperated together after he was gone. We must see to it that this mission of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s is successful. Actually, we are not the doers, we are not the controllers. Kṛṣṇa is the controller. We have to qualify ourselves to become instruments in the hands of Kṛṣṇa and Śrīla Prabhupāda. The master craftsman or surgeon may be the most qualified in the world, but if his tools are inadequate or primitive, then the work will be hindered. We must not hinder the mission of Śrīla Prabhupāda either by our material desires, the influence of our false ego, or our lacking of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The duty of the disciple is to assist his *guru* in serving his spiritual master. This is the perfection of our lives.

We pray that we may all become precision instruments in the hands of Śrīla Prabhupāda, working to cut out the illusion of material happiness and sense gratification from the hearts of conditioned souls and replacing that suffering condition with the blissfulness of devotional service. But, as Śrīla Prabhupāda said, the highest principle is that one has to save oneself first. If we don’t do this, then our attempt to save others, no matter how sincere that attempt may be, will not go on forever. We must seriously take to the process that Śrīla Prabhupāda has given us and get that process to work on ourselves. In the beginning of our movement, Śrīla Prabhupāda said that he would consider his mission successful if he could deliver one person back to Godhead. Let’s assist Śrīla Prabhupāda in his mission and make his glories known throughout the three worlds by delivering unlimited living entities back to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, starting with ourselves.

All glories to His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Kīrtirāja dāsa

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Agrāṇī Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Each day I offer obeisances and a flower at your lotus feet. In so doing I am reminded of the perfection for which my whole life aspires – to become your pure servant. I understand the obeisances as a gesture of surrender of my body, mind, and words in obedience to your instructions. And the flower represents my heart, which I offer to you with all my love.

I feel greatly fortunate to be in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and thereby able to perform this daily devotion, for there is no more suitable person than you to whom to surrender myself, body and soul. As Lord Caitanya’s greatest emissary, you are delivering the whole world, and I have somehow also been favored by your boundless mercy. During rare moments of clear consciousness and illumination, I am carried to a wonderful awareness of how immensely fortunate one is to have you as his spiritual master. At such times I am moved to tears of happiness and gratitude, and I want to proclaim to the world your joyful message and glorious personality. These are treasured moments, which I take to be glimpses of what a true loving relationship with you actually is, and I hanker for that day when these special moments will blossom into uninterrupted reality.

As your great gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is infinite, I am infinitely indebted to you. How can I possibly repay you for what you have given me? At this stage, in my daily offerings of obeisances and flowers, I can only pretend to be giving myself to you in complete love and surrender. Consequently, I am very ashamed that I fall so terribly short of reciprocating the love you freely bestow upon me. Thus I pray that, lowly and fallen as I am, you kindly accept my awkward and inadequate expressions of devotion to your lotus feet.

You are the most wonderful person of all. Despite my many *anarthas*, I want more than anything else in the world to serve you, to be with you, and to love you forever.

*Your grateful servant,*  
Agrāṇī Swami

## Balavanta dāsa

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

When you gave us Kṛṣṇa, you gave us everything, but your legacy is living and gives us the chance to go on giving back to you.

As a preacher of *Bhāgavata-dharma*, you are unsurpassed and unequaled, and therefore you are our *ācārya*. Pitāmah, you have given the framework of absolute peace and universal joy.

But, as Lord Caitanya spread His teachings throughout India, kindly leaving the rest of the world for ISKCON, you have given a philosophical basis mercifully leaving its implementation undone.

Like young *brahmacārīs* with no life other than Vedic study, you blessed us with years of unimpeded absorption in pure Kṛṣṇa realization, our only task to associate with you and assist in the publication of books in the creation of your mission.

Now, give us the qualification to do you some service in the establishment of your vision. Let us helpt to build the house for which you have left the plans.

Let us in our lifetimes see *brāhmaṇas,* pure and principled, with only the asset of good quality, teach the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Let us see the beginnings of *gotras* for such persons with traditions of purity and simplicity. Let us take pleasure in the establishment of transcendental centers of learning to secure your teachings through every discipline. Let these institutions extend beyond the length and breadth of one man or generation, although each may leave his mark according to stature. Let these hermitages be for the education of saints, where parents compete for admission, fully confident in the result.

Let us see sages, learned and gentle, traveling in the ten directions, enlightening the world. Let them be free of blemish, highly respected, emissaries of spiritual truth, unencumbered by ordinary perceptions, covetousness, and all forms of mundane duality. Let their intellectual strength and personal purity be a bold challenge to all materialistic philosophies.

Let us see your followers living peacefully and happily under the protection of selfless administrators and executive heads whose only purpose is the protection of others and who readily risk all personal gain to remove the threefold miseries from the heads of the *prajā.*

Let us see agricultural development of abundant production in grains, vegetables, and fruits exceeding all needs for use, distribution, and sale. Let us see cows without anxiety in verdant fields of plenty. Let us see successful business enterprise sown in the fertile ground of generosity and charity. Let us see the old, the sick, women, children, and especially the *brāhmaṇas* and saintly persons protected through knowledge that all excesses are meant for distribution.

Let us also see servants and assistants unexploited, secure, and without need. Let us see them proud in their honest labor, trusting and trustworthy, not blindly but with good cause, and with the intelligence to give service where it will come to good, not ruin. In short, let us see the ISKCON that you saw.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us develop, and let us have a part. Ultimately, give us your greatest blessing: give us service, give us service, give us service.

*Your servant,*  
Balavanta dāsa

## Bhakticāru Swami

If I do not remain true to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
I’ll lose everything that I spiritually gained.  
Then my heart will become hard as thunder,  
And my consciousness will become eternally maimed.

With a little knowledge about the spiritual sky  
I’ll think myself to be a liberated soul.  
Not knowing that I am just a conceited fool,  
I’ll forget forever my ultimate goal.

Lust, greed, anger, and other vices  
With their incredible force will invade my heart.  
Soldiers of Māyā, enemy of Kṛṣṇa,  
They will mercilessly tear my consciousness apart.

I’ll fall out of your grace, which flooded the earth  
With love of Kṛṣṇa, the most magnanimous gift.  
An unfortunate soul, not knowing my goal,  
In the ocean of suffering I’ll drift.

If I do not remain true to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
I’ll want to occupy the seat you adorn;  
Knowing not how to struggle for Kṛṣṇa,  
In the abyss of anguish I’ll be thrown.

Forgetting that I am your eternal servant  
I’ll try to lord over everything that I see,  
Lost as I am since time immemorial  
In the dismal world of agony.

Thinking you to be an ordinary soul  
I’ll try like a clown to imitate you.  
Seducing the souls that sincerely want Kṛṣṇa  
I’ll try to become a cheating guru.

Māyā will offer me all that I want  
—wealth, women, and name and fame.  
Thinking that they are the result of my devotion,  
I’ll try to justify my deceptive claim.

If I do not remain true to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
I’ll surround myself with a bunch of fools.  
Thinking myself to be as great as you are,  
I’ll join one of those deviant schools.

Not recognizing my genuine friends  
Who criticize me when I do something wrong,  
I’ll stay with the ones who flatter me  
To make my position deceitfully strong.

Forgetting your glories as the Lord’s chosen one,  
I’ll try to eclipse you, as Rāhu does moon.  
In my treacherous attempt to compete with you  
I’ll become bereft of the Lord’s greatest boon.

Māyā then will offer me enough rope  
To hang myself with wealth and fame.  
Smearing myself with the stool of pig  
I’ll forget Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.

If I ever deviate from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please bring me back to your lotus feet.  
Now that I have realized the consequence,  
Help me become free from Māyā’s deceit.

To follow your order is such an easy job,  
And the reward is as high as the spiritual sky.  
Four regulative principles and chanting of names  
Will take us back to Godhead when we die.

My will is weak and heart so impure,  
But still I want to receive your grace.  
My only hope is the blessings of the Vaiṣṇavas  
And the blows from your divine mace.

Let me be with you wherever you are  
And serve you so that I can be  
A speck of dust at your lotus feet  
To regain my eternal identity.

*Your most unworthy servant,*  
Bhakticāru Swami

## Bhaktitīrtha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Every day another associate from ISKCON joins us, and I am beginning to wonder who those rare few were who went directly to the spiritual kingdoms. Here we are, Śrīla Prabhupāda, enjoying like anything in the heavenly planets. Most of us have wonderful empires. We have beautiful bodies that have tremendous longevity. We’re surrounded by luxuries, extremely potent intoxicants, and gorgeous damsels. We are all, however, suffering tremendously, as we are slaves to all these opulences.

I don’t know how the others got here, but I can clearly see how I built my staircase to this heavenly dungeon.

I remember you so often stressing that we are not the body. I, however, found myself many times thinking, “Why does he repeat this so much?” I remember you attacking the impersonalists with tumultuous vigor, but I could not at that time appreciate the significance of these attacks. I recall you pleading with us to give up sense gratification once and for all. In those days I thought that you were speaking mainly to the nondevotees, and I did not realize these instructions were for us.

Amazingly, you wrote in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that there is an invisible line between the devotees and the demigods. I remember especially you explaining how the pure devotee, the spiritual master, is the sum total of the demigods. You informed us how demigods coordinate practically every detail of the day-to-day existence of the conditioned soul, such as his respiration, defecation, digestion, and even the blinking of the eye. When you elaborated upon the constant war between the *asuras* and the *devas*, somehow in those days it all seemed irrelevant or too theoretical.

But then, during that period in the late 1990s, when *asuras* started attacking the devotees more vigorously worldwide and the demigods came back, I could understand that all along you had been preparing us to help change this world. We had become too engrossed in making *material* changes, overreacting and arguing like Harvard lawyers, while increasing our *aparādhas* and sense gratification year after year, that we were totally unprepared to withstand the attacks of the demons and to engage properly in spiritual statecraft, planetcraft, as Kṛṣṇa consciousness demands, since this is a movement for establishing a new world order based on the autocracy of the Vedic cosmology. Now not only did we see the demigods but we also saw their sovereign strength and jurisdictions. But due to our lack of becoming more internally connected with you, we are mostly now prisoners of the heavenly dungeons….our devotion could take us only this far.

I have been informed that on earth they are having a Vyāsa-pūjā celebration for you. My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if I could be present there or communicate properly with those still around, how much I would warn them not to make the mistake that I and those who are with me here made. I would especially try to encourage them not to over- or underreact, but to go on taking your messages as our life and soul, because in time, if we persevere, all things will become clarified. I think this would be the way I would try to glorify you on your Vyāsa-pūjā, for you are the one who is the sum total of the demigods.

What ridiculous mistakes I made. Now I am sentenced to luxurious sufferings and harassments decade after decade as a prisoner of the heavenly dungeon. O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if only I had another chance! You did in fact show us and give us everything. Kṛṣṇa’s taking you away substantiated that you had actually given us everything we needed to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. You were the perfect *ācārya,* as you showed us how to do everything by your perfect example. But we were thinking so differently, as we started making so many changes. There you were in your books, tapes, pictures, and even in your *murti*, constantly speaking to us, but we thought that you had departed fully. I especially was too immature, afraid, and foolish to understand.

*Inmate of the dungeon,*  
Bhaktitīrtha Swami

## Danavir dāsa Vanacārī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. On this most auspicious day, I beg you to let me continue to serve in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. With your permission, some accomplishments of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness after your departure are presented herewith, for your pleasure.

At the time of your departure from this world, ISKCON had 96 temples and 12 farms, totaling 108 centers. Today it has 209 temples and 30 farms, totaling 239 centers. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Before your departure, 64 million literatures (in 27 languages) had been distributed; since then, 75 million more (including several additional languages) have been distributed. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Before you departed there were only a few Russian devotees; today there are 700 full-time fixed-up devotees in Russia. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

You wanted the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* completed: today the entire eleventh and twelfth cantos are published, and the tenth canto is almost done. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Over a dozen authoritative biographical volumes describing your glorious life of devotion have been produced in quantity and relished universally. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Scholars continue to marvel at your books and your movement, so much so that now well-known commercial publishing houses are compiling these praises into books and selling them profusely to the public. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

You encouraged the opening of restaurants; now there are over 40 operating daily. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

You requested magnificent Vedic temples to be built; devotees in New Vrndavan, South Africa, and Vancouver have responded. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Life membership enrollment has topped 40,000. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Congregational programs known as Nāma-Haṭṭa, Friends of Lord Kṛṣṇa (FOLK), etc., have incredibly escalated to enormous proportions. The envious demons are truly alarmed about this. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

The Bhaktivedanta Institute draws leading scientists to its World Congress. Similarly, other ISKCON scientists are lecturing and writing books, magazines, and papers defeating atheism and stunning the scientific communities. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

A devotee of yours is even a chosen leader of the Pope’s World Religious Peace Council. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

ISKCON T.V., cinema, and radio continue to expand broadcasting networks. In some places, twenty-four-hour Kṛṣṇa consciousness is aired through the radio waves. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Padayātra* has been constantly on the road for over two years, with their beautiful bullock carts, walking Vaiṣṇavas, and Caitanya *kīrtanas* touring holy places in India. You requested it: now this popular, powerful program is purifying the planet. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

You wanted mass *prasādam* distribution; Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life serves millions of plates, often receiving governmental support and acclaim. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Ratha-yātrā* festivals, Festivals of India, as well as other festival parties keep rolling along with increased prominence. Children’s literature, college preaching, cooking courses, and academic curriculums continue to improve. Also a gorgeous doll museum was opened at Detroit. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Back to Godhead*, the magazine of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement you started in 1944, has appeared timely in over 100 high-quality monthly issues since your departure.* Saṅkīrtana Newsletter*, BBT report, and* ISKCON World Review *have regularly resounded with good tidings of world preaching news. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!*

*You magnificent Indian projects in Bombay, Vṛndāvana, and Māyāpur are nicely maintained, while attracting and accommodating thousands of visitors with their* gurukulas*, Deity worship, guesthouses, dramas, dioramas, restaurants, gardens, and preaching. In Māyāpur, one gentleman has pledged a one-million-dollar donation to build a new guesthouse. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!*

The Governing Body Commission meets annually at Śrīdhāma Māyāpur during the *Gaura-pūrṇimā* Festival, as do the ISKCON temple presidents. In 1986, the five-hundredth anniversary of Lord Caitanya’s appearance, over twelve hundred ISKCON devotees came from around the globe, as well as over five million Indian pilgrims. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

These achievements all belong to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. They are your honors and credit. Not ours.

However, we alone can take full credit for minimizing or neglecting some of your important instructions. Sadly, yet with sincere hopeful prospect for improvement, and with your permission, I’ll mention a few. We have not read or distributed your books enough. Some of our leaders did not follow the four regulative principles, chant at least sixteen rounds daily, rise for *maṅgala aratrika,* or attend *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class daily; thus they fell down. Sometimes we thought it wise to imbibe advice on managing ISKCON from those other than Your Divine Grace. Sometimes we even doubted you by considering it impossible to organize a worldwide preaching movement under a GBC. Sometimes we invited mad elephants into the gardens and began cursing and counter-cursing Vaiṣṇavas. We displayed some tendency for demon-crazy politics, complicated centralized corporate structures, meeting marathons, court litigations, treachery, as well as campaigning to become the next *ācārya*. We are still struggling with the heavy weight of becoming bona fide gurus. Due to our impurities, we drift back into mundane business pursuits, neglecting to beat vigorously the *bṛhat mṛdaṅga.* We have not yet completed your *samādhis.* These and crores more deficiencies we displayed after your departure. We pray that you rectify us and lead us on the right path to reach you. Although we are unfit, we depend solely on Your Divine Grace to save us.

I am a most shameless dog; still, my beloved master, I beg you with a straw between my teeth to kindly not kick me away from your transcendental house. Let me serve your mission eternally, to canvass all the fallen souls to come back home, back to Godhead. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your eternal servant,*  
Danavir dāsa Vanacārī

## Gaṇapati Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my lowly obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories unto you.

O Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead!  
O most confidential associate of Śrīmatī Rādhārānī!  
O personification of the compassionate glance of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu!

Just twenty-two years ago today, Your Divine Grace was ecstatically anticipating your bold voyage to the West to share with us the treasure of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Rūpa Gosvāmī termed Kṛṣṇa consciousness as *kṛṣṇa-bhakti-rasa-bhāvita:* to be absorbed in the mellow taste of executing devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. Simply by giving us your association you delivered this treasure, because you are the embodiment of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the person *Bhāgavata.* In one sense, you didn’t bring much: a trunk of books, a sack of grains, and a few sets of cloth. But just as an enormous banyan tree is contained within a tiny seed, so Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and the whole spiritual realm dwelled within your devoted heart. You opened up your heart to us in the simplest of ways: by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, discoursing on *kṛṣṇa-kathā,* performing the “bell” ceremony, and distributing sliced apple *prasādam.*

As we reciprocated your affectionate advances and absorbed your flaming enthusiasm, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness naturally arose to fulfill your desire of a worldwide association of Vaiṣṇavas. You wrote in the *Ādi-līlā:* “On these principles only does the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement go on. We have no business other than to talk of Kṛṣṇa, worship Kṛṣṇa, and chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra*.” You informed us that ISKCON is one of the branches of the Caitanya tree, and as such, “those who are actually servants of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu must seriously distribute this movement all over the world to render the greatest benefit to human society.” Lord Caitanya said He would be known as a pious man and would be glorified with great pleasure if the fruits of love of Godhead were distributed all over the world. Similarly, we can understand that your deserved reputation as the Lord’s right-hand representative will be expanded universally as this movement flourishes. As you explained: “However, don’t create any awkward situation that may be criticized. Caesar’s wife must be above suspicion. Up to now as I have got respectable situation, I wish that all my disciples will have similar respectable position in society. That will keep my name good. Like father, like son.” You cautioned us to maintain the purity of the Society, lest it become like the colored water in a showbottle: “Now people are seeing how genuine our movement is, they are coming forward to offer us so many places. We simply have to maintain our strict principles, keeping ourselves pure. Otherwise, there are so many bogus institutions doing business in the name of God and simply cheating the people. We have to be careful not to degenerate like these others.”

To evoke a reverential and responsible attitude of service, you termed ISKCON as your body, the BBT as your heart, and all your faithful disciples as your limbs: “You are all my limbs of my body. Unless you cooperate, my life will be useless. The senses and life are correlative. Without life the senses cannot act, and without senses, life is inactive.” You taught us to identify the ISKCON society as our life and soul. Once when a guest misjudged that our attachment to ISKCON was mundane, you reprimanded him by explaining that because Kṛṣṇa is absolute, Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa’s institution are nondifferent. To identify with Kṛṣṇa’s institution was to identify with Kṛṣṇa directly.

Whenever some disciple wanted to leave ISKCON and become Kṛṣṇa conscious independently, you chided them, saying that it was impossible: “But I do not advise that you approach the matter of perfecting your life in this independent way. Unless there is a connection with a bona fide spiritual master, coming in the line of disciplic succession, there is no possibility of making progress in spiritual life. So I have established ISKCON centers for the purpose of catching up the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa by intimate connection with the spiritual master. These are my authorized centers for that purpose.” When others said that they had faith in you but not in your society, you chastised them thusly: “When they say they don’t like ISKCON and the GBC, they are really saying that they don’t like to follow my order. That means they don’t like my order. That means they don’t have faith in my order. That means they don’t have faith in me. That means *guru-aparādha.* To say they have faith in me is just hypocrisy.” When some challenged that the absolute spiritual nature was something different than following rules and running an organization, you strongly countered: “We have to be very organized! Very strict! We cannot deviate an inch! …..We have a mission to cultivate all over the world. We have to print and distribute books and open centers and train devotees, and we cannot do that unless we are very organized and efficient. If we are to conduct a mission to save the fallen souls, which is the desire of Lord Caitanya and the previous *ācārya,* then we have to organize the *saṅkīrtana* movement very nicely. And we have to be strict devotees.” And again: “Obedience is the first law of discipline. We are thinking of a great worldwide organization which is not possible to be executed if there is disobedience.”

But you repeatedly emphasized that the whole system was to be based on spiritual love and trust, not mundane methods of management. Surrender and submission had to be voluntary. Respect had to be commanded, not demanded. “After all, you are all working so hard to please Kṛṣṇa only out of love for me, so there can be no question of force if love is there. We should never try to force anyone or reduce our society to an impersonal business exchange; this will kill everything. So without the spirit of love and trust, it will be impossible for us to overcome the problems confronting ISKCON.” In another letter you very wonderfully described the “Vaikuṇṭha mentality” needed for the success of our society: “You speak of pure devotee, and you think only spiritual master is pure devotee, that he is *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* that we should obey him only – these things are the wrong idea. If anyone thinks like that, that a pure devotee should be obeyed and no one else, that means he is a nonsense. We advise everyone to address one another as *prabhu. Prabhu* means master, so how the master should be disobeyed? Others, they are also pure devotees. All of my disciples are pure devotees. Anyone sincerely serving the spiritual master is a pure devotee…Why you only want to be in the spiritual sky with Siddhasvarūpa? Why not all?……We will have another ISKCON there.”

You further instructed us that this love and trust amongst Godbrothers was only possible if we satisfied you, our spiritual father: “This organization is not run by bylaws, meetings, or vouchers, but by following the instructions of the spiritual master with sincerity. Whatever success I have is due to that. To one with faith in the spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa comes knowledge of the scripture. Without that faith and sincerity, bylaws are useless.” And you noted that our efforts were doomed if we displeased you: “You are right when you say that the movement will come to nothing if I am not satisfied with your actions.”

One Godbrother recently complained that since you are now personally gone, all love and trust has disappeared from ISKCON, and thus we must find another forum for collaboration. But according to your instructions, there can be no other foundation for a spiritual society but love and trust. Thus we seem to be hopelessly lost in the painful dilemma of choosing between the futile struggle for an illusory, earthly coalliance on one side, and merely “giving up the ship” on the other. Some say that Kṛṣṇa has left ISKCON or that you have left ISKCON, reminiscent of the rumors spread in 1970 which said that you had withdrawn your mercy. But I cannot believe that you have abandoned us, because you are the most merciful father. You have taught us that the relationship of a father with his sons is that he will always extend his compassion towards his repentant sons, no matter how neglectfully or offensively they may have previously behaved. It is with this desperate hope that I beg you to please purify our hearts and consciousness so that we can attract your attention and always appreciate your transcendental presence. Please kick out our personal ambitions with your lotus feet. Caress our war-torn minds with the strokes of your loving words. Help us to maintain this movement as you have requested us to do: “You love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate to keep this institution together after I am gone….If you love me you will not fight. Stay united. Don’t let this become another Gaudīya Math. Maintain the society in my absence. Do not let it deteriorate.” Help us silence the critics who say that ISKCON is finished when you said it is eternal. (Where are they all now?)

One last request I have from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that you and your disciples kindly not be offended by my entreaties to you on their behalf as well as mine. While I have no real love for you, I realize that many of my Godbrothers do have genuine devotion to your lotus feet. As such, I am especially speaking of myself when I speak of impurities and insufficiencies in character. If I can somehow be of assistance to your faithful disciples with my body, mind, and words, then my human form of life may have some value.

*Praying at your lotus feet for your causeless mercy,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Gīrirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your eternal presence in your ISKCON body.

In the agony and the ecstasy, the perfection is to remember you. During the year, my thoughts have been expressed in the words that follow:

**No Pain Greater**

We have forgotten you for so long,  
please remind us:  
You are everything to us.

Why can’t we serve you properly?  
Our hearts are full of sinful desires;  
We need your guidance.

The pain of separation  
Is  
most terrible.

**The Heart**

Full of perverted, twisted,  
dirty, dark thoughts.  
You enter and sound  
the transcendental vibration  
which clarifies everything just  
like the sun.  
You enter,  
but the evil elements rise again:  
Who is this intruder who has  
disturbed us?  
Let him go!  
You are available whenever we want you,  
but you do not force.

**How You Work**

We know that we are very imperfect  
but you are very kind and work  
through us  
because you know that only you  
on behalf of Krishna  
can rescue the fallen souls  
from material delusion  
and establish them in their eternal relationship  
with Krishna.  
We do not know anyone but you,  
and yours.

Our words are very nice, but we  
know that we have failed you – badly.  
Please forgive us for all of our offenses and  
give us another chance to serve you better.

On this occasion I also address all of your followers – past, present, and future: You are all great souls. In your effort to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have put so many obstacles. Please forgive me.

**Vyāsa-pūjā Offering**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Once a devotee asked you,  
We have the perfect philosophy,  
Krishna consciousness,  
and we have the perfect spiritual master –  
Your Divine Grace –  
everything about Krishna consciousness is perfect,  
yet we always seem to have so many problems –  
why?

And you replied,  
Because the bramacārīs  
and sannyāsīs  
associate too much  
with women

We take shelter of your lotus feet!

Once an insignificant devotee asked you,  
Now you are here  
so everything is all right,  
but in the course of time,  
when you are not here,  
if the society deviates from your standard,  
what should I do?

And you replied,  
You are also one of the important members of the society,  
so you work for the correction,  
but DO NOT LEAVE.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Once a devotee said,  
The way you look right now  
is just like a photo  
I saw of your Guru Mahārāja,

And you replied,

All that glitters is not gold:

my Guru Mahārāja was like gold –  
I am like iron.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Once a devotee said,  
I can surrender to you,  
but whenever I surrender to your disciples,  
they always cheat me.

And you replied,  
Don’t surrender to me –  
I will also cheat you –  
just surrender to Krishna.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Once a devotee asked,  
you know everything about us –  
you know us even better than we know ourselves –  
yet sometimes you put someone  
in a very elevated position  
and he falls down.  
How is that?

And you replied,  
The living entity is always marginal,  
so whatever position he is in  
he can always use it  
either for  
Krishna’s service  
or for  
personal sense gratification.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we take shelter of your lotus feet!

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. On this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day we honor you because you have always been the true representative of Vyāsadeva.

From the *vyāsāsana* you have always spoken the Absolute Truth. From your transcendental appearance till your disappearance in 1977, you demonstrated nectarean pastimes that will be appreciated by all the Vaiṣṇavas for the next ten thousand years. You are incomparable in your service to Caitanya Mahaprabhu. You opened so many temples, farms, and restaurants, wrote books, and gave *darśanas.* Due to your tireless efforts, the course of history has been altered – from the path of illusion and bewilderment to a path of Krishna consciousness, peace, and prosperity. Due to your purity and unlimited mercy, the Krishna consciousness movement is continuing to expand. The present devotees are training other new devotees, and thus we see that your mercy is ever expanding.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always bold and determined. Just as grandfather Bhīṣma was peaceful and perfect in his meditation on Lord Krishna even though he was being pierced by arrows, you are always fixed in your determination to preach Krishna consciousness, in spite of heavy odds.

To call oneself a member of ISKCON means that one knows your instructions and works tirelessly to execute them without deviation. Your instructions are crystal clear. Let us all keep you in the center by worshiping the instructions that you have kindly given to us. Though the list is unlimited, some of the major ones are:

-to chant our sixteen rounds  
-to read your books  
-to distribute your books  
-to perform gorgeous Deity worship  
-to distribute Krishna *prasādam*  
-to work cooperatively under the direction of the Governing Body Commission.

These instructions have to be the object of our constant meditation. We have to chant our rounds very attentively and try to finish them in the morning. We must develop the habit of reading your books regularly. One of the main reasons why so many of your beloved sons have left their service is because of the weakness in these important spiritual activities.

Our book distribution has to increase all over the world. You wanted to double the book distribution every year. Your instruction that we all work together under the overall supervision of the GBC body should be taken seriously. We have to work together even if there are differences. This is to pass the test of the spiritual master. Even in the spiritual world there are sometimes different parties among the *gopīs*. But the goal of loving service to Krishna is never forgotten. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no doubt in my mind that we can please you by remaining inside the ISKCON body, and accepting the authority of the GBC body. We have to remember your famous statement “Your love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate to keep this institution together after I am gone.” We must see, as you said, “the BIG ISKCON.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this auspicious day, I thank you for giving me the opportunity to serve in your great mission of fulfilling Lord Caitanya’s prediction that this Krishna consciousness will spread to every town and village of the world. Please let me go on serving you life after life. I have no other shelter but at your lotus feet.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Gopāla Krishna Goswami

## Guru-Gaurāṅga dāsa

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalakayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the decade since your physical departure, we have often felt the ship of ISKCON heave in the turbulent waters of the age of Kali. In the absence of your direct captaincy, we have shown ourselves to be a callow and unseasoned crew as we cope with the daunting challenge of crossing the ocean of material existence.

If I have understood anything at all, it is that we may weather all storms *only* by executing your crisp commands unstintingly. As long as we adhere to your instructions without embellishment or pretension, you remain the captain of the ship for those of us who desire to cross this difficult ocean.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your menial servant,*  
Guru-Gaurāṅga dāsa

## Guruprasāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, which are giving shelter to countless devotees, both now and in the future.

It is the duty of a disciple to carry out the mission of his spiritual master. Since you brought the *saṅkīrtana* movement to the entire world single-handedly, you had to entrust its continuation to all of your disciples, instructing them to execute it cooperatively. You knew that such inexperienced devotees could be successful in such a monumental task only by working together.

Now we are learning why you emphasized cooperation. You painfully saw the progress of your spiritual master’s mission come to a halt, and as a result had to face times of great difficulty in fulfilling his order to preach all over the world. Even those around you could not understand the depth of your determination.

We are facing difficult situations in the continuing of your movement. I am trying to reflect on how you pushed on, beyond countless setbacks, for years on end, only out of intense desire to satisfy *guru* and Krishna, which is actually everything. Time, age, money, lack of assistance – nothing stopped you. Now it seems that we are facing the same kind of test. Krishna tested you, even though you are eternally situated in pure love of Krishna. Then how much greater will be the test we have to face to demonstrate our loyalty to your mission? This is especially true for myself, who am an incorrigible fool. Therefore, my only hope is that you engage me as a tiny, insignificant cog in the *saṅkīrtana* movement and make me spin by the momentum of the other, bigger parts.

You accepted a thankless task. After years of struggle to create the League of Devotees, you had no apparent success eternally. Yet your determination remained undaunted. Your inspiration was Krishna, your only motivation to fulfill the desire of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. The *Bhāgavatam* states that great souls traverse the earth on Krishna’s order to deliver the conditioned souls, who have no interest in surrendering. You made such a sacrifice, and no one came forward to reward you. Only Krishna could see your relentless dedication, and He removed all obstacles, giving unparalleled victory to His pure devotee. Undeterred by any and all difficulties, you demonstrated that the *guru’s* order is the beacon that guides the disciple past insurmountable odds. Please never allow me to forget your shining example of what it takes to please the spiritual master.

When we encounter obstacles in our devotional service, many times we question why this is happening. We are not so quick to accept Krishna ‘s mercy. Some have chosen to find a new source of inspiration. Others have given up for the time being. But your example was that even alone one can carry out the order of the spiritual master. Of course, being the topmost pure devotee of Krishna, you could face such obstacles alone because you were actually never alone. You were personally in the association of Krishna. That is why you have left the necessary ingredients with us to carry on your mission. We do not have to be alone. We have perfect guidance from your books; temples everywhere to provide shelter for everyone; and there is a network of devotees all over the world.

Lord Brahmā pleaded with Lord Viṣṇu to keep him from becoming proud of his big position and just engage him as an instrument. Lord Viṣṇu gave him the necessary understanding and paraphernalia to do his duty. Lord Brahmā was cautious not to abuse the great responsibility he was given. Although my ability to serve you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is insignificant, the responsibility you have given us to continue the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Caitanya Mahāprabhu is inconceivable.

Please help all of us to fully understand the importance of your legacy. I personally have failed to grasp its urgency in the wake of sense gratification. I beg you to give me sufficient intelligence to help further your mission in some small way. Please penetrate my hard heart, which is shielded by layers of false ego, faulty intelligence, an uncontrolled mind, and unlimited bodily conceptions. Such a request can only be fulfilled by someone of your spiritual stature. Please help me to develop greater appreciation for the shelter you have constructed in the form of the ISKCON movement. That shelter is supported by the strength of devotees whose only desire is to serve you. By your vigilant guidance and causeless mercy, I may have some hope of service in the future.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Guruprasāda Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my most reverential and devoted obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

I am lowborn and fallen by nature. You picked me up from the darker regions of ignorance and gave me the chance to chant the holy name of the Lord and serve you, His most perfect representative. I am eternally indebted to you for this. Therefore I shall go on serving your lotus feet despite all the disturbances which continuously attack the body and mind.

I feel grateful that you still engage me in your service, and I pray that you continue to do so. I cannot live without your service. Please always protect me from *māyā* and keep me sheltered in your service. Never let me forget the real purpose of this movement and the real goal of my life. I pray to you, my eternal master, since you are the one upon whom I always depend. It is obvious to me that you are the real friend and the real father. Engage me as you like; I have no other thought than this. I simply want to be always fully engaged in your service in whatever way you think best. My deepest desire is to fully satisfy you. Please help me purify my existence and ultimately situate myself at the lotus feet of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Krishna. Your pleasure is the perfection of my life.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

On this most auspicious day, the appearance day of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottaraśata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, I am once again faced with my own poor-heartedness, my inability to properly glorify my own spiritual master, who is the great spiritual master and founder of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s ISKCON.

Prabhupāda’s purity is such that simply by remembering his divine appearance day, and one’s duty to properly worship him on this day, one immediately remembers one’s own dull and humble position. Many times I have sat to write a Vyāsa-pūjā offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda, and now, thinking back on what I have felt and written previously, I do not think that in my fallen condition I will have much new to say. Perhaps one humble realization is worth passing along to those fortunate persons who will read and hear these many offerings to Prabhupāda.

I offer this simple fact: We must worship and glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda by serious dedication to his divine service. Everyone has heard this point, and I myself have stated it in many previous Vyāsa-pūjā offerings. Therefore, I can only submit at the feet of the Vaiṣṇavas that by the causeless kindness of Śrīla Prabhupāda, this year I feel an extra inspiration, a special comprehension of this simple, yet all-powerful fact – that we must surrender to Śrīla Prabhupāda by selflessly dedicating and rededicating ourselves to his preaching mission, and our loving service in spreading his mission will truly establish and clarify his glories throughout the world.

Yes, of course it is true that the path is fraught with dangers, that we become lazy, selfish, foolish, and even crazy due to the pernicious atmosphere of this age and our own woeful impurity. Still, the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda is so sublime that it will lift his sincere followers over the monstrous obstacles placed in our path by the illusory potency of the Lord.

A comfortable life in the domestic spirit is a great obstacle both for householders and *sannyāsīs.* A cheerful, loving willingness to forego such ease and comfort and undergo personal inconvenience for the sake of our beloved master is certainly the right medicine and the right nectar to bring us to a happy, pure life of eternal loving service at the lotus feet of Lord Krishna. Praying for the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas who truly love Śrīla Prabhupāda, I remain a tiny servant at the feet of his servants.

Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

**Can I see You again soon?**

Will I see you ‘gain soon? With your Friend?  
*Since you left me here crying alone,*  
*I’ve been wondering, “What have I done?”*

I’m now anxious to get to the end  
Of this lengthy and troublesome road.  
Please protect me from all allurements  
Which appear left and right on the way.

There are promising side tracks all ‘round  
That look much like the road you have shown,  
But in fact they are treacherous and dark,  
Leading only to failure and doom.

You are beckoning us to come home,  
But to hear you one must be sincere.  
Yet because I’m so foolish and slow,  
I am hardly responding at all.

Can I see you ‘gain soon? With your Friend?  
You have left me here crying alone,  
And I’m wondering what to do now.

Jagadīśa Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine teaching and example. In every way you are the perfect ā*cārya,* and you have left your books to guide every future Vaiṣṇava. Your own activities have glorified you perfectly.

We are weak and have difficulty even following your most basic instructions. Even so, we tried to imitate you. We are fools, and we become very easily proud, forgetting you and Krishna. Please ask Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to help us. If we can simply follow your basic instructions, we can serve you nicely. Give us the strength to study your books and to follow seriously for the pleasure of Krishna.

Here in Japan your books are being received very nicely. You said that they would be the last to come, so we are trying to be patient. *Harināma* goes on regularly, and many are coming to the temple and chanting and dancing and taking *prasādam.* You kindly came here and blessed them, so they are fortunate. We want you to be here always, so we are distributing your books very enthusiastically.

Please give us your mercy to be your simple followers. There is no better position in the three worlds.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Kavicandra Swami

## Mukunda Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your impeccable purity and your glorious vision of Krishna consciousness spreading – by the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu – all over the world.

*brahmāṇḍa brahmite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their karma, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Krishna. By the mercy of both Krishna and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā,*** **19.151)**

“Very fortunate” can be the only meaningful description of my association with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As stated above, one is lucky indeed to meet a “bona fide” spiritual master, and of thousands of such masters, one is most fortunate indeed to contact a *mahā-bhāgavata* like your good self.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-lila,* Chapter 17, you write: “The *mahā-bhāgavata’s* heart is completely freed from material contamination……,” and in *The Nectar of Devotion* we read that from the pure heart of a devotee “there is a diffusion of ecstatic love which is more glorious than the sunshine.” It is just such a radiance that emanated from your heart, warmed the hearts of all you met, and surcharged the hearts of your devotees. And that same radiance was the spark that touched off a spiritual explosion on this planet. Indeed, you are no ordinary spiritual master.

Your perfect self-realization is to be found in your unparalleled personal qualities and by the thousands who gave their lives to Krishna because of your association. You explain the nature of such realization in your purport to the first text of “The Appearance of Crī Nārada” in the *Crīmad Bhāgavatam:*

Personal realization does not mean that one should out of vanity, attempt to show one’s own learning by trying to surpass the previous *ācārya.* He must have full confidence in the previous *ācārya*, and at the same time he must realize the subject matter so nicely that he can present the matter for the particular circumstances in a suitable manner. The original purpose of the text must be maintained. No obscure meaning should be screwed out of it, yet it should be presented in an interesting manner for the understanding of the audience. This is called realization.

This passage perfectly describes the nature of your preaching.

My very good fortune was to have the opportunity to bathe in the rays of your effulgent personality, to gain inspiration from your flawless transcendental intellect, to admire your “down-to-earth” analogies, which brought to light profound philosophical truths, and to discover how a pure devotee expertly “functions” from the transcendental position in this world of duality. Operating in this way, entirely from the spiritual platform, you taught all of us not only what devotional service was, but also how to practically engage in such service simply by following your flawless example. You write in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* “When by learning from the self-realized spiritual master one actually engages himself in the service of Lord Viṣṇu, functional devotional service begins….and one who teaches how to approach Krishna is the functioning form of the Personality of Godhead.”

You often said that your only credit was that you invented nothing but simply repeated with firm faith the message and words of your spiritual master. Your glory is that you were and are a transparent medium of your exalted spiritual master (and Lord Krishna ’s) message. You are no ordinary spiritual master.

Thus in trying to glorify you by following in your footsteps, I can find no better words than those you have written yourself, for these descriptions of the pure devotee are in fact descriptions of your very self.

On this occasion of your *Vyāsa-pūjā* day, my opportunity to repeat these words is my extreme good fortune.

Mukunda Goswami

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Recently while reading the prayers of Queen Kuntī, I came upon a wonderful verse spoken by Kuntīdevī to Lord Krishna that caused me to reflect upon my own relationship with you. Kuntīdevī was praying to Krishna, “As the name and fame of a particular body is finished with the disappearance of the living spirit, similarly, if You do not look upon us, all our fame and activities, along with the Pāṇḍavas and Yadus, will end at once.”

Although this body bears no fame, my actions somehow continue, with hope that some day I may actually become qualified to be your eternal, *menial* servant. Full absorption in service to you can only mean that you will continue to engage me eternally, according to your desire. And by giving me orders, I will capture your attention and will possibly become the fortunate recipient of your most merciful glance.

I know that you have already given your order to chant Hare Krishna, distribute your books, and preach this Krishna consciousness all over the world. Only a fool would think he’s lacking personal service to you in this mission. Your vision and compassion are so deep that you handed this most confidential service to all of your disciples, knowing full well that in that instruction is everything we need to gain your favor and thus your eternal association through service in separation. You trained us, continually preached to us, and sustained us by your pure, ideal example of an unalloyed devotee of Lord Krishna. You gave us your books so that we could not forget your instructions, and you told us that they were nondifferent from you. You left us your movement and called it your body, so that we could serve the body of Vaiṣṇavas and serve you as well. You gave us service to Krishna, as only a pure devotee of Krishna could give, and thus we learned the true meaning of religion. It’s so easy to see that you gave us *everything* needed for unlimited engagement in Lord Caitanya’s eternal *līlā* of awakening forgetful souls to their constitutional nature. If I could only immerse myself in these valuable gifts, I know I could continually bathe under your radiant glance.

But, I remember you saying that your Guru Mahārāja would say that we should not be concerned for how we can see Krishna, but how we can be seen by Krishna. Similarly, I want to be seen by you. I want to be an obedient son, always prepared to execute your slightest request. I’ll bring your *prasādam,* I’ll slip on your shoes, I’ll clean your room, I’ll bring you people to preach to: anything you ask. You don’t have to pat me on the head or promise me that I’ll get something in return. Just your glance upon me will keep me going, giving me life.

You have already said everything. If I want to be with you, then I must follow the orders have already given. No new concoctions can give me a short-cut. And nobody else can replace your relationship with me.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I humbly prostrate myself before you and beg that you don’t give up on me. Please don’t turn me away in disgust for my failure to completely surrender to you after so many years. And, most of all, please be merciful, and please look upon me favorably, even for only a moment, so that I may continue to live with hope for your next instruction, whenever you’ll have me with you again.

*Your most unworthy servant,*  
Nirañjana Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Even the dumb man can talk,  
The blind can see stars in the night time,  
And the lame o’er mountains can walk.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
We cam easily comprehend  
The power of the Lord’s pure devotee  
To turn hell into heaven again.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
In the darkness of Kali-yuga  
The sun and the moon have arisen,  
‘Gaura-Nitāi’ he has brought in the world.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
We’ve found a society strong  
Where devotees of different nations  
Unitedly live in ISKCON.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
The whole world can live in one house,  
Including men, women, and children  
From east or west or north or south.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
So many hippies transformed  
Into happies who bright shining faces  
Are with tilak and neckbeads adorned.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
We are chanting the Lord’s holy name,  
Reciting the nectarean glories  
Of His qualities, pastimes, and fame.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
We’ve got Gītā and Bhāgavatam,  
Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,  
and the Vaiṣṇava ācāryas’ songs.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Krishna stand  
in so many beautiful temples,  
throughout many different lands.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
We observe many sumptuous feasts  
By respecting kṛṣṇa-prasāda,  
And thus we advance while we eat.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
The city of Māyāpur Dhāma  
Is arising beside the Gaṅgā,  
Calling all to the Lord’s holy land.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
We can visit Vṛndāvana Dhāma,  
Take bath in the Yamunā River,  
See Krishna and Śrī Balarāma.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
The big Ratha-yātrā carts roll  
Down the streets of the world’s largest cities  
Lord Jagannātha, the Supreme Soul.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
You can go and live out on the farm,  
Drive bullocks, graze cows in the pastures,  
Be peaceful in varṇāśrama.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
You can find them out on the street,  
Book distribution, hari-nāma parties,  
Giving Krishna to all whom they meet.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
The principle’s utility,  
Books the basis, preaching the essence,  
And the force is purity.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Lord Gaurāṅga is close at hand  
Encouraging us all to distribute  
His name and deliver these lands.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
We should work under GBC,  
Show our love through cooperation.  
Really, what is the difficulty?

Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Pṛthu dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Your mercy is all I am made of. While the dust is settling and everyone is sitting through the rubble, there is good reason to feel confident that your society, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, will save the world, as is the desire of Lord Caitanya. You are our foremost guide, and you are lending us everything we need to spread your mission, and Krishna ‘s grace is abundant – provided we return every bit of it to Him, as nothing is ours. We are not here to take, but to give; not to rule, but to serve. “With mind intent” on Krishna let us fight like Arjuna did, on streets, at house doors, everywhere, but in our meetings let us fight with our foremost enemy, our false pride. “Only a person in false ego takes credit,” you said. “That is a symptom of his nescience.” Let us learn the lessons of history. So many big egos have come and gone – let us stop to persecute our own faults that we see in others, to be open-minded, straightforward in our dealings, kind to all living entities, patient with those who do not share our views, and tolerant with those who hate our guts.

You have “created our good fortune,” as you said in Paris. Now please help us to maintain it.

*Your servant,*  
Pṛthu dāsa

## Rūpānugṅa dāsa

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances.

You said our love for you will be shown by how much we cooperate amongst ourselves after you have gone. In other words, the development of our cooperative spirit is the proof of our love for you. Absence or presence of such a cooperative spirit would seem to indicate how our love for you has been developing. Has our love developed enough to inspire us to cooperate with each other?

Love for the spiritual master is no mere sentimental attachment. How is it manifest? What is the evidence that a disciple loves his spiritual master? Surely we love you because you saved us. Yet such love, no matter how intense, cannot be enough. Appreciation is real love, but better love includes reciprocation or service, making a sincere attempt to repay you. That repayment is, of course, impossible, but the attempt to do so is love.

That attempt must be solid work – realistic and appropriate for pushing on the movement you gave us. Undoubtedly, the most solid work is to carry out your orders in an attempt to fulfill your desires. “One should accept the instructions of the bona fide spiritual master as one’s mission in life. Therefore the spiritual master’s order should be taken as the prime duty of life.”

What are your orders? Some of your disciples received specific orders; many did not. The general orders apply to disciples and followers according to their service. Both general and specific orders apply to our leaders.

So, can we not say that our love for you is tested by how we carry out both general and specific orders? Certainly, if we are all trying to do that, cooperation will be easy. Cooperation would seem to be the first order, because without it, how can we carry out your other orders? So cooperation must mean cooperation to carry out your orders, being inspired to reciprocate with your love.

I am especially interested in carrying out the orders you gave during your lifetime which we have barely started to execute or didn’t start at all. Or if we did, we made mistakes, and perhaps continue to do so – orders like *varṇāśrama* colleges, economic self-sufficiency, steady increasing of book distribution, organizing *varṇāśrama* in our society, the completion of international projects, etc.

Perhaps if we could concentrate our collective efforts in carrying out such orders, we could realize the wonderful transcendental potency inherent in the cooperative spirit, which could push our movement to successes we have yet to imagine. We have tasted some bitter fruits in the absence of that spirit: separatism, isolationism, cultism – in short the party spirit, the party of the agents and relatives of Kali.

Now it appears that we are facing the choice whether cooperation is to be the exception or the rule. Kindly continue to kick us in the right direction, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your most fallen disciple,*  
Rūpānuga dāsa

## Tamal Krishna Goswami

*The following is an excerpt from Act Two of* Prabhupāda Bhakti Rasa*, a ten-act drama depicting Śrīla Prabhupāda's final pastimes, to be published late in 1987.*  
*(Śrīla Prabhupāda lying down, with Tamal Krishna Goswami standing by his side.)*

Prabhupāda *(opening his eyes):* Tamal Krishna?

Tamal Krishna: Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Have you rested well?

Prabhupāda: Lift me up. *(Tamal Krishna places Śrīla Prabhupāda ’s arms around his neck and thus raises Prabhupāda to a sitting position. Sitting behind him, Tamal Krishna proceeds to scratch Śrīla Prabhupāda ’s back.)*

Tanal Krishna: Śrīla Prabhupāda, Upendra must have put your shirt on backwards.

Prabhupāda (*smiling while remembering):* My mother used to do that purposely so I wouldn’t remove it. I think I’m becoming like a child again.

I was my mother’s pet child. Once I swallowed watermelon seed, and my friends told me it would grow into a tree in my stomach. When I ran to my mother, she told me not to worry. She would chant a *mantra* to prevent any harm. *(He laughs.)*

I was so dependent on her. I would run to her even for asking to go to the privy. “Who is stopping you?” she would ask. “Yes, you can go.” Only then would I go. Now I have so many fathers and mothers, and once again I have become so dependent that I take help even to sit up, to dress, or to bathe.

Tamal Krishna: Krishna plays the same way, saying, “Mother, please feed Me. Please give me protection.” He sustains great universes with His breath, but He shows dependence to evoke His mother’s love.

Prabhupāda: Yaśodā’s love is so strong that Krishna is actually dependent. He is so bound by her loving affection that He is afraid to displease her. And I am the same. I am living only due to my disciples’ love. Otherwise, in this condition, no one can maintain his life.

Krishna has always surrounded me with well-wishers. Not only was I my mother’s favorite, but my father’s as well. When I was still a youth, one of my father’s friends said to him that I was very intelligent so he should send me to England to become a barrister. But my father said he would never send me to become a *mleccha.* His only wish was that I become a devotee of Rādhārānī. Still, when I grew up, I liked that friend’s idea and thought, “Why not go to England and get a white wife and white children?” *(They both laugh.)*

Now in recent years, when I would take my morning walks here near the law colleges, I was thinking, “Now I’ve got thousands of white children, without the help of a white wife.” *(Again laughter)*

Tamal Krishna: Like Lord Viṣṇu conceiving Brahmā without Lakṣmī’s help.

Prabhupāda: The other day I was thinking how fortunate I was to be always surrounded by devotees. One old friend of mine, a pleader named Nandulal Pine, was such a nice devotee. He remarried when his wife died, though he was seventy years old. When offering obeisances, sometimes, because of his age, he would stay asleep like that for two or three hours. Yesterday, just by thinking of him, I felt purified.

Whatever I have introduced throughout the world – *prasādam* distribution, Deity worship, Ratha-yātrā – I was trained for in my childhood. I have simply imitated what I learned then. All except for writing books. That I have done on the order of my Guru Mahārāja. *(Pausing.)* The fact is that in those days everyone was a devotee.

Tamal Krishna: Even now in India one can still feel the presence of Vedic culture.

Prabhupāda: *(distastefully)*: But the present government is discouraging religion, and therefore it is becoming hellish.

*(Upendra enters, offering obeisances.)*

Upendra: Śrīla Prabhupāda, Harikeśa Swami has arrived with the German and Swiss devotees.

Prabhupāda: *(pleased and excited):* Oh! Let him come at once! *(Upendra offers obeisances and exits.)* Seeing all of you gives me life.

Tamal Krishna: Seeing your lotus feet, all of Europe has gotten life.

Prabhupāda: That you could transplant a half-dead lotus without losing its life is a great credit.

Tamal Krishna: I think you are the first lotus to thrive in this dreary climate.

Prabhupāda: I used to wonder, if the sun never sets on the British Empire, why it never rises in London? But so many devotees have come means so many suns. Therefore I am feeling better.

Tamal Krishna: Night makes the lotus wilt, but the sun gives life to its drooping face.

Prabhupāda: Yes, for me, India was a long, dark night. I was rotting in Vṛṇdāvana, thinking, “What is this slow death?” Coming to London was the right decision. Krishna-Balarāma has placed me in the care of Rādhā-Gokulānanda. Bhagavān has come and told me of the preaching in France and other countries. When I get reports like that my chest swells with pride to hear how our movement is expanding.

Now help me up. It is a little cold, so only half-bath.

*(Tamal Krishna helps Prabhupāda rise and they slowly exit. After a short interval, Prabhupāda, nicely dressed, enters assisted by Tamal Krishna and moves to a seat. He is wearing dark sunglasses. Harikeā Swami, Ghanaśyāma, Abhirāma, and a few others enter, offering full* daṇḍavats.)

Prabhupāda: Harikeśa?

Harikeśa: Yes, Prabhupāda?

Prabhupāda: *(with intense emotion and tears):* Come here! When I sent you away, you thought I was degrading you. I thought, “Here is a very intelligent boy. I already have ten servants. Why should he be typewriting when he can do so much more?” Rain hardly benefits the ocean. But how welcome it is to the parched earth!

*(To the others):* He was typing so furiously, pushing for more dictation. And cooking all varieties of foodstuffs, even when I asked for simple *kichari. (To Harikeśa):* Now do you understand?

Harikeśa: *(also crying):* Now I understand, Śrīla Prabhupāda.
