Prabhupāda: *(giving inspiration):* The whole world is simply waiting for Krishna consciousness. Let us give them something. Let us make them happy. Open temples! Print books! *(Seeing him still crying):* Why are you crying?

Harikeśa (*between tears):* Since you sent me away, my constant meditation has been to join you again. The new temples, the books, the devotees made – all just to attract you to look my way again.

Prabhupāda: *(apologetically):* I know that. The *gopīs* also meditated like this.

Harikeśa: *(fighting back the tears):* But I am not so advanced as the *gopīs.* I am just a conditioned soul. *(Beseechingly.)* Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, get better, more healthy.

Prabhupāda *(with a detached calmness):* Healthy? I have nothing to do with this body. I have to do with the spiritual world.

Harikeśa: When I see you in this condition, I feel ill, too. And with your loss of appetite, who can think of eating? Seeing you wear those dark sunglasses makes me want to cry.

Prabhupāda: You are a young man; you have the right to live.

Harikeśa: Then I offer you my youth, as Puru offered his.

Prabhupāda: But I have no Devayānī. How shall I use your youth?

Harikeśa: For preaching! To deliver the world.

Prabhupāda: *(with calm reassurance):* That I shall do through all of you. At my age, death is not surprising. Still, if it is possible, I want to see you all a little stronger; a father is always concerned to see his estate nicely managed. *(There is silence.)*

Tamal Krishna: Śrīla Prabhupāda, Ghanaśyāma Prabhu has come from Eastern Europe.

Prabhupāda: *(He looks and, seeing him, smiles.)* Come closer. *(Ghanaśyāma moves to Harikeśa’s side, and Prabhupāda strokes his head affectionately.)* This is the *paramparā* system. My Guru Mahārāja pushed me, I am pushing you, and you are pushing others. *(Appreciately):* You are risking so much!

Ghanaśyāma: That is my only hope. Living behind the Iron Curtain, I know that Your Divine Grace and Lord Krishna are my constant protectors.

Prabhupāda: I never expected standing orders from the Communists. In selling a thing, these points are important: the appearance of the salesman, the sweet language he uses, and the familiarity with the product. But in your case…they have never seen a black man, they do not know the language, and the subject is very difficult. Yet despite all these obstacles, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is going through. It is wonderful.

Ghanaśyāma: The whole world feels indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Prabhupāda: *(proudly):* They must! I am giving real civilization. There is no hope for this Western so-called civilization. Hari Sauri’s grandmother advised his mother to kill him while he was still in the womb. There is no culture, no religion. For women – no husband; for father – no son.

Ghanaśyāma: And in many places – no food.

Prabhupāda: That I have also experienced. In Russia we could not get sugar, wheat, rice, milk, or fruit. As they make their materialistic programs, these things will finish. It is stated in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam. (Indignantly):* You can tell them frankly, “If you don’t take to Krishna consciousness, these things will not be available. Then you will have to eat your philosophy. You will have to eat your own son’s fetus.” *(He pauses, reflecting on the injustices perpetrated by a godless society.)* Such a nasty civilization. *(There is silence. Tamal Krishna seeks to divert Prabhupāda’s thoughts to a more pleasant subject.)*

Tamal Krishna: Śrīla Prabhupāda, Rāmeśvara Mahārāja has sent something very special for your pleasure. *(He hands Śrīla Prabhupāda a new publication.) Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Tenth Canto, Part One.

Prabhupāda: *(respectfully touching the* Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam *to his forehead*): Ah, this is my life! If book distribution increases, I shall never die. I will live for centuries. *(His strength has noticeably increased just by his holding the new book.)* Life is not within these bones, or manufactured in the chemist’s lab. The living force, the spirit soul, is superior to these bones and flesh. There is a science by which you can sustain life only in bones. Hiraṇyakaśipu did it.

Abhirāma: You are also doing it, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Prabhupāda: So, take care of the bones as long as possible. But always remember what real life is. *(He turns the pages, enlivened.)* Print more and more books. All that you collect, use half for printing, and half you may spend as you like. Whatever English books we have, translate them into all languages.

Ghanaśyāma: One Communist professor remarked that all intellectuals and scholars should read your books.

Prabhupāda: If they don’t, their learning is imperfect. *Asam Pūrnam.*

Harikeśa: Your books teach ideal communism. *Bhāgavataa* communism. They are the only solution for the world’s problem because they contain directions for every section of society. No one has ever done a greater service.

Prabhupāda: *(smiling):* These books have got a standing appreciation. Wherever they go, we shall conquer.

Harikeśa: Everyone else speculates.

Prabhupāda: But our philosophy is realistic and practical. There is no imagination. Everything is based upon authoritative statements of the *ācāryas.* That is the difference between the materialists and us. They only take interest if something is different and new.

D.L. Roy has written a song *(singing):*

Put your head down,  
Your legs up,  
You must do something new,  
No matter how old or ugly  
Or rubbish it is!

*(They all laugh.)* But we say, “Stick to the old.” Childish means you cannot stick to one principle. Therefore I say the Western civilization is childish. One car design finished, and next year a different one. *(His delivery quickens as he becomes absorbed in preaching.)* A devil’s workshop. Entanglement in useless work. Chewing the chewed. Topless, bottomless. Naked dancing. This is material civilization. This is their business. Am I right about this? So remain Krishna conscious. Stick to the principles. You are making definite progress. I can see you feel it. *(Looking to each for confirmation):* Harikeśa Mahārāja? Tamal Krishna? Ghanaśyāma Prabhu? *(Each nods in agreement.)* This *Bhāgavatam* is sufficient to nourish us all.

Harikeśa: So you must go on feeding us. Give us more and more books.

Prabhupāda: *{humbly):* I am only a child. *(Crying:)* But I have tried to please my Guru Mahārāja. I have not written. It is Krishna. Krishna and my Guru Mahārāja. They have sent all of you to help me. Otherwise, what could I do, all alone, an old man with no friends in America? America is so good to me, to give me money, men, everything – I see no difference between one country and another, but because the Americans have given me much facility, I cannot forget my obligations to them. I want to make them happy, and through them the whole world.

*Your humble servant,*  
Tasmal Krishna Goswami

## Vāsudeva dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet on the auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

It is very difficult for even the mystic *yogīs* to see a saintly person like you. Indeed, saintly persons are very rarely seen. I do not know what kind of pious activity I performed for you to grace me by coming to Fiji and staying with us without difficulty.

When something uncommon happens in one’s progressive spiritual life, it should be understood to be incurred by *ajñāta-sukṛti*, or pious activities beyond one’s knowledge. To see personally the Supreme Personally of Godhead or His pure devotee is not an ordinary incident. When such things happen, they should be understood to be caused by previous pious activity, as confirmed in *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.28): *yeṣāṁ tv anta-gataṁ pāpaṁ janānāṁ puṇya-karmaṇām.* One who is completely freed from all the resultant actions of sinful activities and who is absorbed only in pious activities can engage in devotional service.

I am very fortunate to have you as my spiritual master. Please guide me so that I can be fixed in your service forever.

*Your servant,*  
Vāsudeva dāsa Puñja

## Yaśomatīnandana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet, which are the shelter for exalted demigods and great sages. As the years go by in the horrible ocean of material life, we infantlike children miss you more and more but manage to survive by remembering your exalted glories. Even though the twentieth century is full of cheaters and imposters, this very century has produced Your Divine Grace, the crest jewel of all saintly persons and devotees. Therefore, this material world with all the twentieth-century *māyā,* atom bombs, slaughterhouses, scandals, and hypocrisy has become glorious. As Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī explains:

*viśvam pūrṇa-sukhāyate vidhi-mahendrādiś ca kīṭāyate*  
*yat-kārunye-katākṣa-vaibhavavatāṁ tani gaurahi stumaḥ*

**“For one who has had the fortune of receiving one merciful glance from Lord Gaurasundara, the whole world becomes fully joyful, and even the positions of exalted demigods such as Brahmā and Śiva are considered by him to be no better than worms.”**

Indeed, this world has received a merciful glance from Lord Caitanya in the form of Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we are full of hope that in spite of this horrible age of Kali, we have been saved by Krishna.

As such, the path of spiritual life is very dangerous: *kṣurasya dhārā* (razor’s edge). But by the mercy of a pure devotee spiritual master, it becomes easy to please Krishna. Now, however, the controversy is how to please Śrīla Prabhupāda. You said, “Simply chant Hare Krishna and dance in ecstasy and take *Krishna – prasādam* and become happy.” The process of Krishna consciousness is very simple – simple for the simple and difficult for the crooked. So whatever difficulties we are facing are due to our crooked minds. The greatest misfortune is that while each of us is trying to serve you, we are all pulling your limbs in different directions, causing you great pain. Worse still is that we use your auspicious name and instructions to support our own desires and opinions. On this auspicious day of your appearance, we pray at your lotus feet so that we may give up all our petty weaknesses of heart, our material desires, and accept only your instructions as our heart and soul. *Guru mukha padma vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, āra nā koriha mane āśā:* “My only desire is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from his lotus mouth.” The most important thing is to unitedly push on your Krishna consciousness movement. By your transcendental vision, you have designed a special plan to free the conditioned souls from the clutches of *māyā.* That plan is to attack *māyā* in a united way. “United we stand, divided we fall.” It is not possible that powerful *ācāryas* such as yourself, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, and Śrīla Bhaktivinoda will emerge every day. But if we stick to your plan, even ordinary neophytes such as ourselves can do the miracles by preaching and distributing your books and bringing conditioned souls to Krishna consciousness. One of us may not be able to take the burden, but all of us combined will give the world necessary direction. Our unity will be the testimony of love and surrender in Krishna consciousness. You achieved so much in eleven short years. You have shown the path, laid the foundation. We simply have to follow you and not imitate you, and we will be protected from the current of this world.

You never once deviated from the instructions of your spiritual master, and that is how you gained strength. All the scriptures sing the glories of the pure devotee spiritual master. Most spiritual masters deliver a few persons at a particular time and space, but you, by your extraordinary vision, will deliver millions of people for thousands of years. That is why you are known as founder-*ācārya.*

Lord Krishna says:

*janma karma ca me divyam evam yo vetti tattvataḥ*  
*tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so ‘rjuna*

**“One who knows the transcendental nature of My appearance and activities as they are, with full understanding, does not have to take birth in the material world upon leaving this body, but comes to My abode.”**

To understand the Lord means to understand through His pure devotees. *Madbhaktāḥ pūjābhyadhikāḥ:* “My devotees are more worshipable than Me.” To know Lord Krishna one must know Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and to know Crī Caitanya, one must know you.

*gaurāngera saṅgi-gaṇe nitya-siddha kari māne*  
*se jay brajendrasuta-pāsa*

**“One who considers the associate of Lord Caitanya to be eternally liberated goes to the abode of the son of the king of Vraja, Nanda Mahārāja.”**

You have fulfilled the prophecy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. The Lord told Rūpa and Sanātana to establish the holy places in Vṛndāvana and write books, and He told Lord Nityānanda to deliver Bengal. But the Lord gave the whole world, with its *kirāṭas, pulindas, pulkaśas, abhṛas, śumbhas, yavanas, mlecchas* and *khasas* etc., to Your Divine Grace to be delivered – a historical achievement unparalleled in the past. Writing books and letters, giving lectures every day, establishing temples and communes, *gurukulas, varṇāśrama-dharma –* so much in eleven years. Vedavyāsa will have to write another *purāṇa,* which will be sung daily in the assembly of demigods and sages. Indeed, one who knows your glories knows the science of Krishna consciousness.

Some people think that giving you such an exalted special seat of honor will mean we don’t want to be like you. We don’t want to take up your legacy. But the fact is that in the entire history of mankind, there has not been as powerful a preacher, as powerful an *ācārya,* as Your Divine Grace. Not just because we are disciples do we say this out of sentiment. Neutral observers, respectable scholars such as Professor Harvey Cox, observed: “This life of Śrīla Prabhupāda is pointed proof that one can be a transmitter of Truth and still be a vital and singular person. At what almost anyone would consider a very advanced age, when most people would be resting on their laurels, he harkened to the mandate of his own spiritual teacher and set out on a difficult and demanding voyage to America. Śrīla Prabhupāda is one in a thousand, maybe one in a million.”

The poet Allen Ginsberg gives you tribute like this: “Swami Bhaktivedanta came to the USA and went swiftly to the archetype spiritual neighborhood, the New York Lower East Side, and installed intact an ancient, perfectly preserved piece of street India. He adorned a storefront as his *āśrama* and adored Krishna therein and by patience and good humor, singing, chanting and expounding Sanskrit terminology day by day established Krishna consciousness in the psychedelic (mind manifesting) center of America East. To choose to attend to the Lower East Side, what kindness and humility and intelligence!”

Thus thousands and thousands of such academic observers are simply astounded by your powerful achievement. How can we, your disciples, take it lightly and think as if “you just tried a little and hit the jackpot. And with little effort I can also do the same”?? Thus thinking you to be ordinary man, without knowing your special potency, if we imitate you that will be a great offense. Certainly we can follow in your footsteps, and by your mercy we can make miracles. But as soon as we think we are doing it, we fall down like so many of my illustrious Godbrothers. ISKCON is all the way yours, and whatever happens in ISKCON will be your credit eternally. And that is the saving grace for me, so that I will never have to become proud and think myself to be dear. It is certainly safer to be a servant of your servant than to be a master. The greatest thing about you is that you never introduced any concoctions. Everything about you was standard. When I first joined, I read *Krishna* book, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Śrī Īśopaniṣad*, standard Vedic literatures. No concoctions. Rice, *dāl, capātī, subjī* for *prasādam; dhotī* and *kurtā,* and even Bengali sash, *sārīs* for women. You had no doubt this was universal dress and must be accepted. Thus without any attachment you introduced all the spiritual paraphernalia one by one with the touch of an expert and conquered the world for Krishna. I am simply a wretched son of a great father like you. Just as an ordinary father becomes very proud and joyful upon seeing his son become a great man, I am an ordinary child relishing the unbounded glories showered by the world upon you. My only claim to fame is that I am your eternal spiritual son. Now if somehow I always remember this and go on serving your mission with my limited potency, my life will be successful.

Seeing so many big, big generals from our side defecting to the enemy camp of *māyā*, we no longer have a desire to become a big general. Let me go on fighting as an ordinary soldier, like you in the old days, when you tried to go shop by shop in Delhi and Vṛndāvana selling your *Back to Godhead* paper, struggling against all odds, struggling against a materialistic nonreceptive, hypocritical populace. Let me go on preaching as a small man door to door all my life, because ultimately, I have to fly my own airplane. My friends and relatives, followers and disciples, temples and gardens cannot take me to Krishna. Only you can take me to Krishna by giving me the power to tolerate everything, making me lower than a blade of grass, removing all false pride.

So, on this auspicious day, please give me a drop of your mercy so that I can go on chanting and hearing your glories life after life, thereby pleasing Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Krishna.

*Your servant,*  
Yaśomatīnandana dāsa

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, the savior of the most fallen.

You have taught the world who God is and what His sublime attributes are. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has informed us that by the grace of the spiritual master, the Lord is revealed, and by the grace of the Lord, the spiritual master is revealed.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for delivering Krishna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Please accept this offering of poetry on this most holy day of Vyāsa-pūjā.

Your Divine Grace has lifted the cover  
That allures the poor soul from his lover.  
This temptress, Māyā, is eclipsed by your words  
When describing the King, the Lord of cowherds.

He is youthful, handsome, with a most cunning nature,  
And pious men utter His pure nomenclature.  
He is beckoned by mother, and then she will chide  
For His pilfering butter and then going to hide.

His sylvan abode, a land of frolic and fun,  
There brother and He do roll, jump, and run.  
Brāhmaṇas, cows, and companions most dear  
Engulfed by His charm, they encounter no fear.

Silken yellows and frame of blue hue,  
A turban, feather, and anklets to view.  
Admirers are striken by the musical stick  
Whose note make animate and inanimate contradict.

The Kauravas rallied in great defiance.  
They mustered a challenge with brute alliance.  
But He came to shield the pious five;  
The asuric force came not out alive.

Spread through the sky, planets, and space in between,  
Eyes, mouths, bellies without limit could be seen  
By the Āryan and celestials; they marveled at His form.  
“Time I am!” He thundered, as if pronouncing the worst storm.

Word wranglers, rogues who garble His song  
Think Him to be human; in this they’ve done wrong.  
Lust, anger, and greed overwhelm them indeed.  
These sins stem from envy, the original seed.

Your Divine Grace has undone the mask  
Of mammon and matter. How long will this last  
In the presence of Govinda, the ultimate truth?  
I denounce my vile body and declare His as sooth.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakty-abhayacaraṇa Swami

*bhavad-vidhā bhāgavatās*  
*tīrtha-bhūtāḥ svayaṁ vibho*  
*tīrthī-kurvanti tīrthāni*  
*svāntaḥ-sthena gadābhṛtā*

**“The saints of your category are in themselves places of pilgrimage. Because of their purity, they are constant companions of the Lord and, therefore, can purify the places of pilgrimage.” (*****Bhāg.*** **1.13.10)**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to the day of your glorious transcendental appearance.

Whenever we are proven by difficulties, the remembrance of your advice becomes more necessary. Again your presence was required in an urgent manner this year through your letters, books, and documents in Māyāpur’s meetings. Your instructions become more important, wise and profound with the passing of time. Ten years after your departure, you still are our unquestionable guide and proprietor. You are the pure preacher, invincible and gigantic that pulverizes any obstacle that presents itself amidst our dull intentions to help your transcendental mission.

Just as Lord Krishna puts the sun and the moon in this universe – dark by nature – to illuminate it, in the same way He has sent you to illuminate those who like me are perpetually in the obscurity of ignorance. You are the chosen devotee of Lord Caitanya to save all the actual and future Jagāis and Mādhāis. What can I understand (a Jagāi and Mādhāi) about your transcendental qualities? I don’t have words to glorify you. Only a pure devotee can understand another pure devotee. I only know that you are my savior. I am your property. You are my master and I am your slave. Hit my hard head. Clean the dust of my heart so that I may become conscious of you and of my condition as a servant. Otherwise, what is the use of living? In my visit to Vṛndāvana this year I heard one of the inhabitants saying this popular phrase: “A wise man can learn even from a dumb man, but a dumb man cannot learn even from a wise man.” This aphorism is very appropriate for me.

You have come to this world to save it. Let me help you. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 1.91, you explain in the purport: “When artificial religion dominates from the mental platform, real religion stays dormant. The living being can awaken this religion in a dormant state, if he hears with a pure heart.” Therefore I beg you so that you would purify my heart more and more with your Bhaktivedanta purports so that I may become helpful in your mission.

You have revolutionized the concept of existence all over the world with your marvelous books. But the world hasn’t experienced yet the most marvelous thing: becoming Krishna conscious. But, by your unmotivated mercy, they will be, in the proper time.

You have kindly favored us, your disciples, with the responsibility of continuing your own mission, despite our being so unqualified as we are, as you followed your own words. “It was the best way to conduct the situation.”

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 23.104, you write: “This Krishna consciousness movement continues the tradition of the Six Gosvāmīs, especially Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī and Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmi. Serious students of this Krishna consciousness movement must understand the great responsibility to preach the cult of Vṛndāvana (devotional service to the Lord) all over the world. We now have a nice temple in Vṛndāvana, and serious students can take up this responsibility and render the best service to humanity by educating people in Krishna consciousness.”

By your infinite mercy I have been able to write you once again my Vyāsa-pūjā offering, and, because of the same reason, I have been able to continue preaching Krishna consciousness another year more in the Spanish-speaking countries. For all this I am eternally indebted to you. Please give me the pure intelligence to continue serving you as you deserve.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Bhakty-abhayacaraṇa Swami

## Bhavānanda Rāya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your appearance I would like to make this humble offering to you, my eternal father. You have trained us to hear the truth and recognize it. You have trained us to seek out Krishna consciousness wherever we may find it. You have trained us to preach Krishna consciousness everywhere, as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu ordered, *yāre dekha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa āmāra ajñāya guru hana tara ei deśa.* You have given us everything, but in our childlike way we have made so many mistakes.

There are three types of disciples:

Third class: one who executes the spiritual master’s instructions without due consideration and usually blunders.  
Second class: one who hears the orders of the spiritual master and very intelligently executes them.  
First class: One who understands the desire or purpose of the spiritual master and takes it upon himself to fulfill it. That is, sometimes the real desire or purpose is beyond just the instruction itself.

You have given us ISKCON and the desire to bring all the conditioned souls within the fold. The spirit of ISKCON lives within the heart of the sincere devotee and cannot be equated with the condition of the form of ISKCON. You did not build temples to facilitate our eating and sleeping, but to attract the less fortunate to Krishna. As you once said in a letter, the temples are meant as bases for our preaching.

In the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* 4.28.31you state: “Among Vaiṣṇavas there may be some difference of opinion due to everyone’s personal identity, but despite all personal differences, the cult of Krishna consciousness must go on.” You have said that we must show our love for you by cooperating amongst ourselves. Without this cooperation we will not be able to stand against *māyā.* Therefore let us put aside all our political differences, personal desires, ambitions, and conceptions of “us” and “them.”

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to your divine lotus feet.

*Your servant,*  
Bhavānanda Rāya Swami

## Bir Krishna dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. Material relationships dissolve due to long periods of physical separation, whereas spiritual relationships grow deeper with time.

As the span of time increases between the day of your leaving this material world and the present, I am finding that my relationship with you is becoming increasingly closer and more personal. Also my appreciation of what your position of founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON means is growing.

The title founder-*ācārya* is composed of the two words *founder* and *ācārya.* The word *founder,* of course, means he whose conceptions, energy, intelligence, and effort comprise the foundation of this movement. The *ācārya* means he who teaches by example. Taken together, the two words signify the person by whose example, conception, energy, and intelligence this movement is based.

You will notice I used the present tense, “is based,” in my last sentence. That is, your position in ISKCON is not a temporal one. Your relationship to the movement as founder-*ācārya* is dynamic and ongoing. You *are* the founder-*ācārya*, not *were* the founder-*ācārya,* and you will continue to be the founder-*ācārya*. That means that your teachings form the basis of our Krishna consciousness movement and the basis of daily lives in Krishna consciousness. Therefore no one else can claim to be founder*-ācārya* of ISKCON or any part of ISKCON.

How are your teachings made known to us? In two ways – by your personal example and by your writings (your books, lectures, etc.). So at the same time that we study your books, we should be studying your example and letting your example be the prototype for our lives.

We can only engage others, ourselves, and the material energy in the service of Krishna by the mercy and direction of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. That is, we do not have the prerogative to decide what is spiritual activity and what is material activity. We do not have the authority to authorize “so-called devotional service” that you would not accept if you were here. We have the responsibility to examine each and every activity that we perform, choice that we make, or direction that we give others, to see if you would or would not accept them. If you would not, we must reject such activity, choice, or direction. If you would accept (if you were here), we can go ahead with our plan.

I may be a *guru,* temple president, GBC, or other authority, but I cannot make an activity spiritual by my authorization. It must have the authorization of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is not spiritual, even though others may become spiritual simply by votes. We cannot become victims of the “emperor’s new clothes” psychology.

At every minute we should be questioning ourselves, “What would Śrīla Prabhupāda say about this? Would he approve? What would he want me to do?” Then we will make the correct decisions in every instance, and we will be realizing at every moment your position as founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON.

*Your servant,*  
Bir Krishna dāsa Goswami

## Daśaratha Paṇḍita Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your two lotus feet. On the most glorious of all days, the appearance day of Your Divine Grace, I would like to offer some small words of praise of Lord Krishna ‘s most dear servant.

Just as it is said of Lord Śiva, *vaiṣṇavānāṁ yathā śambhuḥ:* Lord Śiva is the most glorious of all Vaiṣṇavas, because he delivers the most fallen souls like ghosts and other such beings; similarly, you are also the greatest of all pure devotees, because you have gloriously descended into the Kali-yuga to deliver the most wretched souls, innumerable Jaghāis and Madhāis. Not only do you deliver them from their condemned and sinful ways of life, but you bestow upon them the most wonderful gift – devotional service in the *saṇkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya.

By your powerful and causeless mercy, this degraded world has been overflooded with transcendental literatures, temples, deities, festivals, *prasādam,* and, most important of all, the holy names Hare Krishna, Hare Krishna, Krishna Krishna, Hare Hare, Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. You have taught that the chanting of the holy names of the Lord is the only medicine that can cure all the ills of Kali-yuga and elevate one to the platform of pure love of God.

For a disciple, my only hope for success in spiritual life is to be always engaged in the service of your lotus feet and to try to assist you in your mission of spreading Krishna consciousness. But, as I am still so full of material bad qualities, I am begging you to please destroy all the demoniac residues of material attachment that are still within my heart, so that I will be a help to you in your mission and not a burden. “One must conquer the modes of passion and ignorance by developing the mode of goodness, and then one must become detached from the mode of goodness by promoting oneself to the platform of *śuddha-sattva.* All this can be automatically done if one engages in the service of the spiritual master with faith and devotion. In this way one can conquer the influence of the modes of nature.” *(Bhāg.* 7.15.25)

*Aspiring to be your fully surrendered servant,*  
Daśaratha Paṇḍita Swami

## Dyutidhāra Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirādnhasya jñānānjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitam yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

O blessed transcendental captain, please guide us past the reefs of dissention, protecting us from the waves of despair. Your unfathomable kindness is our sole beacon over the ocean of darkness. In these waters dwell the sharks of impersonalism and the crocodiles of voidism. You boldly steer the lifeboat of Vaiṣṇavism, rescuing us from this Titanic situation. Except for Your Divine Grace, no one searched for survivors.

How can we call upon such a savior such as you with less than full conviction, knowing only you braved such a struggle to save us? In all humility we are praying that now, as you are bringing us nearer to the shore of Kṛṣṇaloka, we not jump back again into the ocean of agitation.

Please protect us by the steady lamp of your brilliant instructions, so that we may always stay moored at the shelter of your lotus feet. Without your guidance we are all fallen, without your compassion all impoverished and without your mercy completely homeless. We cannot survive here in this place but by the mercy of Your Divine Grace.

*A fallen soul calling for your protection,*  
Dyutidhāra Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet on this most auspicious day of Vyāsa-pūjā.

As another year has passed and I am searching my barren heart for some appropriate words to properly glorify Your Divine Grace, I find myself in the midst of a great struggle to defeat *māyā*’s advances in attempting to remove me from the main field of preaching you have given me.

This situation is a source of tremendous anxiety, and ironically, the source of very deep spiritual satisfaction. For to be put into such a helpless condition – in which there is no material solution – I am impelled to meditate upon your *vapuḥ* and *vāṇī* as my only shelter.

Meditation on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is unlimited. You are an unfathomable ocean of purely transcendental qualities and pastimes, so wonderfully attractive that even a heavily conditioned soul like myself is spontaneously drawn to rendering you service in very deep appreciation for what you have so mercifully given. Indeed, complete absorption in serving you is the perfection of life.

Your transcendental personality is an embodiment of all devotional qualities. By your perfect example and sublime instructions you have paved the road back to Godhead. Please give me the strength to faithfully follow the guidelines and instructions you have given, grant me the opportunity to serve your servants, and empower me to fulfill your most cherished desires. The greatest glorification of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is to become your perfect follower, not adding or subtracting anything, and preaching the same to others. For one who calls himself your servant, there is nothing else to aspire after.

This life is very short, and the uncertainty of how and where we may be situated in this material world is a reality that has to be accepted and tolerated. My only prayer on this auspicious day is that wherever or however I may be situated, whether in heaven or hell, you continue to watch over and guide me and allow me to humbly offer my body, mind, an words – absolutely – in your service eternally.

*Your servant,*  
Giridhārī Swami

## Guṇagrāi dāsa Gosvami

**Our Meeting**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, father of all,  
The first time I saw you I slept through your call.  
Though you offered all shelter against māyā’s attack,  
I wallowed in ignorance as I lay on my back.  
My feet at you glaring, no greater offense:  
Bodily comfort at the soul’s expense.  
And the more I reflect on that night I can see  
How degraded and foolish I let myself be.  
And how kind and merciful you were to me then,  
For you sparked my desire to hear you again.  
And by your love and your wisdom, my eyes that were blind  
Were gradually filled with the wisdom to find  
Lord Krishna, for whom I’d searched for years,  
With nothing to show but confusion and tears.  
O dispeller of doubt, you’ve perfectly shown  
That we’re part of Lord Krishna, not drifting alone.  
Nevertheless, I go on still offending,  
A devotee by name, but simply pretending.  
And still you keep giving me chance after chance,  
In the hope that some day I’ll want to advance.  
And now is the time, surrender I must,  
For I don’t want your mission to turn into dust.  
And I don’t want the love for the Lord that you’ve kindled  
To again become lust and gradually dwindle.

**Your Vision**

I think of all your dreams and the parts you wanted played.  
I think of our mistakes and of those who’ve drifted away.  
I think, too, that we’ve gained a lot through the fire of ordeal,  
That we’re wiser now, more on course, in line with what is real.  
Countless aparādhas! Forgive us if you will.  
Without your grace we have no hope, and all will come to nil.  
Tṛṇād api sunīcena….na dhanaṁ na janaṁ na sundarīm.  
We pray the pure in heart will stand as leaders of your team.  
If so, ISKCON again will shine with your presence as before,  
Your vāṇī fully manifest, illusion’s reign no more.  
We’ll all be brought together, the course of action clear,  
Showered with your mercy, with freedom from the fear  
That we will fail to give you what now stands incomplete:  
The fulfillment of your mission, the world at Krishna ‘s feet.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

It is expected, according to the tenets of scripture, and traditionally in the heritage of thousands and thousands of years of disciplic succession, to offer you words of praise on this glorious day in the format of awe and veneration. I dare not supersede such formal laws, lest I commit the offense of considering you anything but what you are: the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, the Lord’s representative within the mundane sphere, you whose glories are sung throughout the three worlds, who are respected and praised even by the topmost of demigods, sages, and saintly personalities. O *śaktyāveṣa avatāra! Pārivrājakācārya!* Topmost swanlike personality!

But, Prabhupāda, I beg you to let me put aside these formal lines and remember and address you as just that – Prabhupāda. Of course, even this name carries the grandeur of “one at whose feet all other masters sit.” But for those of us who knew you and are coming to know you now, that name carries memories of so many moments sublime. I must admit that as much as I can appreciate your unparalleled achievements throughout this world in establishing the *saṇkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, I take shelter in remembering your daily activities in love of God. As we forever remember and repeat the glorious pastimes of the Lord as the sustenance of our very lives, so we now exist in separation by remembering yours.

It was the small moments with you that shook me free from Māyā’s grasp, associating with your transcendental personality, watching in awe the sublime activities of an eternal resident of Vṛndāvana. By Krishna ‘s grace, moments with you remain fresh within my mind, and remembrance of those moments destroys my obstinate nature to seek happiness within this material world. It seems so long ago I left that ugly place, and looking back is only awkward now. Through your grace I perceive the desperate struggle that alone is material life. What would have taken lifetimes of preparation to see this world for what it is was achieved in moments through your association.

It was you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who captured my heart! Once, when I was on the way to Europe with you, the customs official at the New York airport treated you harshly as he asked you to open up your bag. The lock was jammed, and you looked to me for assistance. I remember coming forward and commanding the officer to step aside, chastising that he did not know with whom he was dealing. Taking the bag, I opened the lock and presented it defiantly in front of him, as if to say, “How dare you treat my spiritual master in this way!” And then, as if a small reward from Krishna, the customs official let only you and me pass through, the first to walk to the plane, the other passengers left for the moment in another bundle of confusion.

Suddenly I was alone with you, and you were saying, “Just see the rascals. So much technology and material progress, but no good qualities.” You then turned to me and said, “What do you think?” In my nervousness I could only blurt out, “Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda!” Again you repeated, “All rascals, what do you think?” I again replied, “Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda!”….and then I was convinced forever more that one cannot develop good qualities unless he is a devotee of the Lord.

While in flight the movie came on, and I quickly buried my head in the *Bhagavad-gītā.* Suddenly I heard your secretary laughing, and as I looked over I saw him watching the film, a silent movie of the antics of Charlie Chaplin. I immediately returned to my book and thought, “How is he watching that movie?” Then suddenly I heard you also lightly laughing, and as I turned to see you also watching the film. I then put down the book to watch that Charlie Chaplin. After the movie, at the back of the plane I whispered to your servant, “But what about that movie, I mean, you know movies are….” He interrupted, saying, “Oh, but Śrīla Prabhupāda remarked that Charlie Chaplin’s humor was original.” I thought, “Yes, Prabhupāda is seeing Krishna everywhere. Krishna is the original source of everything, including humor.” I returned to my seat somewhat embarrassed for having seen only Mr. Chaplin.

When we arrived in London, your secretary told me to stay at the airport to await a bag of yours that was arriving late. All of you went to the Bury Place temple, and I remained behind alone, lamenting that I would miss the temple reception. When that heavy bag came hours later I dragged it to the taxi, and upon arriving at the temple I again pulled it up four flights of stairs to your room. Knocking lightly, I entered and was surprised to see you standing just before me! In my exhausted state I immediately offered obeisances, falling upon the floor. Suddenly I felt your sharp slap upon my back! Saying a few words you then left the room, and I slowly stood up, finding only your astonished servant there before me, mouth agape. I asked him, “Prabhu, what did Prabhupāda say?” He replied, “Prabhupāda told you, ‘So much endeavor in the material world, but when you go back to Godhead, everything will be easy and sublime!’” And since then I have never known it differently, even in the most trying of times – simply by remembering you.

So please forgive me if I do not recommit my vows in praising words before you today. And please forgive me if I do not use these minutes to extol your glories in fancy prose. And please do not think I am only offering sentimental words of refrain! I am remembering you as you once appeared before me, and this simple remembrance means no longer to see the endless repetition of birth and death.

O Prabhupāda! Thank you for those precious moments (and more) of your transcendental pastimes. Thank you for saving me from a hellish life, devoid of your association. Thank you for accepting me as your disciple, when in fact I am not the least deserving. And thank you, Prabhupāda, for the privilege to remain behind and carry out your orders divine.

And Prabhupāda, if I could take just one more moment of your precious time on this auspicious day to make a small request…..may I, sometime, somewhere in my long journey back to home, carry that bag for you again? And then perhaps you’d slap me free from Māyā’s grasp as once before and, with the touch of your lotus hand, free me from all inebrieties that keep me from purely serving you. With that grace I’ll toil here for as many lives as you see fit to carry out your mission, knowing one day your promise will come true – you’ll take me home to be with you again, where everything is easy and sublime.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Īśvara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories unto you, on this divine occasion of your appearance day, and all glories to your spiritual family, this ISKCON movement, with all your disciples and followers.

How sweet and beautiful it is to see your loving spiritual family, all assembled together with all your numberless children, ever expanding, all of them united to chant the holy name of Lord Gaurāṅga. “This Krishna consciousness movement is so nice,” you so many times told us. And actually this is something wonderful. It is such a nice thing to be in Krishna consciousness, rendering devotional service, being able to say “no” to this mundane world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I just humbly pray for your causeless mercy so that I can truly taste the mellow of the relationship with you and this transcendental movement of yours, which you declared to be your own body. I know that because of my jaundiced state I cannot taste this sweetness, but I have to keep trying. This holy name you gave us is so sweet, we just need strong faith to go on and not fall prey to so many other so-called tastes of this world. How easy it is for us to go astray, to go back again to chewing what has already been chewed. What fools we are!

We have to realize deeply in our hearts that you were very much fond of this spirit of surrendering everything to Krishna. Actually, you were very proud of all your transcendental sons and daughters who had given up illicit sex, intoxication, meat-eating, and gambling in order to take up this chanting of the holy name. So we know for sure how pure our lives should be, but instead we go looking for something else. And then I realize it is only by your personal example and enthusiasm that we will get the strength to give all this up. You preached and preached, showing us how to do the same. Whenever I see somebody taking up this movement or doing some nice work, this is an impetus for me to go on myself with my duty. When I see setbacks or fights among us, I should try to remember you more, how you warned us not to do so. Actually, I want to realize your words and not stay in this neophyte stage. You said the temples were not supposed to be places for eating and sleeping but places we can get strength to go out and drop the bombs (books) on the laps of all the conditioned souls. How far are we from this realization? You warned us against this tendency of staying at the temple, just ringing the bells. You gave the example of your Guru Mahārāja, who was so morose because of the fights among his disciples living underneath the same roof. He told you he wanted to sell the temple and get money to print books, and he gave you the personal order to always print and distribute books.

But now I ask myself, “Aren’t we committing the same mistake?” How foolish we are, trying to live happily by material arrangements and not forcing our minds to surrender to Krishna! How long are we going to remain on this neophyte and fallen platform? What a shame! We cannot be bad disciples, especially because you are such a wonderful master. All of us, members of this Krishna consciousness movement, should endeavor ourselves to please you by being perfect disciples. We must honor you by being fixed-up devotees of Your Divine Grace.

*Your fallen servant,*  
Īśvara Swami

## Jagat Guru Swami

**The Spiritual Evolution of the Name**

“His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda”

Each and every syllable of the transcendental name of our beloved spiritual master and eternal father brings joy and gladness to our heart. We are full of transcendental pride about the name of our Guru Mahārāja, and therefore we would like to share the nectar of the evolution of that name with all those who adore the lotus feet of our divine master on the auspicious day of his appearance in this world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was born a pure devotee of Lord Krishna, and this fact is accepted far and wide. Not only was he born a pure devotee, but he was also born in the family of pure devotees. Śrīla Prabhupāda writes in the dedication of the *Krishna* book, “To my father, Gour Mohan De (1849-1930), a pure devotee of Krishna….”

When Śrīla Prabhupāda was born, his father named him Abhay Charan. Abhay Charan means “one who is fearless, having taken shelter at the lotus feet of Krishna.” Śrīla Prabhupāda ’s uncle called him Nandulal because he was born on Nandotsava, the day Krishna ’s father observed Krishna ’s birth ceremony. But that was not the choice of his father, and thus Prabhupāda was named Abhay Charan De.

Later in his childhood, the family gave Śrīla Prabhupāda the nickname Kachori Mukhi because of his great liking for *kachoris.* That, though, was only a nickname for his childish behavior, and he soon left that name behind.

When Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a grown man, was initiated by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura in 1933, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta changed his name from Abhay Charan to Abhay Caraṇāravinda. The word *caraṇāravinda* conveys a more developed concept of the word *caraṇa,* or surrendered at the lotus feet.” Thus Bhaktisiddhānta acknowledged the purity of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s father when he said, “His father has already named him.” Śrīla Prabhupāda once wrote about his father, “He was kind to me, and I imbibed from him the ideas later on solidified by my spiritual master, the eternal father.”

After his initiation, Śrīla Prabhupāda became known to his Godbrothers as Abhay Babu. Some years later, after the disappearance of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, a *sannyāsī* Godbrother, Bhaktisaraṅga Mahaṛaja, conferred a title of distinction upon Śrīla Prabhupāda for his exalted service. It is very surprising at this point to learn that Śrīla Prabhupāda received the title Bhaktisiddhānta, the very name of his *guru.*

In this regard one may refer to the League of Devotees registration papers kept in the BBT archives. There one can see in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s own handwriting, “Alias A.C. Bhaktisiddhānta dāsa Adhikāri.” In the League of Devotees’ papers this appears three times in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s own handwriting.

This happened sometime in 1953, and for a period of three or so months Śrīla Prabhupāda was known as A.C. Bhaktisiddhānta dāsa Adhikāri. Then there were some complaints from the Godbrothers. These complaints were not directed at Śrīla Prabhupāda, but out of loving sentiment, some thought that no one should be given the name Bhaktisiddhānta. That name or title should be held as most sacred.

However, it was agreed that Śrīla Prabhupāda should have a title of distinction due to his advanced qualifications in devotional service. At that time the Godbrothers met, and a particular Godbrother, who was very dear to Śrīla Prabhupāda and respected by all the branches of the Gaudiya Math, chose the name Bhaktivedanta, which was then conferred upon Śrīla Prabhupāda. Generally it is known that the Gaudiya Math gave Śrīla Prabhupāda the distinguished title Bhaktivedanta, but few devotees actually know that the dear associate who chose the name for our beloved spiritual master was Swami B.R. Śrīdhara Mahārāja of Navadvīpa Dhāma.

Śrīla Prabhupāda then became known respectfully as A.C. Bhaktivedanta Prabhu, or just A.C. Bhaktivedanta. By 1959 Śrīla Prabhupāda was destined by the transcendental arrangement of the Lord to take *sannyāsa,* the renounced order of life. On September 17, 1959, Śrīla Prabhupāda accepted the renounced order of life from another dear Godbrother, Bhaktiprajñāna Keṣava Mahārāja. At that time Śrīla Prabhupāda received the title and name Swami Mahārāja. This name Swami Mahārāja was one of the 108 names for *sannyāsīs* chosen by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura.

All the time of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *sannyāsa* there was also a Godbrother present named Sanātana Prabhu, who also accepted *sannyāsa.* Sanātana Prabhu’s name became Bhaktivedanta Muni Mahārāja. It so happened, that from 1953 onwards, many members of the Gaudīya Math received the title Bhaktivedanta and that tradition still continues down to the present day. Therefore to distinguish amongst the Bhaktivedantas, they are known amongst one another by their *sannyāsa* names, such as Muni Mahārāja, Nārāyaṇa Mahārāja, Vāmana Mahārāja, etc. Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a *sannyāsī,* is known to his Godbrothers as Swami Mahārāja.

Śrīla Prabhupāda soon began his translation and purports of *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam,* entitled the “Bhaktivedanta Purports.” With the name Bhaktivedanta, our divine master came to the West to fulfill the words of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the desires of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura by spreading Krishna consciousness to every town and village in the world.

But to those to whom he came, to those who loved him, he was known affectionately as “Swamiji.” Of course, this is not really a proper title for one’s spiritual master. But they were young and without any Vaiṣṇava culture, and so out of his ocean of kindness Śrīla Prabhupāda accepted. Then one day a devotee – and I don’t know who, but whoever he is I bow at his feet – asked Śrīla Prabhupāda if there was any special way of addressing the spiritual master with affection, and Śrīla Prabhupāda replied, “Yes. You may call the spiritual master Prabhupāda.”

And a happy day for all of us to have the full-fledged mercy of our eternal spiritual master. Soon, “His Divine Grace” was added, and thus the name His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda came to be, by the grace of Krishna. A happy day for us, but not for all. When Śrīla Prabhupāda returned to India, many of his Godbrothers resented that he was being called Prabhupāda. At that time the same Godbrother who had given Prabhupāda the name Bhaktivedanta and whom Śrīla Prabhupāda loved dearly came forward and defended our spiritual master. He proclaimed that he was “fit to be called Prabhupāda.” He was the most confidential devotee of the Lord, he was empowered to preach Krishna consciousness all over the world, he was the shelter of tens of thousands of Vaiṣṇavas, he was “Prabhupāda.”

When referring to or addressing Prabhupāda, the devotees also began to say “Śrīla,” thus giving the highest respect as well as affection. All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swmai Prabhupāda, by whose grace we are engaged in the eternal service to the divine couple Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda! All glories to our divine master! All glories to his transcendental appearance in this world to deliver the fallen souls!

My dear master, by chanting your transcendental name and singing your transcendental glories, sins go far away. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are an ocean of nectar and the deliverer of your sincere devotees. I take shelter at your lotus feet.

*namaḥ oṁ viṣṇupādāya kṛṣṇ-preṣthāya bhūtale*  
*svāmi-śrī-bhaktivedānta-prabhupādāya te namaḥ*  
*gurvājñāṁ śirasi-dharyan śaktyāveṣa-sva-rūpiṇe*  
*hare-kṛṣṇeti-mantreṇa pāścātya-prācya-tāriṇe*  
*viśvācārya prabhāryāya divya-kāruṇya-mūrtaye*  
*śrī-bhāgavata-mādhurya-gītā-jñāna-pradāyine*  
*gaura-śrī-rūpa-siddhānta-sarasvatī-niṣeviṇe*  
*rādhā-kṛṣṇa-padāmbhoja-bhṛṅgāya gurave namaḥ*

**“I offer my humble obeisances unto His Divine A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Krishna on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Taking the order of his** ***guru*** **on his head, he became empowered by Nityānanda Prabhu to act as a** ***śaktyāveśa avatāra.*** **He distributed the Hare Krishna** ***mantra*** **all over the Eastern and Western worlds, delivering and uplifting all fallen souls. He is the best of millions of** ***jagad-gurus,*** **because he is the personification of divine mercy. He has distributed the sweet nectar of** ***Śrīmad-Bhagavatam*** **and the transcendental knowledge of** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **all over the world. He is constantly engaged in exclusive devotional service to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Ṭhākura, Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, and Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu. I offer my humble obeisances unto Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is like a bumblebee always tasting the nectar of the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Govinda.”**

*Your eternal servant,*  
Jagat Guru Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

The lotus feet of our spiritual master  
Have rescued us from spiritual disaster.  
We bow down to his feet with all affection.

Our spiritual master, friend, and guide  
Cuts down false ego and false pride.  
His words of knowledge give us all protection.

Our spiritual master’s lotus hand  
Palm upwards blossoms and expands,  
Its finger-petals pointing towards perfection.

From his lotus heart and mind  
His master’s teachings, well enshrined,  
Come forth in the nectar of his words.

May we keep those words inside us,  
Deep within our hearts to guide us,  
And tell many others what we’ve heard.

Prabhupāda magnanimously came:

Bold, courageous, fixed in bhakti,  
Emanating prema-śakti,  
Unattached to opulence and fame,

Joyful, friendly, and ecstatic,  
Humbly aristocratic,  
Always chanting Krishna’s holy name –

Prabhupāda, perfect teacher,  
Manifesting Krishna’s features  
Came within our vision like a swan.

Now we preach and sing about you.  
We can’t bear to live without you.  
Kindly take us with you where you’ve gone.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Keśava dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Very humbly I offer my *daṇdavats* at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! On this auspicious occasion of your appearance we can only meditate on your activities, instructions, and fond remembrances of you and pray to you for guidance to expand your movement so that it reaches more and more of the unlucky living entities of the material world and does not become changed by the forces of *māyā.*

When Lord Caitanya asked Rāmānanda Rāya what is the greatest pain, he replied that separation from a pure devotee constitutes the most painful existence. By the grace of Krishna, we fallen souls have come in contact with your merciful lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Such a great mercy is very, very rare. Now by your grace, we have found relief from this desertlike world, in the chanting of the holy name of Krishna; therefore our duty is to fulfill your real desire as your ever-committed servants to save all other souls who are still lost and confused. *Guru-kṛṣṇa-kṛpā:* by the mercy of Krishna one finds the *guru*, and by the grace of the *guru* one obtains Krishna.

You wrote in the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* (2.8.5): “It is said that a single pure devotee of the Lord can deliver all the fallen souls of the world. Thus one who is actually in the confidence of a pure devotee like Nārada or Śukadeva Gosvāmī and thus is empowered by one’s spiritual master, as Nārada was by Brahmājī, can not only deliver himself from the clutches of *māyā,* or illusion, but can deliver the whole world by his pure and empowered devotional strength.”

O Prabhupāda, your preaching potency is unlimited! No one has ever done what you are doing. Krishna says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that His most dear devotee is he who preaches His glories. So it is impossible to comprehend how dear you are to the Lord, because you have spread the glories of the Lord all over this planet, thus giving the opportunity to all living entities around the world to perform devotional service, which is desired even by the demigods.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want nothing again in my life but to remain a servant of your book distribution, although I am not even qualified. Therefore I beg to you, with whatever sincerity and seriousness is in my heart, to please let my mind, my body, acts, and words be interlocked in the service of assisting you in distributing these literatures. They are the proof that you live forever and that your sincere follower lives with you.

*Begging to remain your eternal servant,*  
Keśava dāsa Swami

## Krishna Balarām Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of your transcendental appearance, I am blessed with an opportunity both to glorify you and to confirm my ongoing devotional service by opening my heart to you. Please think of me as your innocent child and forgive me for my unlimited offenses.

It is confirmed in the Vedic scriptures that a pure *ācārya* like yourself is directly sent by Lord Krishna to rescue the suffering conditioned souls from the clutches of Māyā. As Lord Śrī Krishna Himself says, *ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān:* “A true *ācārya* is nondifferent from Me.” Although such a great soul may appear to be an ordinary person, he is in fact a *pārṣada,* or direct messenger of the Lord who descends from Śrī Golokadhāma. If he were not a direct messenger of the Lord, he could not execute *śuddha-bhakti,* the purest type of devotional service. An ordinary living entity, part of the Lord’s marginal potency, may act as a spiritual master of *brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas,* and *vaiśyas,* and he may also become *amala-ātmā,* a purified souls, by following the process of *vaidhi-bhakti,* but he cannot be called a *pārṣada.* A *pārṣada* is addressed as *sādśad-dhariḥ,* “directly the Lord,” as confirmed by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura in his *Śrī Guruvāṣṭaka* prayers. Such a *guru* is understood to be a *pavitra-guru*, the purest spiritual master.

After wandering all over the universe, a soul who is most fortunate comes in contact with a *pavitra-guru*, who instructs him in spiritual matters. A serious soul is one who desires to get out of this material world. Anyone who is hankering to be free from the cycle of birth, old age, disease, and death is a qualified candidate for spiritual life. The *caitya-guru*, who resides within the heart, appears before the fortunate soul in the form of a *vartmapradarśaka-guru,* the *guru* who guides him along the spiritual path and leads him to the *mahānta-guru,* the initiating spiritual master, who gives him *bhakti-dīkṣā.* Thus receiving spiritual guidance from the *vartmapradarśaka-guru* and *dīkṣā* from the *mahānta-guru,* the disciple receives enlivenment form the *caitya-guru* in the form of wonderful realizations. The *caitya-guru,* being the original spiritual master of the soul, gives such a serious person real happiness and satisfaction. Even if the spiritual master is not physically present, the Supersoul helps that individual remain attached to devotional service by giving him realizations from within the heart. Such a fortunate soul is said to have achieved the perfection of his human existence.

The connection with a pure spiritual master brings about purity, enthusiasm, clarity of consciousness, and clear, spiritually elevating realization. This is confirmed in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* where Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu says to His spiritual master (C.c. *Ādi* 7.81), *kibā mantra dilā, gosāñi, kibā tāra bala - japite japite mantra karila pāgala:* “My dear lord, what kind of *mantra* have you given Me? I have become mad simply by chanting this *mahā-mantra!”*

A pure disciple is always afraid of Māyā, and therefore the Lord says, *mā bhaya mā bhaya:* “Do not fear, do not fear.” Indeed, the Lord gives protection to the sincere devotee with His two arms, i.e., *śruti* and *smṛti.* The Lord also says C.c. *Madhya:* 7.129), *kabhu na vadhibe tomāra viṣaya taraṅga, punarapi ei thani pabe mora saṅga:* “If you follow this instruction, your materialistic life at home will not obstruct your spiritual advancement. Indeed, if you follow these regulative principles, we will again meet here, or, rather, you will never lose My company.” As Lord Caitanya confirms above, as soon as a disciple is initiated by a pure spiritual master, the disciple develops a real taste for spiritual life.

Anyone can claim to be a *nitya-siddha* and a genuine spiritual master, but the real symptoms of a true spiritual master are found in the scriptures. When a disciple’s consciousness genuinely improves he ecstatically dances and chants with raised arms, he executes devotional service with full determination and true humility, he becomes free from the propensity to criticize others, and he is interested only in going back home, back to Godhead. Such a disciple is said to have a true spiritual master.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, by some arrangement of destiny I have you as my spiritual master. I fervently pray to you and Krishna to give me the ability to develop the attitude of the *cātakī* bird, so that I may drink only the transcendental nectar of spiritual life. As the *cātakī* bird cannot drink water from the earth, may I also become so sincere that I will be unable to tolerate material life. May the attachment to politics, faultfinding, and fame not touch me, and may my exclusive desire be just to serve you. This is my sincere prayer, but it is impossible to be fulfilled without your causeless mercy. Please be merciful upon me, or I shall only weep. *Hari bol!*

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Krishna Balarām Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

“Cooperate together. Maintain what I have given you. Expand the preaching. Spread Krishna consciousness all over the world. Do something nice for Krishna. Give this one lifetime for Krishna’s service.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us many nice instructions to help us escape the cycle of repeated birth and death. Your life was a perfect example of pure Krishna conscious thinking, feeling, and acting. In a world that daily reveals the degraded and bestial behavior of today’s so-called leading politicians, preachers, and philosophers, your divine purity inspires us to keep trying.

As an expensive diamond displays many brilliant facets, you possess unlimited Vaiṣṇava qualities and potencies that continue to increase as we remember and examine your exemplary life. Though we once took you for granted, we are now beginning to appreciate the value of your association and teachings. Even university professors are becoming conscious of your greatness. Professor Harvey Cox praised you by saying, “Śrīla Prabhupāda was one in a thousand, maybe one in a million.”

Fortunately, for us and for the rest of humanity, you have entered your books to continually nourish and guide us on the path of pure devotional service. Whatever form of devotion we possess has been shaped by the cutting edge of your transcendental words. By this literary manifestation you can simultaneously teach everyone on the planet how to love Krishna and go home, back to Godhead.

Being eternally indebted, our duty is to give you (i.e., your transcendental books) to every human being. We forever pray that you please empower us to distribute millions of your books, so that every person on the earth planet will realize Śrīla Prabhupāda is our ever well-wisher.

*Your fallen disciple,*  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Navayogendra Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Krishna. Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, the savior of the whole world by spreading *sanātana-dharma* all over the world.

Dear Gurudeva, today is your divine appearance day. Yesterday, the Supreme Lord, Śrī Krishna, appeared, and today you appeared. Lord Śrī Krishna gave us the beautiful divine message of *Bhagavad-gītā,* but because of our fallen nature, we forgot this message. However, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, revived this message and gave us the real meaning of the human form of life. We were thinking the purpose of life is to enjoy this life, to eat, to drink, and be merry. But according to your divine preaching, this is not the purpose of life. Sense gratification is on the bodily platform. You gave us the true understanding that we are not this body, but soul, and we should act on the spiritual platform, because we are spiritual beings. Even so-called readers of *Bhagavad-gīṭa* were not able to understand this.

Your revival of this divine message has united the whole world. People from different walks of life, different castes, colors, and creeds, are living together like brothers. Living together for these people was next to impossible, but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, made the impossible possible. Once you were on a morning walk at Juhu Beach, Bombay. There with Your Divine Grace devotees of different nationalities were also walking. While walking, you mentioned that the United Nations has been trying to unite the nations for so long, but has failed, and you pointed out towards your disciples and said, “This is the real United Nations. This devotee is from Kenya, Africa; this is from Germany; this is from England; this is from the USA; this is from India, etc. They are all from different castes, colors, and creeds, and they are living together, eating together. They don’t fight like whites and blacks are fighting in South Africa.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us the highest understanding – that we are not the body. All our problems are due to our bodily concept of life. Very scientifically and logically you changed this concept. Those who are following in your footsteps are really becoming wonderful persons. According to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is the need of the time. Without this understanding the whole world is suffering. Śrīla Prabhupāda, by founding ISKCON you have given us the great hope that we can get rid of this suffering. You instructed all of us to work very hard to spread your divine mission. Please give me the strength so that I can be instrumental in your great ISKCON until the end of my life. Please give us the intelligence so that we can develop some perception and understand things as they really are. May we understand each other and cooperate with each other to push on your great ISKCON.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am so foolish, devoid of intelligence, and I don’t know how to glorify a *mahā-bhāgavata* like yourself. Please forgive me if there is any mistake in my writing or glorification. I have no qualification at all, but I have a great desire to remain your servant life after life.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Navayogendra Swami

## Paraṅgacit Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It is such an important event to celebrate you appearance day that just by doing so, we revive in our memory your divine mission on this planet. As a perfect *pārivrājakācārya,* your only attachments are Krishna and His devotional service in the association of other loving and eternal *bhaktas* of the Lord. As an ideal *ācārya* of the *saṅkīrtana* movement you showed us our total dependence on book distribution. In order to follow your footsteps and understand this simple formula (because you never made anything sophisticated), one has to be pure and humble. Humbleness means to follow without changing the codes given by the Supreme Lord. As you explain in your purports for the *Upadeśāmṛta,* “One has to take up Krishna consciousness very carefully.” This carefulness means not changing your orders under any condition. And in case one does commit such an offense, the material energy starts acting, and then gradually one loses his attachment for Krishna, as the moon loses its size in the waning period. But the bright rays of the full moon appear in the heart of the devotee as love of Godhead, just by following your perfect instructions.

As your eternal servant, I pray to Lord Caitanya to have purity and to go on executing your only order of distributing your transcendental books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda Ki Jaya!  
Transcendental book distribution Ki Jaya!

Paraṅgacit Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In your *Bhagavad-gītā* purport to the fifty-seventh verse of the Eighteenth Chapter you explain that the word *mat-paraḥ* in the verse is very important. These words you say indicate that a pure devotee has no goal in life save and except acting in Krishna consciousness just to satisfy Krishna. And while working in Krishna consciousness one should think of Krishna only: “I have been appointed to discharge this particular duty by Krishna.” This is the method you prescribe for one to naturally think of Krishna and thus make one’s life perfect in Krishna consciousness.

However, to understand Lord Krishna’s desires one must follow the teachings and example of elevated devotees, who have understood Krishna by their proper deliberation and practical activities. Fortunately, by Krishna’s grace we have the association of Your Divine Grace, who has given us Krishna consciousness through your instructions and pure spiritual example. You are still present with us through your ISKCON institution and your instructions. Out of ecstatic love for Krishna and your spiritual master, not only have you distributed Krishna consciousness further around the world and in more diverse ways than anyone in history, but also through your instruction you continue to inspire your followers to maintain and expand the mission that you yourself established. Because you only see Krishna in everyone, and because everyone is very dear to you, you are always trying to distribute generously the most valuable possession you have – pure love for Krishna.

Because you are the most addicted to Krishna, your books are continuously revealing Krishna and helping to expand His glories around the world. Anyone who reads your books regularly and seriously will certainly gradually intensify his awareness of Krishna and thus awaken his natural dormant love for Krishna. You are happy to see others happy in Krishna consciousness, and you appreciate with deep devotional love even insignificant service rendered to please you, and your feelings of appreciation and love help others to advance in Krishna consciousness.

Ultimately to satisfy you we must become pure and continue on with your cultural revolution to spread Krishna consciousness around the world. This revolution is a change in the consciousness of the people by convincing them that Krishna is the Supreme Personality of Godhead and that if they follow His instructions they will revive their dormant loving relationship with Him and be happy. Therefore, by distributing your books, by art, by music, by theater, by dioramas, by any and every means, we must try to convince people that Krishna is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. And if they think that Krishna is mythological, we can introduce Lord Caitanya, who appeared only five hundred years ago. And if Lord Caitanya is still too distant, surely any intelligent person will appreciate your glories, if they are properly presented, and therefore agree to follow your simple instructions to make progress in Krishna consciousness. We pray for your mercy that we can serve as your instruments in this campaign to convince everyone, all Vaiṣṇavas, all Hindus, all religious groups, to unite together and distribute Krishna consciousness all over the world. This is the real meaning of *mat-paraḥ*; this is the real meaning of being your sincere disciple.

*Begging to be your eternal servant,*  
Prabhlādananda Swami

## Raghunātha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Krishna, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet.

With your permission I would like to take this opportunity to say, “Thank you for keeping me in your service.” Although I have no qualification and am always an offensive burden to the devotees, somehow or other you have allowed me to remain. Of course, this is not so amazing considering the fact that you are fully possessed of all godly qualities, one of which is compassion.

I remember arriving in Vṛndāvana in November of 1979 and feeling most fortunate to be there. You taught me many important lessons in the holy *dhāma*, and it wasn’t until the end of my stay that I finally realized that I, too, was a *gurukula* student. Although you arranged for me to assume the role of teacher, a position for which I had no qualification, I got the distinct impression that I was somehow a student. Although I learned quite a lot from the boys themselves, I see this as a manifestation of your mercy. You instructed me through my feeble attempts at instructing them. You know exactly what I need to progress in Krishna consciousness, so now you’ve brought me to the West. When I arrived, I began to realize what an expert manager you really are. It’s exactly as Satsvarūpa Maharāja has written, “He was still in charge.”

Although I miss Vṛndāvana, there is one lesson I’ve learned quite well; that is, how to tolerate the intolerable. Having to leave the *dhāma* after seven years was just that – intolerable. Nonetheless, I will humbly try to follow in your footsteps and preach the glories of Vṛndāvana wherever I’m sent. By your mercy I have some attachment for the holy *dhāma,* and I consider this the perfection of my life. Thank you.

*Begging to serve you eternally, I remain your useless servant,*  
Raghunātha Swami

## Romapada Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The mission is the same. The daily worship is the same. The lessons in the scripture remain the same. Yet something has become different.

“If we keep to the *vāṇī,* the words of the spiritual master, then we remain very fixed up. It doesn’t matter. Just like *Bhagavad-gītā,* it was spoken five thousand years ago, but if you keep to the words of Krishna, then it is always fresh and guiding. Not that because, Arjuna personally listened to Krishna about the instruction of *Bhagavad-gītā* therefore he knew it. That is not the fact. If you accept *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is, then you should know that Krishna is present before you in His words, in the *Bhagavad-gṭā.* This is called spiritual realization. It is not a mundane historical incident. If we keep *evaṁ paramparā-prāptam imaṁ rājarṣayo viduḥ / sa kāleneha mahatā togo naṣṭaḥ parantapa* – if you don’t keep in touch with the original link then it will be lost. And if you keep in touch with the original link, then you are directly hearing Krishna. Similarly Krishna and Krishna’s representative, the spiritual master – if you keep always in touch, in link with the words and instructions of the superior authorities, then you are always fresh. This is spiritual understanding.” (Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Vyāsā-pūjā address by Śrīla Prabhupāda, 2 / 28 / 75)

You order is our life and soul. But our actions are so imperfect.

As we try to do exactly as you say, we find that we have to go back, to listen again and again to your instructions, ever so carefully, just as if we never heard them before, and straining to capture every little drop of nectar of your words. How carefully we try to hear, how intently we listen with no other thought, is how much we are permitted to actually love you.

Your order is our life and soul.

You have taught us to immerse ourselves in Krishna thought. Yet you have just explained that if we keep in touch with your words, then we are directly hearing Krishna.

Your pleasure is more than passive thought.

You have taught us that if we can actually understand who is Krishna, we will automatically serve Him. Included surely is acknowledgment of our own tinyness. We are part and parcel of the supreme great. And you are His dearmost servant. We are tiny before you. We must therefore serve you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.
