You have taught us what is the goal of life. And then you have given it to us, freely. Yet stubbornly we remain fools before you.

“Read my books daily. Hear *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* recitation daily.” Your movement is being carried by those who obediently carry out such simple points of strict *sādhana*.

Please do not abandon us in this hour of *your* ISKCON’s faltering. Keep up the command to become thoroughly pure, to represent your good name with its deserved praise and dignity. Loving sons and daughters can strive for no less.

Your order is our life and soul.

Romapada Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please allow me on your Vyāsa-pūjā day to express my eternal gratitude unto you. I am ever indebted to you because you have convinced me that Krishna is God. You have convinced thousands of this fact, and you have opened and developed the field for the worldwide golden age of Lord Caitanya.

You have convinced me that Krishna is the Supreme Person, mostly because of your own personal presence. By sharing your association with all of us, you have acted as an empowered incarnation, creating faith in the faithless wherever you went.

You have also convinced us with your personal presentation of the authentic Vedic literature such as *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam*. Through your transparent commentaries on these books you have shown us that the *Bhāgavatam* contains the supreme science of God. Although at first we approach the subject matter only theoretically, gradually we become convinced that the Absolute Truth is a Supreme Person, and then we become convinced that the Supreme Person is Krishna Himself, and devotional service to Him is the goal of life.

You have taught us Krishna consciousness in a nonsectarian way so that we can appreciate symptoms of genuine love of God wherever it is manifest, even in other religions. But you have also created the Krishna consciousness society, where we can find the rare association of the Vaiṣṇavas. Only among the servants of the pure Vaiṣṇavas can we share your ecstatic conviction that Krishna is the summum bonum. This conclusion has been stated in the *Mahābhārata* as follows:

*ye ca veda-vido viprā*  
*ye cādhyātma-vido janāḥ*  
*te vedanti mahātmānaṁ*  
*kṛṣṇaṁ dharmaṁ sanātaman*  
*(quoted in Śrīmad-Bhagavatam,*  
*4.24.42, purport)*

Your purport to this verse states that one who has studied the *Vedas* perfectly, and who knows what spiritual life actually is, knows that *Krishna is the essence of sanātana-dharma.*

I pray that you will maintain and strengthen my convictions and allow me to always serve your followers in the Krishna consciousness movement. I pray to gain the realization as expressed by King Kulaśekhara: “My only desire is to be fixed in devotional service to the Lord, even though I may continue to take birth here life after life.”

**Prabhupāda Mūrti**

Affection for you,  
Prabhupāda mūrti,  
rose up recently,  
when I was ill;  
maybe I was afraid,  
I can’t remember.  
Was I feeling all alone?

To pick you up,  
to place you in bed,  
to wake you,  
to bathe your tan effulgent form,  
all these acts became dear to me again:  
as you accompany me  
through the last days of my life.

But I have to act on your behalf.  
I need more  
than a dream,  
or a quote in my ear.  
I need the gumption  
to surrender to your movement,  
I need faith as strong as śāstric injunction  
to know that what I am doing is right.  
Please grant me conviction.

*Your servant,*  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Krishna Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In front of you I am fool,  
but you had a vision,  
made me into a tool  
in this saṅkīrtana-mission.

Saṅkīrtana from the morning to the evening  
we practiced every day;  
already in the beginning  
by your mercy I am still on the way.

A decade and a half,  
so much in the past,  
I have practiced this path,  
by your mercy I have held fast.

Nothing is impossible;  
let there be much more in the future.  
there was a change in my heart,  
all glories unto you, our divine tutor.

A foolish disciple sure of success,  
knowing his master to be perfect,  
protected by your mercy  
like the child by the father.

However insignificant the offering may be,  
you will accept the sincerity  
with which it is offered to thee;  
that is the solace for a person like me.

Will there ever in the future  
be such opportunity again  
to listen to your lecture  
and relish your gems?

Opening your books,  
reading them carefully,  
we will get off our hooks  
and dance in ecstasy.

By your mercy a blind man can see,  
a lame man can walk,  
and a fool like me  
tries to write some poetry.

Please help me to always  
in your service endeavor,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, O master,  
my hope forever.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Smita Krishna Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet. Please hear my prayer. Somehow or other force me to become qualified to assist you in your great missionary movement.

I am a devout materialist with an entrenched egocentric mentality. I seem to lack the ability and determination to successfully present this Krishna consciousness movement or to properly represent you. These points are not false humility or sentimental emotion, but are easily verifiable. What is even more troublesome, however, than my abject failure as a preacher is the foolish idea that someday I may become qualified. Utter hopelessness is not as pitiable as is hope against hope. But for some reason I can’t shake off this hope that is troubling me so much. I am sorry, really sorry, and in this mood I am turning to you for shelter. You can easily pick me up if you desire. Please do think of me sometimes.

*Your wretched servant,*  
Trivikrama Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Adelaide

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalakayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda said that our ability to cooperate amongst ourselves after his departure would prove our love for him and would in fact prove our sincerity to become Krishna conscious. Allegiance to the order of the spiritual master ensures that we progress nicely on the path back home, back to Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted that we preserve intact the international society that he created, the society that he created on the order of his spiritual master – a society created for “the upliftment of all human society.”

What this means is that even though we may have difference, for the betterment of the movement they have to be resolved and put aside in deference to the greater principle – ISKCON. If we truly love Śrīla Prabhupāda, if we are truly serious about Krishna consciousness, then we have to act sensibly to resolve all problems.

Of course, the difficulties faced by our movement have not been easy to overcome. But if we have faith in Śrīla Prabhupāda and stay loyal to his orders, then all problems can be overcome in due time.

Śrīla Prabhupāda had prefect vision, and the movement that he created is also perfect.

*The imperfections that we appear to see in ISKCON are not ISKCON’s fault; they are our own impurities.*

Whilst we remain loyal in following the regulative principles of Krishna consciousness and distributing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, then there will be no threat to our movement either from within or without that we cannot deal with.

On the auspicious occasion of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā, let us once again renew our vows and renew our determination to cooperate to establish the “International Society for Krishna Consciousness,” which is essential for our own spiritual health and the spiritual welfare of society.

*Your humble servants at ISKCON Adelaide, Australia,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Kṛpākāra dāsa / Ādi-Puruṣa dāsa / Jaleyu-devī-dāsī

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Pañcaratma dāsa / Sarvadāmana dāsa / Mahābuddhi dāsa / Dhaumya dāsa / Sudarśana dāsa / Bhṛgu dāsa / Bhīma dāsa / Ṛṣi-kumāra dāsa / Rāmānuja dāsa / Guru-kṛpañ dāsa / Kiñkiṇīk dāsa / Bhakta Graham / Satyabhāmā-devī dāsī / Subhujī-devī dāsī / Jīvānanda-devī dāsī / Śyāmāsakhī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Julie / Bhaktin Samoa / Bhaktin Audrey / Bhaktin Rhonda

**Children**

Kaustubha Līlāmṛta / Nṛsiṁhānanda / Kadamba / Kṛpī / Ādi-līlā / Tilakinī / Jānakī

## Ahmedabad

*Evaṁ paramparā-prāptam*  
*Imaṁ rājarṣayo viduḥ*

**“This supreme science was thus received through the chain of disciplic succession, and the saintly kings understood it in that way.”** ***(Bhagavad-gītā*** **4.2)**

Krishna – In the dawn of creation the first-born *jīva, veda garbha* Lord Brahmā, was engulfed by void and darkness, vainly ascending and descending the abdominal lotus stem, searching for his very purpose of being. Then three times Śrī Ādi-guru gave His disciple the order *tapaḥ tapaḥ tapaḥ.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda – In the dawn of Kali-yuga, 482 Caitanya Era, billions of bewildered *jīvas* were engulfed in the darkness of the age, vainly searching for their true identities, through drugs, pseudo spiritualism, and other anomalies. Then to thousands strong Ṣrī Guru gave the order *tapaḥ tapaḥ tapaḥ,* and his disciples heard; chant sixteen rounds, no meat-eating, no illicit sex, no gambling, no intoxication.

Lord Devarṣi Nārada sat at the feet of Prajāpati, Dharmapati, Lord Brahmā, his spiritual preceptor, and by his mannerly behavior, meekness, and sense control Nārada pleased his father and received directly from Goloka Dhāma the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam.* Nārada Muni in turn brought the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* to this world and gave it to his devoted *śiṣya,* Vyāsadeva.

Śrīla Prabhupāda received this mercy by sitting at the feet of *nṅsiṁha-guru,* His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Ṭhākura, and by his mannerly behavior (exhibited to the bums on Second Avenue, and to world leaders like Indira Gandhi and Alfred Ford), meekness (when asked if you were ISKCON, you replied, “I am a member of ISKCON”), and sense control (when in New York you were left alone with a wealthy widow who was in the habit of seducing holy men and rewarding them with wealth, you calmly chanted Hare Krishna on your beads and later remarked, “Then they knew I was not like the rest of the *sādhus.*”), you exemplified all the qualities of a true *sādhu* and received the instructions “If you ever get money, print books.” You have passed this instruction down to your devoted disciples: “Print as many books in as many languages as there are around the world, and distribute them widely.”

Śrīla Krishna Dvaipāyana Vyāsa appeared when the second millennium overlapped the third, as the son of Parāśara and Satyavatī. On the sacred bank of the Sarasvatī River at Sāmyaparāśa Baḍarikāśrama, the literary incarnation of Lord Nārāyaṅa then divided the v*edas* into four (Atharva Veda, Ṛg Veda, Yajur Veda, and Sāma Veda), wrote the *Vedānta-sūtras, Purāṅas,* and *Mahābhārata,* assisted by the Tumuli of Gandi Rāja Ganesh and, still feeling despondent, he approached his eternal *guru*, Śrī Nārada, and was instructed:

*tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*yasmin prati-ślokam abaddhavaty api*  
*nāmāny anantasya yaśo ṅkitāni yat*  
*śṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ*

**“On the other hand, that literature which is full of descriptions of the transcendental glories of the name, fame, forms, pastimes, etc. of the unlimited Supreme Lord is a different creation, full of transcendental words directed towards bringing about a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization. Such transcendental literatures, though imperfectly composed, are heard, sung, and accepted by purified men who are thoroughly honest.”**

Upon receiving this instruction, he wrote *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* for the benefit of all in this age.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you appeared in the millennium of Vaivasvata Manu as the son of Gaura Mohan De and Rajani on the first of September, 1896, on the banks of the mighty Bhagirathi, blessed by mother Sarasvati to deliver *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* to the English-speaking world. Whether in Vraja *dhāma* on the bank of the holiest of all rivers, Yamunā; sitting in your tiny room at Keśi-ghāta; or on the celestial Ganges flowing gently through Gauḍa-deśa upon the crest jewel of Navadvīpa, Śrī Māyāpur *dhāma;* or on the bank of the Hudson River in *mleccha-deśa* New York City; whether by pen, Dictaphone, or by the typewriter – Śrīla Prabhupāda, you fearlessly preserved the tradition of the Brahmā Mahdva Gauḍīya *sampradāya* by translating into English the *Upanisads,* the spotless *Purāṇas,* with elaborate Bhaktivedanta purports and perfectly presented the *param tattva,* Śrī Krishna, for the benefit of all souls of this fallen age.

Madhavācārya performed Pada-yātrā from Kanyā-kumārī to Baḍarīnātha, expounding the doctrine of Viśiṣṭādvaita, defeating *nirveśeṣa* and *śūnyavādī* philosophies.

Śrīla Prabhupāda inspired, by his order, his disciples to perform Pada-yātrā from Dvārakā to Kanyā-kumārī to Baḍarīnātha, while he himself fulfilled perfectly the prayers uttered by thousands of disciples daily at least three times: *names te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe / nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe.*

Mādhavendra Puri revealed to our *sampradāya* the highest mellow of devotional perfection, *mādhurya-rasa,* and taught us the worship of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī along with the *arcā-vigraha* of Śrī Krishna and imbibed the same *prema* in his beloved disciple Īśvara Purī. Śrīla Prabhupāda is glorified in every corner of the globe daily in the auspicious *brāhma-muhūrta* with the melodious select stanza sung by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura in his *Guruvaṣṭakam: śrī-rādhikā-mādhavayor apāra-mādhurya-līlā-gūṇa-rūpa-nāmnām / prati-kṣaṇāsvādana-lolupasya vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda has gifted his disciples with the greatest fortune of the opportunity to assist Rādha-Mādhava in their *aṣṭaka līlā* in the groves of Vṛndāvana, regardless of their qualifications by birth as *śūdras, mlecchas,* or *yāvanas.*

Īśvara Purī was the embodiment of humility as he, with his own hands, cleansed the stool of his divine *guru* and as he instructed his *ṣiṣya*, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, *nācā gāo bhakta-saṅge kara saṅkīrtana / kr nāma apadeśi’ tāra’ sarva-jana:* “My dear child, continue dancing, chanting, and performing *saṅkīrtana* in association with devotees. Furthermore, go out and preach the value of chanting Krishna *nāma,* for by this process you will be able to deliver all fallen souls.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda personally, by his own hand, taught us the brahminical principles, replaced our toilet paper with a shiny copper lota, even showed us how to wash a floor, and gave us the sublime *Upadeśāmṛta* to chant “Hare Krishna” and be happy by chanting, dancing, and feasting.

Śrī Krishna Caitanya Mahāprabhu orders, *pṛthivīte āche jate nagarādi grāma / sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma:* “In all the towns and villages all over the world, everywhere, this *saṅkīrtana* movement will be preached.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as member of Lord Gaurāṅga’s own spiritual family, has taken this order on his head and personally chanted the sounds of Nitāi-Gaurāṅga on every continent of the planet, from the city of New York to the streets of Bombay, in the Avalon ballroom, Moscow, Tokyo, and remote villages of Africa to one or millions.

Rūpa Gosvāmī and Sanātana Gosvāmī, on the order of Gauracandra Śacīsuta, lived under a different tree nightly while establishing magnificent temples to glorify Rādhā and Krishna and revealed Vṛndāvana to humanity through the writings of innumerable *śāstras* with the aim of establishing eternal religious principles for the benefit of all fallen conditioned souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Gosvāmī, established and lived in different palaces, adorned with the merciful shelter of Rādhā and Krishna *vigrahas* every night, revealed Vṛndāvana to the blinded vision of the bewildered souls of Kali-yuga, and wrote and translated innumerable Vedic literature into Sanskrit, Bengali, and English, with the aim of establishing eternal devotional service to Krishna in order to enable a misdirected society to have direction for at least ten thousand years to come. Bhaktivedanta Book Trust ki jaya!

Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has sung such selected Vaiṣṇava poetry as: “Vaiṣṇave Vijñāpati Saparśada,” “Bhagavad-Vīrṇa Janita-Vilapa,” “Śrī Guru Vandana,” “Manaḥ-Śikṣā,” and “Lalasā-mayī Prārthanā” for the enlightenment of all the conditioned souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda has sung such selected *vaiṣṇava* poetry as “Prayers to the Lotus Feet of Krishna,” “Markine Bhāgavata Dharma,” and “Adore Ye All” for the enlightenment of all the conditioned souls.

Śrīla Kedaranātha Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, the pioneer to give Krishna consciousness to the Western world, envisioned a beautiful temple near the confluence of the Sarasvatī and Ganges from which tumultuous *kīrtanas* of “Jaya Śacīnandana, Gaura Hari” would resound from the lotus mouths of thousands of Vaiṣṇavas from all the continents on the planet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda sailed in the *Jaladuta* on August 13, 1965, bound for Commonwealth Pier, Boston, as an emissary to the order of Bhaktivinoda, the father of his divine master, Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. Then, in March 1970, he laid the cornerstone for the envisioned prophecy of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura at Śrī Caitanya Candrodaya Mandira and yearly danced in ecstasy with literally thousands of Vaiṣṇavas from all continents on the planet to the chanting of “Jaya Śacīnandana, Gaura Hari!”

Jagannātha dāsa Bābajī, although lame and bent over with more than one century and one score of years, revealed the lost and long-disputed site of the birth of Nimāi Paṇdita, the beloved son of Jagannātha Miśra, to the Vaiṣṇava world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, despite heart attacks, accidents, and many difficulties, at an advanced age of 69, revealed to the world Śrī Māyapur Dhāma and ordered the building of a spiritual city in which the whole world could live and worship Gaura Krishna, the beloved husband of Viṣṇupriyā, at Māyāpura.

Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvātī Ṭhākura applied the principles of *yukta-vairāgya* perfectly with motorcars, big buildings, three-piece suits, borrowed *tri-daṇḍīs,* and saffron colors for *brahmacārīs,* according to time and place, and said, “If I have offended them, let me suffer, but let the suffering people get Krishna.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the perfect master because he was the perfect disciple, continued the principles of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, flying in airplanes, driving in motorcars, constructing huge buildings, floating in boats, using printing presses, and handling billions of dollars without extracting even a farthing for his own personal sense pleasure, bestowing the matchless gift of love of Krishna to whomsoever he touched, without a taint of offense.

The self-effulgent perfect master is the perfect disciple of the *guru-paramparā.* “On my order become *guru*.” We, the unworthy disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda and granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda, have accepted you as our eternal *guru* and humbly pray: *gurudeva! Kṛpā-bindu diyā, karo’ ei dāse, tṛṇāpekhā ati hīna, sakala sahane, bala diyā koro’ nija-māne spṛhā-hīna.* Allow us to serve you in the beating of the *bṛhat mṛdaṅga* life after life.

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Alachua

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You have fully received the blessings of your spiritual master. Bless us so that we may be similarly receptive to your mercy. Your pleasure is the advancement of the Krishna consciousness movement for the salvation of the conditioned souls. Please remove our haunting desires for sensual pleasure. You actually understand that the Deities and *prasādam* are Krishna appearing in His various forms. Forgive our continuous offenses to the Deities and Their *prasādam,* and let us hear your correcting chasetisement. The exalted loving affairs of Śrī Rādhā and Śrī Hari and Their associates are known to you. You are assisting Them in Their pastimes. While we struggle to become purified, may we not take these pastimes cheaply. May the glorification of you as the empowered representative of Krishna be always progressive as we realize more and more what a complete sacrifice you made to deliver the most rare gift. Remembering you three times a day is not enough in this world of make believe, but please grant us at least that much remembrance of you. Falling in the dust of your lotus feet, we beg to remain your servants.

(Written by Vāṇīnātha Vasu dāsa)

Your servants at New Ramaṇa-retī, Alachua ISKCON

## Almviks Gard

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

At the time of the Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony, those who consider themselves the beneficiaries of the instructions and care of the spiritual master, and especially the disciples of the spiritual master, should offer prayers in praise of the *guru* and his unique and uncommon qualities and accomplishments.

One of your accomplishments was the establishment of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, ISKCON – “the house in which the whole world could live.” You yourself have described your unique position in relationship to ISKCON as its founder-*ācārya,* a post which no one else could ever occupy, and a title which was to appear forever on ISKCON’s buildings, letterheads, documents, etc.

In a letter to Janārdana dāsa in 1968, you described how there are two categories of *gurus:* those who are eternal associates of the Lord, and those who are not eternally liberated, but are themselves conditioned souls, rising to the platform of pure devotional service. I think no informed Vaiṣṇava would question to which category you belong. But why do we find eternal associates of the Lord like yourself here within the material world? In your comments on the story of Sakṣi-gopāla in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you explain: “By the order of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the *nitya-siddha* remains within this material world like an ordinary man, but the only business of the *nitya-siddha* is to broadcast the glories of the Lord.” *(Madhya-līlā* 5.114)

So you come with a mission to fulfill: “Whenever an *ācārya* comes, following the superior orders of the Supreme Personality of Godhead or His representative, he establishes the principles of religion, as enunciated in *Bhagavad-gītā*.” (*Bhāg.* 4.28.48, purport) You were kind enough not only to save us, but to engage us also in that same mission. But now you have gone on to associate with Krishna in His spiritual realm, or to serve Him by preaching in another place, and we are experiencing how hard it is to keep together the house you built without your personal presence to keep us on the right track. But you have also given the solution to the problem:

“…the disciple and spiritual master are never separated, because the spiritual master always keeps company with the disciple as long as the disciple follows strictly the instructions of the spiritual master. This is called the association of *vāṇī* (words). Physical presence is called *vapuḥ.* As long as the spiritual master is physically present, the disciple should serve the physical body of the spiritual master, and when the spiritual master is no longer physically existing, the disciple should serve the instructions of the spiritual master.” (*Bhāg.* 4.28.47, purport)

So what are the instructions that we must fulfill to succeed in keeping your house in order? We should not be surprised to find a difficult task, as you yourself pointed out that when the *ācārya* disappears, things become more chaotic and disordered. But we can prove our love in this difficult tak by taking up your clear challenge to our tendency to act independently: “Your love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate to keep this institution together after I am gone.”

You also called ISKCON your body. So we have been left with a unique spiritual legacy: we can serve your *vapuḥ* even after your disappearance – by serving your *vāṇī* to cooperate.

How do we cooperate to keep the institution together, even in the absence of its *ācārya* and founder? It would be easy to say, “We just have to preach, and that will keep Prabhupāda in the center.” But it is not so simple, because we are fallible human beings with many different ideas on how to follow your orders and continue your mission. Therefore our endeavor to work together must be coupled with extraordinary efforts to cooperate by (1) carefully following your orders on managing the society, (2) being willing to work within accepted limits of Vaiṣṇava behavior as defined by you, the scriptures, and the *sādhus* of this movement, and (3) making sure that we develop trust in each other as *vaiṣṇavas* by executing the six kinds of loving exchanges outlined by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī in the *Upadeśāmṛta.*

Therefore, if we want to please you by keeping ISKCON intact, no effort must be spared to maintain trust in each other by following your orders in regard to ISKCON. If, with those orders in mind, we join together and seriously get down to the business of preaching, then we will achieve cooperation and bring pleasure to you through our actions.

“…As the Supreme Personality of Godhead comes down upon this earth to reestablish the principles of religion, so His representative, the spiritual master, also comes to reestablish religious principles. It is the duty of the disciples to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly. Otherwise, the disciple should decide to die along with the spiritual master. In other words, to execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations.” (*Bhāg.* 4.28.51)

We therefore pray to you to please help us with the intelligence and strength of will to lay down our lives – in the way that is most pleasing to you – and keep working together to spread this movement of yours to every corner of the planet.

Your servants,

## Amsterdam

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

All glories to your divine lotus feet, which can give shelter to all living entities. Thank you for coming to rescue us. Thank you for giving us the holy names. Thank you for translating Lord Krishna’s message and for giving your pure, everfresh Bhaktivedanta purports. Thank you for showing all of us how to practice Krishna consciousness twenty-four hours a day. Thank you for your Sunday Love Feasts. Thank you for the association of your sincere disciples. Thank you for allowing us to visit the holy *dhāmas.* Thank you for giving us the taste for spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement by distributing your books. Thank you for giving us Krishna *prasādam.* Thank you for giving us Kṛṣṇadhām. Thank you for inviting Krishna in His Deity form. Thank you for giving us ISKCON.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to protect your helpless grandchildren; your causeless mercy is all we want to obtain in our lives. We pray that your glories will be spread to all the forgetful living entities, so that they will be blessed with your appearance.

*Your servants in Kṛṣṇadhām, Amsterdam,*

## Athens

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this auspicious day of your appearance.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for appearing in this horrible material world and undergoing so many difficulties only so that conditioned souls like ourselves could have the opportunity to taste the bliss of Krishna consciousness and go back home, back to Godhead. Even though we deserve to stay and rot here for millions of lifetimes, and even though under illusion we think this world is quite a nice place, and we are quite attached to staying here, still you came to drag us back home to Krishna.

Once a reporter asked you how many people you thought could become Krishna conscious, and you replied that “I can tell you, but if you refuse, what can I do?” You have given us the perfect solution to all problems, and you have mercifully given us the solution in such a simple way, which is easily accessible to us, and still we are obstinately holding on to our material desires and attachments and refusing to surrender.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that you will please allow us to understand the meaning of your appearance and of your mission to save the fallen souls so that we can give up sense gratification and material attachments and engage in your mission.

Praying always to remain serving at your lotus feet.

*Your servants in Greece,*

Vijñāna dāsa / Dvārakānātha dāsa / Bhakta Yorgos / Prema-bhakti-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Angela

## Atlanta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. You are constantly guiding ISKCON through its progression. According to your disciples’ and granddisciples’ surrender and sincerity, their freedom form material, selfish motives, and their purity, you enlighten them and infuse them with the potency to preach Krishna consciousness to the devotees and to the common people.

We can see that you are monitoring the activities of the Krishna consciousness movement, nurturing it along its way. Therefore ISKCON will be able to accomplish the goal for which it was established – to steer human society back to the kingdom of God.

We trust you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We trust that you will take full care of us, that you will not permit us to fall away as long as we adhere to your will and instructions. We know that you are always available and that it is because of you that we are able to be Krishna conscious. As long as we strive wholeheartedly to please you, to push on the mission you started, carrying on the order you received from your spiritual master, we know you will bless us with knowledge, realization, intelligence, expertise, favorable results, and love of Krishna.

One cannot give love of Krishna, or knowledge of Him, unless one has the same. You are giving love and knowledge of Krishna because you are infused with the same. Your love for Krishna and the world will save the world and deliver them to Krishna.

Like the six Gosvāmīs, you are appreciated by everyone because of your purity – *dhīrādhīra-jana-priyau priyakarau nirmatsarau pūjitau.*

If we become attached to you, being attached to pleasing you, which is pleasing to Krishna, then we will attain all success. We will attain you and Krishna, and the whole world will get a chance to go back home, back to Godhead.

*Koṭi-koṭi-daṇdavats:* many millions of thanks for your appearing to us to save us from being lost forever. We humbly beg you, as you unceasingly shower your mercy upon us, to help us stay above the deadly, stormy waters of the unfathomable ocean of *māyā*. By steadfastly clinging on to your mission, we hope to be dedicated with a pure heart to propagate this knowledge of Krishna consciousness, so essential for the salvation of mankind.

Your fallen servants of New Ṣrī Pānihāṭi Dhāma

## Auckland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances on this most blessed event of your appearance day.

Last year I wrote to you explaining my meager realizations about the importance of a disciple to maintain his remembrance of how totally dependent he is on the *guru’s* mercy.

One year later, I again realize the great benediction of the wonderful day of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā. It is today that the Supreme Lord, Śrī Krishna, blesses us with the great opportunity to glorify you, His pure, unalloyed devotee, and thus again make a little progress in spiritual life.

If not for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the transcendental message of Śrī Vyāsa would have remained “the most secret of all secrets.” If not for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would still be left to suffer the full reactions of our foolish material desires. You have given us everything Śrīla Prabhupāda, and in time this truth will be revealed to everyone.

Before your departure to the Lord’s supreme abode, you gave us one of your final instructions. “Your love for me will be demonstrated by how well you cooperate together when I have gone.”

Recently I have been trying to deepen my understanding of what you meant by *cooperation.* Fortunately you have already explained to us the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which gives us the formula for successful cooperation.

In His *Śrī Śikṣāṣṭaka,* Lord Caitanya prays, “One should chant the holy name of the Lord in a humble state of mind, thinking oneself lower than the straw in the street. One should be more tolerant than a tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige and always ready to offer all respects to others. In such a state of mind one can chant the holy name of the Lord constantly.”

When we imbibe the humble mood of the Vaiṣṇava, then we are well on our way to cooperation. When we are trying to maintain a humble state of mind, when we think of ourselves as lower than the straw in the street, when we always remain tolerant in spite of all difficulties and provocations, when we become freed from false egotism and we are always ready to offer all respects to others – only then can we truly say we are following your orders and showing our love for you.

On this wonderful day of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, we all pray for just one drop of your causeless mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This will surely enable us to cooperate together as one united family under your expert guidance.

(Written by Jaya Śīla dāsa)

*Praying at your lotus feet, always desiring your mercy, we remain your insignificant servants,*

## Baltimore

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

At different times I have different recollections of you.

Sometimes I see you the way I saw you at JFK in ’75, radiant, a halo of soft, bright light emanating from your pure heart, bathing your surroundings. Anyone who enters that halo can feel immediate relief from the pangs of material life – hunger, thirst, lamentation, illusion, greed, anger, lust. What to speak of relief, one can even feel spiritual ecstasy. Whenever I recall this I always remember the song about Lord Nityānanda’s lotus feet being “more soothing than millions of moons.”

Sometimes I see you sitting within my heart, the luminous custodians of an inexhaustible storehouse of kindness and grace, and every time my enthusiasm flags, just when I think, “I’m finished, I can’t go on. I shall never get over my shortcomings and erring ways,” you give me some mercy from your reserves, urging me to push on – just as you inspired me in Calcutta when you told Malatī, regarding Śyāmasundara, “Tell him to forget everything and just come back,” with inexpressible humility in your voice, a tear in your eye, and complete forgiveness in your heart. I remember thinking, “How did I come upon such a worthy and wonderful master?”

Sometimes I see you as Dhruva Mahārāja withstanding the onslaught of the *yakṣas* in the painting on the back cover of the *Bhāgavatam.* Who but someone equal in faith to Dhruva could come to the West, the abyss of ignorance in this age, and bring the light of Krishna consciousness, dispelling the dense darkness with the vibration of the holy name? If Lord Caitanya gave awards to His preachers for their bravery in the face of incredible odds, then you would have won them all.

Sometimes I see you as an errant knight, Krishna’s champion, daring bold, roaming the four corners of the globe, waging war on Māyā-devī, urging the conditioned souls to join Krishna’s forces. When someone said you were always in the mood of Prahlāda Mahārāja, you said, “Yes, I’m always thinking, “How to save fools and rascals?” You can’t rest until the oppressed souls have all revolted against sense gratification and lost their minds in the *kīrtana* of the holy name. Seeing this gives you great satisfaction.

Sometimes I see you as a meek and humble swanlike wandering mendicant, acting as Krishna’s instrument for teaching bewildered souls how to distinguish reality from illusion, giving your mercy freely to whoever lends a receptive ear to your message, which is none other than Krishna’s message. With unfathomable kindness you coax us out of our causeless unwillingness to serve the almighty Personality of Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my only regret about being your disciple is that despite my conviction of your status as a *śaktyāveśa* incarnation of God and as an intimate associate of Lord Gaurasundara, I am not blessed with sufficient ability or realization to better glorify you; and, because of feeblemindedness, I am not steadfast in your service. Instead I plod along, distracted by the glitter of the material energy, like a bewildered child in a toy shop. I can understand this failing is due to my pride.

On my own, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I find I’m unable to overcome this enemy of self-realization. And so I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you make your mission of mercy successful by teaching me humility – at whatever the price. Without my first becoming genuinely humble I want no big position, no big title, no grand facility in your mission, for it will only cause me anguish. Besides, should I become your humble servant at heart, as I’m supposed to be, these things would not be necessary anyway. In other words, Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly bless me with the power of recollection that I’m nothing but a servant of your servants, at all times, in all places, and in all circumstances. This one realization I would very much like to have.

**A Prayer of Longing**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will that day be mine when, out of an unflinching desire to serve you, I shall proceed straight on the path of liberation?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will that day be mine when I, unshakable in my conviction of Krishna consciousness, will set aside all my fears and rationalizations and preach Krishna consciousness as it is, without reservations, without compromise, just as you did for your spiritual master, inside as well as outside of ISKCON?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will that day be mine when I dutifully serve you day after day, hour after hour, minute after minute, moment after moment, both within and without – with no desire for anything in return: no profit, no recognition, no adoration, no distinction – for having done my basic duty to you?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will that day be mine when I shall be victorious over my enemies – pride, anger, greed, lust, and envy – and become indifferent to the sense objects, especially the opposite sex?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will that day be mine when I shall feel close fellowship with and love for *vaiṣṇavas* everywhere? When shall I feel joy in my heart to see them, greet them, and serve them, and not be concerned whether they’ll recognize me, appreciate me, or serve me?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will that day be mine when I shall give up my mentality that to serve you I must have a glorious service, a so-called big service, so everyone will see me as important, heroic, a “big devotee”? When will I be convinced that the biggest devotee is the smallest devotee?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when shall I give up selfishness and personal ambition and be content to serve you in the most insignificant and menial way, and even in the most harrowing circumstances, fully satisfied to do so indefinitely, just knowing that I’m serving you, the most worthy master?

## Bangaladesh

**It’s catching on, Śrīla Prabhupāda**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Krishna consciousness is catching on in Bangaladesh, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Of course, the Hindu population here has sustained their tremendous enthusiasm and respect for us ever since we first came. After all, the culture of East Bengal Hindus is Caitanya Vaiṣṇavism – worship of Śrī-Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Krishna with *khol, kartalas,* and *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana.*

For eight years our traveling parties have crisscrossed this country, staging mass public festivals. Some of our devotees have become very well known, and your books are in thousands of homes. This past year we’ve been observing some of the effects of this work. The consciousness that, along with chanting Hare Krishna, one should practice *sadācāra* (especially vegetarianism – sad to say, although people worship Gaurāṅga, they still mostly eat fish) is definitely growing. More and more people are coming forward for initiation. Some of the local devotees are taking up leadership roles and establishing preaching programs in different parts of the country. Śrī Puṇḍarīka Dhāma, our first Rādhā-Krishna temple in Bangaladesh, is also the first major public temple established in East Bengal in living memory. It has greatly increased the prestige of your movement here.

Now another project is beginning - Śrī Śrī Rūpa Sanātana Smṛti Tirtha, near the site of the house built by Sanātana Gosvāmi’s father, Kumāradeva, which is mentioned in *Bhakti Ratnākara* and also in one of your *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* purports. The festival programs are continuing, and book distribution is steadily increasing.

Still, there are many difficulties to overcome. There is a long, long way to go. But, by your grace, the prospects are bright. Please give us your blessings to continue this work.

(Written by Ilāpati dāsa)

*Your servants in Bangaladesh,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Jaya Pātaka Swami / Prabhaviṣṇu Swami / Ilāpati dāsa / Kiranāśa dāsa / Nisthula dāsa / Rasika dāsa / Sarvākṣa dāsa and many granddisciples, life members and friends.

## Baroda

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept repeated obeisances at your lotus feet from the most fallen souls that we are.

We have firm faith that you are watching us and guiding us from within. We pray that you are pleased with our humble efforts to spread the ISKCON movement.

We pray that you may bless us with extreme tolerance, so that despite this dark age of Kali, we may always go on with our efforts at spreading Krishna consciousness.

We pray that you may bless us with humility, so that we may not fall into that last trap of *māyā, abaṅkāra.*

We pray for your grace so that we can go on advancing in Krishna consciousness and never be attracted to the sinful activities, which pass as pleasures but lead to the darkest regions.

We thank you for teaching us by your personal example what brahminical qualities actually mean:

*śama* – peacefulness. Despite extensive travels and many taxing management problems, you always had a totally fresh outlook to deal in a friendly way with your disciples and members of the public.

*dama* – self-control. You never indulged in any unnecessary activity, and took only what was required for keeping body and soul together.

t*apaḥ* –austerity. On many occasions you fasted for even days at a time. You so many times accepted what “we may not want to accept,” and thus showed us what is austerity.

*śaucam* – purity. By maintaining the highest standard of physical cleanliness and constant Krishna consciousness, you taught the meaning of purity.

k*ṣānti* – tolerance. You tolerated the most wretched person, and you trained so many from the lowest levels of human society and thus demonstrated what is tolerance.

*ārjavam* – honesty. You endeared yourself to any and all who met you by showing not a trace of duplicity in your dealings.

j*ñānam* – wisdom. You debated with philosophers of all shades, traditional Vedic and atheistic Western, and could defeat them all, thus exhibiting an ocean of wisdom.

v*ijñānam* – knowledge. The depth and variety of your daily lectures on *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* showed us your vast knowledge.

*āstikyam* – religiousness. Your unswaying faith in *guru,* Gaurāṅga, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Krishna, and the constant mood of servitude towards them, demonstrated your unstinted religiousness.

We pray on this most holy Vyāsa-pūjā day that we may always continue to derive inspiration from all of your exalted qualities.

*We beg to remain your eternal servants,*

## Belgium

We offer our humble obeisances at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is the eternal image of the *mahātmā.* He always accepted great challenges in the service of his Lord, Śrī Krishna. Under his leadership all the devotees of ISKCON believed in miracles, that Krishna was no poor man, and that *impossible* was found only in the fool’s dictionary. In his presence we had confidence that everything and anything could be achieved as long as our desire and practice were pure.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is the great *ācārya* who transformed us from self-satisfied, lazy small-timers into big thinkers. And he himself perfectly demonstrated that the symptom which distinguishes the Vaiṣṇava from the Māyāvādī is his recognition of and reliance upon the inconceivable potency of God. We offer our humble obeisances at his lotus feet.

Dear brothers, keeping this all-victorious image of our found-ā*cārya* fresh in mind, let us remain always confident that Śrīla Prabhupāda ’s ISKCON is a transcendental entity conducted by transcendental laws of nature. Let us go on making our “lifetime plans” for vastly increasing the book distribution, for grand-scale temple projects, for worldwide *prasādam* distribution, and unlimited devotee-making. And let us never doubt that this movement will achieve uncommon, unimaginable success, or that in the future it will be credited with having saved human civilization in the darkest hour.

Let us therefore oppose with our lives such forces or philosophies that may arise from time to time threatening to erode Śrīla Prabhupāda’s achievements or to distort or obscure his example and teachings. Above all, let us beware of any preaching philosophy which suggests that “small is beautiful – easy is spiritual.” That seemingly innocent doctrine is actually a viral infection which has deadened whole branches of the Caitanya tree and which can expose the infected parties to the ravaging influence of impersonalism and voidism.

Let us be very wary of any so-called preaching that suggests that risks should not be taken, that big projects or big communities of devotees are only a burden, that book sales cannot be doubled again and again. Dear brothers, let us recognize this as the symptom of the third-class man described by our founder-*ācārya*. Such a person sometimes dreams of doing something ambitious, but does not dare to make an actual beginning because of the problems that he imagines he will encounter. He therefore feels justified in his inertia and goes on only dreaming of great achievements. May Lord Caitanya protect us from such a mentality. If we followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda ever allow ourselves to become lazy day-dreamers and do-nothings, it will mean our spiritual disenfranchisement, disinheritance. *Yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi* – what would become of us then?

Let us therefore make great plans. Circumstantially, we may have to begin a project in a small way, a helpless way. But let us never be content for an ISKCON project to remain small or ineffectual. Let us never be satisfied with merely creating unambitious little centers, whose main goal is to avoid “impractical” or expensive aspirations. Let us rather wrack our brains how to build, how to attract, how to develop, how to achieve the colossal goals which Śrīla Prabhupāda set for us. Let us take on gladly such burdens of preaching, book distribution, and ambitious projects, which can only be borne by a great company of Vaiṣṇavas working together in love and trust. Let us welcome that great load that will break our backs and crush us so that if we are ever so proud and foolish as to strike out alone, giving up the help and association of our Godbrothers. Let us happily throw ourselves into such strenuous labors of love that cannot accommodate even a momentary dalliance in sense gratification.

Let us always remember the pastimes of Śrīla Prabhupāda, who took great risks for the sake of his Guru Mahārāja and his Lord. Following in his footsteps, let us deliberately seek those challenges and difficulties in devotional service that will keep us feeling helplessly and forever dependent upon the causeless mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Let us become as bold as Prabhupāda was, daring everything – confident of his guidance and protection. But let us never fall victim to pride, thinking ourselves great preachers, the doers of mighty works, or the natural leaders of men.

Such false pride is often the undoing of the second-class man described by our Ācāryadeva. Such a man is better than the idle dreamer, for he actually begins the great undertaking. But when difficulties arise, he fails to meet their challenge and instead gives up the attempt in disgrace.

Let us therefore take very seriously Śrīla Prabhupāda ’s warning about the external allurement of money and sex, which, he says, have mislead many great stalwarts in the spiritual line. But let us also beware of the fell blows of an even more powerful enemy – that small voice within that is the last snare of Māyā and that whispers, “You are so nice.” Let us pray constantly at the lotus feet of Krishna that in the midst of our strenuous efforts we will never imagine ourselves to be the doers, but that we will always remember our humble position as mere instruments in the capable hands of the predecessor *ācāryas* – as bits of dust at the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray at your lotus feet that we will never give up the idea of the “Big ISKCON,” a worldwide society of pure devotees, a competitionless society based upon trust amongst Vaiṣṇava gentlemen and common love for Śrī Krishna. Please make us your fearless followers prepared to enter the mouth of the serpent if necessary for your service – always remaining utterly confident, cheerful, and convinced of your protection and guidance. And please create such a familial bond of love between us, your followers, that we will prefer to die rather than to allow petty rivalries or sense gratification to divide us from each other.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we thus pray to be allowed to become first-class followers of Your Divine Grace. Let us be such followers who can undertake great things on your behalf, who are alert and expert in overcoming difficulties that may arise, whether external or internal, and who are determined enough to stick with the task, no matter how long it takes, until there is glorious victory. And please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, during the fight, please remain in our midst, for your mercy is all that we are made of.

(Written by Baṭu Gopāla dāsa)

*Your humble servants in Belgium,*

## Benin City, Nigeria

O mind, worship the lotus feet of A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,  
who delivers all from the ocean of nescience.  
By working, living, and speaking the transcendental sound,  
he has destroyed the vanity of modern science.

O mind, worship the lotus feet of A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,  
whose voice is like a lion’s and whose pen is like a lion.  
Journeying to all the corners of the earth,  
he has frustrated Kali-yuga’s terrible network of illusion.

O mind, worship the lotus feet of A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,  
who brought to us the eternal science of Bhagavad-gītā.  
Following in the footsteps of the mahājanas,  
he established the actual Absolute Truth, Ajita.

O mind, worship the lotus feet of A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,  
which are the abode of the great paramahaṁsas.  
Sometimes laughing, sometimes crying, sometimes chastising,  
this mahātmā has created in this world an army of Vaiṣṇavas.

O mind, tired of the endless tossing of Māyā’s waves,  
now free yourself from the false allurements of this dreamland,  
and like a honeybee, O mind, immerse yourself in bhakti-yoga  
and thus satisfy the soul of A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

*Your servants,*

## Berkeley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances.

From the front, from behind, form all angles of vision, you are the pure devotee of the Supreme Lord.

The other day on Market Street in San Francisco we had a *mahā-hari-nāma,* composed of Trivikrama Swami, the Berkeley devotees, the Bhaktivedānta Institute devotees, and many other guests. We had a book table and hundreds of onlookers. At a certain ecstatic moment during this happening I realized that your movement is alive and well and still has the potential to take over the world. The formula for success is the same, original formula that you gave us many years ago, namely, hearing, chanting, distribution of books and *prasādam*, worshiping the Deity, etc. As long as we’re able to follow this, success will automatically follow.

Our movement is definitely going through a rocky-boat stage, but you are still our captain, so we pray to you,

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇadhāram ivārṇavam*

“We think that we’ve met Your Goodness by the will of Providence, and thus we accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the ocean of Kali, which destroys the good qualities of the human being.

“Your Goodness can go everywhere in the three worlds like the sun and can penetrate into the internal regions of everyone, like the air. So kindly find out the deficiencies in us, although we are engaged in disciplinary regulation and vows.

“All glories to you, O sagacious sage of steady heart. You are expert in all subjects, and you are vibrating the pure message of Krishna Caitanya, which enlivens all the distressed souls of the universe.”

We, the devotees of Berkeley, pledge the following:

To continue our Food for Life program seven days a week.

To continue *hari-nāma* in the streets of Berkeley and San Francisco – minimum five days a week.

To increase your book distribution in the Bay Area and other surrounding localities.

To worship Their Lordships Jagannātha, Balarāma, Subhadrā, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda with pomp and circumstance and to increase the cleanliness and punctuality.

To welcome all conditioned souls and devotees to the temple and encourage them in their spiritual quest.

To improve our New Devotee program (which now consists of eight new men and one *bhaktin*), so that they will be granddisciples that you can be proud of.

To make our temple a focus of the Indian community, with a vigorous Life Membership program.

Please tolerate our inadequacies, Śrīla Prabhupāda; our minds are giving us a hard time. We know *māyā* is a pure devotee; she gets up in the morning before we do. She’s expert, but wherever there are your books, the holy name, and *prasādam*, there will be all auspiciousness and victory. It is said, “An ounce of devotion is worth tons of faithlessness.” So let devotional service to Krishna through you be our salvation.

*The devotees of ISKCON Berkeley*

## Bhaktivedanta Āśrama

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this auspicious day, the celebration of your appearance, your Vyāsa-pūjā

Each year, in trying to glorify you as the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva and the *paramparā* system, we fall into awe at the magnitude of your position. You have said that some people act nicely but don’t preach, and some people preach but don’t act nicely. However, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have seen that you always preached and acted perfectly.

You taught us that there is the book *bhāgavata* and the devotee *bhāgavata.* The book *bhāgavata* is the transcendental literature to bathe our intelligence, and the devotee *bhāgavata* is the person who lives the *bhāgavata* philosophy, whom we should follow.

The complexities of trying to fulfill your order and dream for ISKCON have the manipulating effect of forcing us to surrender to both *bhāgavatas.* Often, at a time of decision-making, we are faced with the questions: How did Prabhupāda act in such a situation? What did he say? We want to follow your order and desire in every decision.

You showed the perfect example in following your Guru Mahārāja. Because he had an immense desire to preach in the West, your mind never deviated from the divine purpose of fulfilling this mission. Even throughout your startling success, you would always remind us that it was simply the mercy of your Guru Mahāraja.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are definitely all that we are made up from. We pray to you to please give us the intelligence to simply follow you, and then, as in your life, everything will be sublime.

(Written by Kārṣṇa dāsa)

*Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Āśrama,*

## Bologna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious occasion, Krishna has shown us His mercy by sending you, a pure devotee coming from the spiritual world. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, like the merciful father taking care of his beloved sons and disciples, have enabled us to grow up so that we can also go back to Krishna, back to Godhead. In this material world there is nothing more important than to associate with you. This is the gift of providence, that I met Your Divine Grace, meeting a great soul, a swanlike man. You have taught me how to develop divine qualities and separate them from my demoniac habits. You personally guide me in your books. You give me the enthusiasm to carry out my service to you. My debt toward you increases day by day. For this reason you are my eternal spiritual master, the most worshipable personality. Other than you, nobody could save me. You are unique, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The way to satisfy you is to distribute your powerful books. You said that the key of success is to be always enthusiastic and to enthuse others. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow me to be always enthusiastic for the whole year, until next Vyāsa-pūjā, and in this way thank you more in offering better service and better qualities.

This book distribution is the one thing that conquers my sex desire. Plesae, Śrīla Prabhupāda, wash away this dangerous, sinful enemy. Pray for me to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva, for you are a great devotee, and He will listen to you, who are so full of compassion, full of mercy, always ready to preach the Lord’s mission all over the world. I bow down under your holy lotus feet, taking the dust. Your lotus feet are guiding all the world toward Lord Krishna. I beg you to kindly give me strength so that I can succeed in helping you to preach and distribute more books and increase devotee understanding of how to preach.

(Written by Durgama dāsa)

*We beg to remain your humble servants,*

## Bombay

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

As we sit and meditate on your achievements in preaching Krishna consciousness, it’s only a stonelike heart that cannot glimpse some appreciation of your greatness.

It is said that the pure devotee of Krishna is *tri-kāla-jña,* that is he can see past, present, and future, and certainly you must have had that vision to push so determinedly for your Hare Krishna Land project here in Juhu. It was by your compassion, felt for suffering humanity, that this wonderful project was carried to completion and consequently now attracts lakhs of thirsty people weekly. No one else could have built such a grand glorification for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsavihārījī and satisfied Them as much as you did.

In fact this same desire to preach Krishna consciousness manifested wherever you went in the world. Those who know will attest that your ISKCON society is present and vigorously preaching in over one hundred countries worldwide, thus making you India’s greatest representative of all time; and what is more is that instead of delivering some streamlined version of the Absolute Truth covered in impersonalism and voidism for the attraction and gratification of the masses, you have simply preached *jīvera svarūpa haya kṛṣṇear nitya-dāsa –* that we are all the eternal servants of Krishna.

You knew our attraction for emulating Krishna, and thus in your purports to *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* you constantly referred to Krishna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You knew our desire to imitate Krishna was the cause of our repeated birth and death in this material world. You never gave up reminding us of the distinction between *ātmā* and *paranātmā* and how they are never equal, or one quantitatively, either now or at any time in the future. In this way, you have saved us from spiritual suicide even whilst on the spiritual path by being always vigilant to crush the impersonal calamity. Indeed, your own Guru Mahārāja, Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda, was most thankful for your poem in this connection on the occasion of his Vyāsa-pūjā here in Bombay in 1935. You glorified him for the same qualities you likewise in turn imbibed.

Another of your glorious qualities is *vairāgya,* or reunciation. The word Bhagavān means “he who possesses all six opulences in full.” Of course this refers to the Supreme Lord, but one or more of these opulences can be possessed to different degrees by the finite *jīvas,* and each opulence is very attractive in itself. Consequently, as you so often did, you showed us your opulence of renunciation. This opulence is natural for a pure devotee; but you showed it in such a special way – and that was in the preaching field.

Whatever did not facilatate the preaching was passed over by you and given up. For example, you would go anywhere to spread the mission of Śrī Caitanya, even if no facilities were present. We remember you at Bhubaneśvara in 1976 in the mud hut, but also at Māyāpur. Indeed, you seemed more happy and blissful in such circumstances of very simple living.

Other times when you were ill, sometimes severely ill, you would still keep your scheduled lecture program. This happened at Cross Maiden during a large *pandal* that was organized for you. In spite of great difficulty, you renounced your comfort for the spiritual gain of others, and you delivered the lecture regardlessly.

Your most enduring quality must remain, though, as that of fathomless compassion bestowed on all you contacted. You have listed twenty-six qualities of a devotee in your books, but this one, namely compassion *(karuṇā),* is very prominent in you. You would never turn away anybody from contacting Krishna, and you devised so many loving tricks to ensnare even the most doubting soul. Even if they rejected everything, they could be heard to repeat at some later time, “Oh, yes, he was so nice and personal with me.”

Prabhupāda, you force us to enter into a loving network that could never be broken as so-called loving affairs in this material world are broken. You showed us glimpses of the ever-fresh, sweet, pure, transcendental love that is there in all of us for our eternal Lord, Śrī Krishna. You have shown us real life beyond this temporary phantasmagoria of bodily relationships in this dirty material world.

May we take shelter at your most cooling lotus feet and pray that we may ever be able to remain situated under them. We know that it is only here that all desires can be satisfied and that the perfection of life can be attained. Thank you for allowing us the opportunity to serve you.

*Your servants at Hare Krishna Land, Juhu, Bombay,*

## Boston

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet.

It was not until the advent of Your Divine Grace that the prophesies of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura came to pass. They predicted that one day there would appear a great *ācārya* who would spread Krishna consciousness throughout the world and cause the Lord’s holy name to be sung and heard in every town and village. Indeed, Lord Krishna caused you to appear in the same year as the Ṭhākura’s passing. This is most significant.

In your childhood horoscope it was foretold that at the age of seventy you would cross the ocean, become a powerful religious leader, and open 108 temples. By the mercy of Lord Nityānanda, Jagāi and Mādhāi were transformed into *kṛpāsiddhas.* The Lord has now extended that mercy in the form of Your Divine Grace, who will undoubtedly continue rescuing innumerable Jagāis and Mādhāis from the ocean of birth and death.

As you have said, “I will live forever in my books.” By constant, submissive oral reception of your Bhaktivedanta purports, devotional service to Ṣrī Kṛṣṇa is established within one’s heart as an irrevocable fact. This is because you are that *nitya-siddha* devotee, the person *Bhāgavatam,* acting as a transparent via medium between Lord Krishna and ourselves.

The Lord has sent you, His eternal companion, as our deliverance. By your association, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our heartfelt love for you cannot help but spring forth unchecked. Now we are all desiring to remain your eternal companions in service and surrender, birth after birth, if you so desire. We don’t mind. You have convinced us beyond a doubt that one can return to Godhead in this lifetime. Because you have assured, therefore we are assured.

Always clinging tightly to your lotus feet, we are your permanent disciples, granddisciples, and sincerely aspiring candidates of Boston ISKCON. Please kindly help us.

(Written by Viṭhṭhaleśvara dāsa)

*Your servants at Boston ISKCON,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Brisbane

Almost a decade has elapsed since Śrīla Prabhupāda passed from our view. That time has been one of learning through painful experience. Individually and collectively, we have been forced to mature. One obvious fact has, however, become increasingly clear, regardless of any other difference of opinion: Śrīla Prabhupāda was and always will be *the greatest of all.*

In light of our continuing struggle to preach the achievements of Śrīla Prabhupāda – who came to the West at an advanced age from a diametrically opposed cultural background – in single-handedly establishing this *saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Caitanya all over the world seem increasingly inconceivable. And as we realize more and more our own spiritual frailty, it increases our awe of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s unique status as the empowered representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Krishna. In retrospect, it becomes all the more obvious that he is the spiritual master at whose feet all other spiritual masters will eternally sit.

In studying his life even more deeply, what we see emerging in the character of Śrīla Prabhupāda is the most amazing combination of a profound awareness and acceptance of his own unique transcendental destiny coupled with an absolute personnel humility. He was, and in the future will become, recognized by everyone as the ONE who came and saved the world in its darkest hour.

Yet this most elevated of all souls, who walked amongst us with such quiet dignity, actually associated with us. To we who were most fallen he gave himself freely and unassumingly. And although he was the protector and savior of us all, he allowed us to help him and take care of him.

Who was and is Śrīla Prabhupāda exactly? We may not know, but at least one thing is clear: *He was and always will be the greatest of all.*

## Chicago

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet as they take to the path of annihilating *māyā* and enlivening the devotees to take up pure devotional service. As we strive to preserve your teachings both in our hearts and in the preaching mission, we pray to understand more how to continue your ISKCON. It is not an easy accomplishment to preserve what you have given us in your struggle to establish Lord Caitanya’s mission. We pray for the strength to cooperate and inspire this devotional service in our ISKCON family and in society at large. As we read the stories in your *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* of different falldowns and deviations, we had little realization of how these factually applied to our personal lives. Of course, we had your example of perfect discipline. Now we can understand more how much love you had for your disciples and for humanity at large and how much you tried to help us over *māyā’s* hurdle.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we desire very much to mature to the higher level needed to cooperatively spread this Krishna consciousness. When you were asked questions about being qualified to carry out some particular accomplishment, you would say, “Just do it!” Just as the devotee wants to serve Krishna, Krishna wants to serve His devotee more. Just as much as the devotee wants to serve you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you desire even more that the devotee become advanced and offer that service. Please let us become instrumental in your preaching mission. Let us engage in distributing more and more books, helping more and more conditioned souls, distributing more and more *prasādam*, and making more and more advancement ourselves!

To serve Krishna is the only way  
That is topmost, you would say.  
When you were here you made it easy  
For everyone to engage in this way busily!  
Actually it was you that we would serve,  
Offering everything to Krishna you had that nerve!  
No false ego amidst all the glory,  
For us conditioned souls to study that story.  
To use our intelligence for Krishna only,  
You gave us the ISKCON family – we’re never lonely.  
We pray to be humble – mind, body, and words surrender,  
Transcend the illusion that we deserve the splendor.  
You never wanted one to say he was in control;  
This ruins cooperation – the most devastating toll.  
This ISKCON movement is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
To be part of it we are very proud!

Just as in Māyāpur you chastised the Rādhā-Dāmodara party for pushing and shoving other devotees in their enthusiasm to dance “enthusiastically” for you, now different devotees are pushing and shoving to “please you” again. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that the pushing and shoving will subside and we can once again confidently and assuredly proceed to enthusiastically spread this Krishna consciousness movement you have organized. Then we will also feel the family spirit, love, and devotion you instilled in this International Society for Krishna Consciousness and reunite in *saṅkīrtana* with redoubled strength!

Praying to please you always in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement and in ISKCON,

*Your devotees in New Sevākuñja*

## China

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

We are happy to report that your mission is still expanding here. This is happening in this atheistic and materialistic place to the astonishment of everyone, including ourselves, whose poor-heartedness presents yet another impediment to the spreading of Krishna consciousness here. We are therefore continually being made aware of your presence with us and your special mercy upon us, because there is no other explanation for our continued success. We are actually seeing how you have built a house in which the whole world can live, and that Krishna consciousness is truly all-attractive. We feel eternally grateful for the priceless opportunity you have given us, and we pray for greater strength and resolve to serve you more purely, so that the movement can grow here more quickly, giving this same chance to all the Chinese people.

Begging to remain your humble servants,

*Your servants of the China Mission,*

## Christchurch

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement!

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, our glorious founder-*ācārya.* We worship you because you are *nitya-siddha,* an eternal associate of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Krishna.

Whenever there is a decline in religious practice, Krishna comes Himself or He sends His representative. Being very merciful to the conditioned souls, Krishna sent you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to rescue us from from our wretched material existence.

Just as a father provides all necessities for his children, so you have provided us with all we need in order to return to Krishna in the spiritual world. You have given us ISKCON, your books, the G.B.C., *gurus*, the associating of devotees, Deity worship, and *prasādam.* In short, you have given us everything. Such is your potency, that you are transforming *mlecchas* into *sādhus.*

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda, *patita-pāvana,* savior of the whole world!

*Your disciples in Christchurch*

## Dallas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

All glories unto you  
O great master  
Who are the hope and light  
In this dark Kali age.

All glories unto you,  
Who brought the Absolute Truth, Śrī Krishna, out of obscurity  
And gave Him to the world.

All glories unto you  
O modern Vyāsa  
Whose translations and purports have opened millions of eyes.

All glories unto you  
O great transcendental alchemist  
Who have taken drunken and foolish mlecchas  
And made them dancing devotees,  
Intoxicated with love of God.

All glories unto you  
Who have left a legacy of temples, farms, schools, and literature  
For the benefit of all living entities.

All glories unto you  
Who have saved us from mindless hedonism  
And the bottomless pit of voidism and impersonalism.

All glories unto you  
O great and merciful spiritual master  
Who have given us the sublime and limitless  
Treasure of bhakti-yoga.

All glories unto you  
Whose mission to save this world form Kali’s hell  
Has become our mission.

II

O great and merciful spiritual master  
We pray that we may be able to fulfill  
Your mission in this material world.

We pray also, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
That you grant us the vision  
To understand that this Krishna consciousness movement  
Is successful by your mercy only.

We beg that you never let us forget  
That you alone  
Can make the entire world Krishna conscious  
And that we are but your insignificant servants.

O lord and master,  
On this most auspicious anniversary of your holy appearance  
We pray that we may be engaged  
In glorifying  
Your thoughts, your words, and your deeds  
Today, tomorrow, and for all tomorrows yet to come.

(Written by Dṛṣṭā dāsa)

*Your humble servants at Rādhā-Kālachandjī Dhāma,*

## Denmark

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories unto you, the modern-day Vyāsadeva!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We have never met you, neither can we say that we know you. The only connection we have with you is the legacy you left behind—your sincere representatives and your books. Hearing from those who have met you, we get a glimpse of your unfathomable glories, and through your books we are brought into your intimate association. Being simple and naive, we do not understand the various discussions and opinions going on in the movement. We do not know what it means to put you back in the center. We only know you through your books—your ecstatic emotions—and how much you wanted them distributed profusely.

We were not so fortunate to have your personal association. Therefore, we are all the more desperate to gain your association by reading your books, producing your books, and distributing them. "If you want to know me, read my books" (June 1977). From your many letters we can understand that the production and distribution of your books is most pleasing to you. As far as our limited understanding goes, putting you in the center means putting your mission in the center of our lives. You gave us the motto: "Preaching is the essence, books are the basis, purity is the force, and utility is the principle."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have not left us. You are living forever in your books. The world may say that the founder-*ācārya* of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement has died, but the fact is that you will never die as long as your books are being distributed. "If this book distribution goes on, I will never die. I will live for centuries." As long as your books take a central role, you will always remain in the forefront and center of this preaching movement. Your life is made of preaching, and you are continually doing so, daily encountering so many conditioned souls, preaching the eternal message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We are not satisfied with just offering you worship of flowers and praise, nor a big, opulent *vyāsāsana* placed in the center of the temple room. We want to go out on marathon, distributing your books and placing the *saṅkīrtana* results at your lotus feet. "The sales figures—this is the only solace in my life" (13 / 8 / 74). We just want to produce your books in the local language and bring them to the conditioned souls. You are the best preacher, and you are able to touch and change their hearts, and through this book distribution you will continue to create a revolution in the impious lives of this misdirected civilization.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we may not have met you, but somehow or other, by daily reading, producing, and distributing your books, we feel close to you.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, we want nothing more than your blessings to become more fanatically attached to reading, producing, and distributing your books, for there lies all success. Begging for your mercy, we pray to perfectly follow in the footsteps of those who are fully surrendered and dedicated to maintaining and expanding the eternal message of the *guru-paramparā* as found in your books. We beg to remain eternally prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet.

*Your disciples in Denmark,*
