## Denver

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances at your lotus feet.

Today we reaffirm our relationship with you as our spiritual master, dearmost friend, and "ever well-wisher." You know more than anyone our frailties in the execution of devotional service. We are foolish and still immature in spiritual life. Yet knowing you were always expert at appreciating the spiritual utility of nearly anything, we offer ourselves to you, hoping that we can be useful instruments in your service.

It occurs to us, by careful study of your Bhaktivedanta purports, letters, and tapes, that service to you can't be grasped without an appreciation of your intense desire to see your books distributed worldwide. Your immeasurably potent books are "directed toward bringing about a revolution in the impious lives of this world's misdirected civilization." Preaching must deliver society from impiety. Anyone who takes the time to carefully study what you have written in your books will appreciate that preaching in synonymous with book distribution.

Therefore you repeatedly asked us, encouraged us, even pleaded with us, to "Distribute books! Distribute books! Distribute books!" You want them in every home; never mind that people may not immediately appreciate them. *Idaṁ bhāgavataṁ nāma purāṇaṁ brahma-saṁhitam.* These books you have given us are not different than the Lord. Indeed, you have written, "they are to be respected as much as one respects the Lord."

We don't know how we will satisfy your desire, and we doubt our ability to see it done. After all, this is Kali-yuga, when *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is thrown in the garbage, and trash is profitably marketed at every corner bookstore. In spite of all the obstacles we face, our prayer is that you guide us in our meager attempts. We prostrate ourselves before your divine order and hope that somehow or other our dull brains can conceive of how your books are to be distributed to every door.

*Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and Lord Caitanya Himself predict that a flood of love of God will soon inundate human society and "young men, old men, women, and children" shall be drowned in its all-encouraging blissful waves. Proceeding this flood of love of God and the tumultuous chanting of the holy names, your books have covertly entered millions of materialistic households. As the appearance of Hanuman at the shores of Lanka foretold the destruction of the demoniac kingdom of Rāvaṇa, similarly, the appearance of your books in this misdirected civilization heralds a sane. God-conscious society.

Please allow us to ever bear these thoughts in mind and keep them close to our hearts. Please let us be to you what the forest monkeys were to Lord Rāma. Please engage us in your continuing assault on atheism, godless material science, impersonalism, and other countless varieties of irreligion.

Even though the opposition is so great that we sometimes hear words of discord even from among our own ranks to the effect that "Book distribution just agitates people," or "Book distribution is ineffective," let no one forget that it is your order, and you have said that by book distribution Kṛṣṇa consciousness will "automatically increase." Please give us the intelligence to do it nicely, exactly according to your will.

We are not very intelligent or advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but we desire to keep your words close to our hearts. We want to please you. Please do with us whatever you will.

Offering ourselves and all that we possess to you, we eternally desire to serve you. Please let us never leave that service.

*Your aspiring servants and book distributors at ISKCON Denver.*

## Detroit

**Gifts of His Divine Grace**

When a special person gives a special gift, every time that gift is seen or used, the person who gave it is remembered. By meditating on the gifts of Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can think of him throughout each of the twenty-four hours of the day.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this day and every other we are aspiring to remember you by taking advantage of all the gifts you have freely given.

You have given us your *praṇāma mantras,* which we are meant to recite loudly enough for you, who are always by our side, to hear. Thus many times a day, such as upon rising, entering and leaving the temple, upon bowing before the Deities, during your *guru-pūjā,* before respecting *prasāda,* and before taking rest, we can intensely remember you.

You have given us the early morning hours, the best time to rise up and and without fail or conditions attend *maṅgala ārati* and dance before the beautiful Deity of the Lord. Let us depend on Kṛṣṇa and so find the strength to continue on.

You have given us the auspicious sound of the conch, heralding the appearance of the Lord; the ringing bell; the sweet-smelling incense; the fragrant flower garland *prasādam* to wear about our necks and purify our hearts; the flaming ghee lamp; the graceful whisk; the peacock fan; and the nectarean taste of the *caraṇāmṛta,* which has glided down from the Lord's lotus feet.

You have given us the *ācāryas* and their thoughts, words, and songs.

You have given us Tulasī-devī to daily worship and circumambulate and so cleanse away our sins.

You have given us our daily *japa* quota—for those who follow your instructions, the best of times.

You have given us cleanliness—everywhere, internally and externally, everything should sparkle like crystal. Let us always remain refreshed and jubilant, and not muddy ourselves again like the elephant.

You have given us *tilaka* to regularly adorn our bodies in all twelve places as temples of Lord Viṣṇu. You have given us *dhotis, kaupinas, śikhās,* neck beads, and saris. Let us be proud to wear such Vaikuṇṭha Vaiṣṇava dress.

You have given us *prasādam,* a friend in need, Kṛṣṇa's mercy, to respect, enjoy, and distribute to others and so soften the hardest heart. Let us not eat lumps of sin and so become like the pig.

You have given us the four regulative principles, and so we are elevated to the standard of human beings—let us always follow our sacred vows.

You have given us the opportunity to associate with devotees—the greatest treasure of this world, the loss of which is the greatest fear. Let us now learn how to associate with devotees, the greatest and most difficult art.

You have given us humility and meekness, without which it is not possible to attract Lord Kṛṣṇa’s attention. Let not our one ounce of knowledge make us the victims of devilish pride and self-righteousness.

You have given us the broadest vision, practical common sense, and piercing logic. Let us not become dry Bible-bashers or lounge in watered-down deviations, but let us live the life of a Vaiṣṇava.

You have given us appreciation of others—anyone can criticize or be criticized, but you inspired in everyone's heart divine qualities, because your heart was devoid of the propensity to find fault.

You have given us optimism, the power to see the bright side, to make the best use of apparent bad bargains. Let us not sink into cynicism, negativity, pessimism, and harsh sarcasm—all enemies that kill the delicate creeper of remembrance of Kṛṣṇa.

You have given us the daily *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, *nityaṁ bhāgavata-sevayā—*let us all hear those same old instructions, ever fresh and new to the swanlike devotee.

You have given us *Jaya Rādhā Mādhava,* your own devotion and meditation on the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Let us also be transported to Vṛndāvana at the dawn of every day.

You have given us your books, your very self, living eternally in sound vibration. Your personal ecstasies unfold on every page. Let us drink and drink again and again of this immortal nectar, irresistible for those expert in relishing mellows.

You have given us book distribution—the strategy to destroy all materialistic concepts, the potency to change the course of history. Let us be your instruments for executing your Guru Mahārāja's order.

You have given us *harināma saṅkīrtana* to garland the world with the wreath of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, to cleanse the world of its din, to awaken the world from the deathly snake-bite of false dreams, to penetrate to the core of everyone's inner self. Let us all go out together and chant, give out *prasādam* and BTGs and forget all our relative differences.

You have given us your daily *guru-pūjā,* a chance to beg your forgiveness for our wrongs, a chance to worship you, united at your feet, all your little children.

You have given us a mission, a goal for our lives that's worth giving every moment for. You've roused us from the dead. Let us not sink once again into oblivion.

You have given us ISKCON, the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, of which you were so proud—that youthful, vibrant movement energetically bringing about a revolution in the lives of the world's misdirected civilization. Let us manage things in such a way that we are also proud to unfurl the ISKCON Jayapatāka victory flag.

You have given us wonderful Deities of the Lord to worship and become attached to. Let us make the Deity the center of our lives and serve Him with feeling, otherwise we will feel an unbearable burden, and gradually our temples will fill with many neglected Deities and become resting places for the pigeon.

You have given us courage and conviction, no more place for the word *impossible.* You have given us tolerance, gravity, silence, and expertise. You have given us sanity, magnanimity, self-control, victory, and peace. Let us cultivate all good qualities in ourselves and so attract the bewildered populace, who helplessly ride on the chariot of their lusty minds.

You have given us the intelligence to reform our society before it is too late.

You have given us Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and installed Them on the lotus of our purified hearts.

You have given us yourself—unstinting in your kindness, without consideration of personal inconvenience. Let us try to give ourselves to others as you gave yourself to us.

You have given us the wonderful preaching facility of Devasādhana Mandira. Our hope is that we may be able to give you in return the small gift of a progressive unfolding of your vision for the future here by working together in purity and unity, love and trust.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us never forget you by never forgetting to appreciate your unlimited gifts.

(Written by Rohiṇīnandana dāsa) *Your servants of the community and congregation of Devasādhana Mandira,*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Not enough words can be spoken to praise your glories, but on this auspicious day we're humbly trying to express our appreciation to you for having saved us from this horrible material existence. By your grace, here in Santo Domingo the people are taking up Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We are all begging at your lotus feet to please allow us to remain in your divine service. And for your pleasure and the pleasure of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, allow us to continue spreading the chanting of the holy name for the benefit and saving grace of all mankind.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants in the Dominican Republic.*

## Durban

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

The Supreme Lord, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, has appeared in the form of this ISKCON movement just to glorify you, His pure devotee. Your intense battles and struggles to establish this movement have already become immortal pastimes in the annals of Vaiṣṇava history. Your loving exchanges with Śrī Kṛṣṇa are as unfathomable as the ocean.

How could you have achieved so much alone, with no association except for your neophyte, uncultured disciples? Jesus Christ uplifted his own people, but you uplifted all nations of people. You converted barbarians into *brāhmaṇas.* What greater miracle has ever been performed in human history? You were empowered beyond all realms of speculative imagination. Who can dare to deny this?

Those who surrender to you become happy. Others simply suffer, bound up by the knots of their own pride and envy. Your gentle compassion knows no bounds. It is an ever-flowing stream of understanding. All the transcendental qualities of the pure devotee adorn your personality, like shining jewels. Your childlike simplicity and your grave wisdom combine together to charm and attract everyone who has met Your Divine Grace.

Why could we not appreciate the rare opportunity of your association, available to so few? Why did we not take full advantage and surrender one hundred percent in the presence of your lotus feet? And why did you leave us so soon?

This movement is struggling bitterly in your absence, surviving an unlimited array of trials and tribulations. It is like a ship without a captain. We have no other shelter besides Your Divine Grace. Thus we are experiencing the bittersweet taste of your separation.

We hereby fall down in the dust of your lotus feet and beg you to illuminate our hearts with the brilliance of your divine teachings, that we may live eternally with you. Help us to always stick rigidly to your instructions so that we may continue to develop your transcendental movement in the right way, without becoming deviated onto any of the innumerable sidetracks laid by Māyā. If only we can become empowered by a drop of your mercy, then certainly ultimate success will be ours, and Kṛṣṇa consciousness will become accepted as the world religion for this age.

Please allow us to serve you eternally, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your mercy is all that we are made of.

*Your humble servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha Temple, Durban,*

## East Lansing

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, I would like to thank you from the bottom of my heart for giving me the opportunity to engage in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is most miraculous how you came for such a short time to this Western world and changed the hell-bent lives of so many of us. You put us on the right course leading to Lord Kṛṣṇa's lotus feet. You are teaching us that it is indeed possible to go back home to Godhead in this very life.

Although your movement has been going through some "rocky" times during these last several years since your disappearance, I feel hopeful that in due course of time the proper adjustments will be made. We are seeing improvements more and more in recent times. You said that we have to "boil the milk." I am praying that I will come up to the standard and not be scorched or evaporated in the boiling. Rather, I beg to become solidly fixed at your lotus feet with a proper service attitude. It has been a great delight to always have "too much service." But now I am feeling that the time has come for me to increase the quality of my service to you by fully engaging this wretched mind in the flawless science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is my duty to assimilate this knowledge by scrutinizingly studying your books. Through the proper medium of hearing, chanting, and honoring *prasādam,* the whole world could be liberated from its suffering condition. As your disciple, there is unlimited work to be done on your behalf to fulfill this mission. I have been "dragging my feet" for too long now and finally want to only act instrumentally.

In many letters to your disciples you stressed the importance of making devotees from the student or intelligent class of society in order for our Society to progress. So, here in East Lansing we are making a humble attempt in trying to expose the students to this wonderful philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

My prayer is that you please always keep me linked up to you by remaining connected to this ISKCON. By associating with devotees who are fixed up and dedicated to studying your books and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I gain much inspiration. I simply want to follow in your footsteps and "fight to the last breath."

*Your eternal servants in Lansing, Michigan,*

Apūrva dāsa / Kamalinī-devī dāsī / Pañca-tattva and Amala-purāṇa / Bruce Callopy / Dave Phillips

## Fiji

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda. You carried out the order of your Guru Mahārāja and greatly pleased him. This is the method of understanding the true meaning of Vedic knowledge, i.e. working with full dedication to please Guru and Kṛṣṇa. Offerings on Vyāsa-pūjā by disciples and granddisciples are expressions of appreciation and indebtedness for what you have done for everyone's welfare.

You explained to us our true identity as spiritual sparks and servants of Kṛṣṇa. By your books, lectures, interviews, and all of the devotional processes, you taught surrender to Kṛṣṇa, as everything is working under His direction. By simple logic and analogies, simple scientific observations and deductions, and also on the basis ofbona fide scriptures, you taught most difficult concepts from the *Vedas.* You established faith in Vedic scriptures as the source of perfect knowledge of all kinds. You explained that the defects that we see are due to our incomplete knowledge of the material and spiritual phenomena. To those who accepted the *Vedas,* you provided sastric evidence that Govinda in Goloka Vṛndāvana is the Supreme Absolute Truth. You pointed out that this is also the realized conclusion of all great personalities of past and present history. There are others who, despite so much logical understanding and evidence, refuse to accept Kṛṣṇa as the supreme master. To them you explained that God's supremacy is not dependent on individual or collective denial; His stringent laws will affect us regardless and force us to repeatedly suffer birth, death, old age, and disease.

Many sincere and intelligent persons came forward. To them you revealed that our relationship with Kṛṣṇa is eternal, but we have forgotten this due to absorption in the material energy. Thus you gave the pure, simple, and practical path of *bhakti* to revive our lost relationship as servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

The most special aspect about your presentation has been that you have repeatedly drawn our attention to those activities detrimental to spiritual progress, i.e. the four sinful engagements. On the other hand, you gave us positive activities to gain purification by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and engaging in devotional service in so many ways. Knowing the nature of Kali Yuga, you repeatedly emphasized the basis of spiritual life and how to progress to the middle and advanced level. All your teachings are appropriate for distribution to the whole spectrum of devotees, to guide society at large. Thus people have benefitted from your most auspicious appearance in this world and will continue to do so for a long time to come.

Personal Recollections: You are my Inspiration.

By your mercy and the mercy of your servants, I had the opportunity to spend the winter of '85 / '86 in Vṛndāvana and perform some small service to your lotus feet in your Samādhi Mandira. I still recall the boon of being at your home in Vṛndāvana—the Kṛṣṇa Balarāma-Mandira, the Gurukula, guesthouse, your personal quarters and courtyard, your *bhajan kutir* at Rādhā-Dāmodara Mandira, and all of Vṛndāvana Dhāma. I recall hundreds and hundreds of pilgrims visiting the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira, both from India and abroad. Many pilgrims would talk among themselves, appreciating what a wonderful place you had created. I still remember the beautiful Deities presiding there and blessing all who come before Them. By your exceptional mercy you give us the opportunity to reside in "Bhauma Vṛndāvana."

During the *day,* the loudspeakers at the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira would resound with the "Cintāmaṇi prayers" and *"govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tarn ahaṁ bhajāmi."*

Your pure voice, simple instruments, exceptional timeless melodies, and deep concentration would fill the air, Śrīla Prabhupāda. *Vow* devotion to Kṛṣṇa can be appreciated so much in your singing.

It gladdens our heart and fills us with newer inspiration of what human life is meant for—devotion to Kṛṣṇa under the guidance of his intimate servants. In such a mood I repeatedly resolve in my heart, "Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I will serve your mission to make my life and others' lives successful."

I know this will greatly please you, the *guru paramparā,* and Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu. Therefore, bless us that we may not deviate from this determination and may humbly serve in your lotus footsteps for your pleasure. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

(Written by Rāmacandra dāsa Gaura Vanacāri)

## France

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Gradually our devotees realize the need and try to become more and more dependent on your instructions.

Despite the fact that there is no official leader here, we feel your presence, which is manifested by the desire to live by your orders. Thus all the devotees are cooperating, especially because this was your deal-most desire. Indeed, as you yourself said, the reason for having a community such as ours is to promote association of devotees in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa.

Kṛṣṇa consciousness appears really to be a spiritual science. Through our present difficulties we are obliged to live more simply, and our thoughts naturally rest upon you and upon Śrī Kṛṣṇa, in a more intense way.

The practice of devotional service seems complex without the presence of a pure devotee such as you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. However, by following your instructions to the best of our ability, we feel ourselves perfectly connected to you. This helps us not only to maintain but to redouble our efforts in order to satisfy Your Divine Grace. We feel very strongly inspiration from you and help from Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

As our vision gets purified, the future of New Māyāpura becomes clearer, allowing us all to participate in this "unity in diversity."

The French Yātrā is taking a new turn. Rich in our past experience, we are confident of the future. We promise and we also pray for the strength, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, to maintain our efforts by your great mercy.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the savior of the fallen souls.

*From your devotees at New Māyāpura, France,*

## Gītā-nāgarī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa! Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this the ninety-first anniversary of your all-auspicious appearance in this mortal world!

Ten Augusts ago in England, during your *antya-līlā,* your secretary noted how much you were looking forward to traveling to America. "I want to live a little longer," you said, "to make everything more perfect."

"Will you do this by enthusing the devotees by staying with them," asked your secretary, "or is there a specific program?"

"A specific program," said Your Divine Grace. "I want to introduce *varṇāśrama.* At our Pennsylvania farm, the biggest problem of life is solved: food."

The food is better than ever, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and our country-style Food for Life program delivers Kṛṣṇa's bounty thrice-weekly to the area's needy.

But as we take our first few steps toward a real *vamasrama* village (It's about time!), we find that we are the neediest of all. We need desperately to remember you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You were the great general charging across the no-man's land of *varṇāśrama.* And by recalling even one of your favorite verses from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* we live with you by your divine instructions.

"O best among the twice-born, it is therefore concluded that the highest perfection one can achieve by discharging the duties prescribed for one's own occupation according to caste divisions and orders of life is to please the Personality of Godhead." (S.B. 1.2.13)

Like our "barnashram" mothers, the cows, we should learn how to take a little and give a lot. If we look out for number one—Kṛṣṇa—we will be able to understand *varṇāśrama's* transcendent unity in diversity (and diversity in unity) and please Your Divine Grace.

"A hospital for the heart" was your specific metaphor for Gītā-nāgarī. Please help us, your patients, to sincerely chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, so that we can build that "house in which the whole world can live." You gave the blueprints. Now it's up to us.

*Your servants at Gītā-nāgarī.*

## Goteborg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.  
Darkness is spreading all over the place;  
we cry out and pray to Your Divine Grace.  
Save us, help us, so we may act right;  
the dark age of Kali has arrived.  
As many people, as many words;  
sometimes they fight with insults like swords.

But prabhus are taking more shelter under your preaching mace.  
Prabhupāda, the heat of the fire is cooled by the breeze of your grace.

Your books and instructions save us from trouble,  
so let's give them to others and not waste time with wrangle.

So many souls bewildered by demoniac illusions,  
never heard the holy name, looking for solutions.

The future of your mission is there, on the road,  
"Preach nicely and do good to others "—so you wrote.

Lecturing at Sunday feast—your presence so near,  
repeating your words—Prabhupāda is here!

As you once wrote in a wonderful letter,  
even on the spiritual platform, there is a "better."  
Book distribution and preaching are especially dear to the Lord;  
this is the way to receive your mercy in this world.  
Kali is preaching, too, with his malicious smile:  
"Forget the books and rest for a while.

"Sit down and discuss, spread your thoughts,  
don't try to reach all these foolish souls."  
But there again, Prabhupāda, you raise your divine mace:  
"Go out and preach! They all suffer in their gratification race."

Even our endeavors are bearing but a small result.  
What else is there to do in this lifetime but to preach and chant?

The holy name is still there, resounding from your lips.  
We will repeat it in hari-nāma; let the agitators raise their fists.  
O Prabhupāda, this world is an empty place;  
it is not possible to live without Your Divine Grace.

You saved us so many times by spreading your wing.  
Prabhupāda, protect us; Kali-yuga is in full swing.

Darkness is spreading all over the place.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for your books.  
All glories to Your Divine Grace!

(Written by Maṇidhāra dāsa)

## Guadalajara

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

In your book *The Nectar of Devotion* you give the following instruction: "One should not fail to offer some praise in the presence of the spiritual master."

So on this Vyāsa-pūjā day we have the opportunity to do this by trying to offer our humble homage at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Someone may say that this can only be done when the spiritual master is in his *vāpū* manifestation, but the answer to that is clearly described in a letter you wrote to one of our Godbrothers: "You write to say, 'I really miss Prabhupāda and my Godbrothers' association so much,' but I may remind you that I am always with you . . . Please remember always the humble teachings that you have received from me and that will keep you always associated with me and with your God-brothers also."

So on this occasion of your appearance day, let us offer our humble homage at your lotus feet and try to select nice words in your glorification.

When will Caitanya be kind to us  
so that we may take full shelter  
in the tranquil shadow of your  
divine lotus feet?

Holding a bit of grass  
between the teeth and  
most humbly and respectfully  
falling flat at your divine  
lotus feet, we surrender our  
words, actions, and minds  
completely to your divine mission.

O divine Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa,  
please cast Your merciful glance on us  
and increase our faith  
in Śrīla Prabhupāda at every moment.

This worldly ocean cannot  
be crossed without the  
mercy upon us  
of the divine holy feet of our  
divine lord and master  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

To conclude our humble homage, we have two humble requests.

Our first is that on this day, during the year, and throughout our lives, as you say: "Remember the teaching that you have received from me."

Our second one is as you say: "That will keep you always associated with me and with your Godbrothers also."

Let us fall flat at your divine feet. Let us keep you always in our hearts with the association of our Godbrothers in any of your ISKCON temples.

(Written by Rāma Govinda Swami)

*Your servants at Gaudalajara Mandira,*

## Heidelberg

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. As the years go by we can more and more gain an appreciation of what it means that you are the representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa and the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON. You are the actual preacher of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. By your transcendental literature you are powerfully preaching to the conditioned souls.

Just yesterday alone our Heidelberg temple received a few letters out of which two where quite amazing. One came from an aristocratic, well-known countess, ordering more of your transcendental books. She concluded by writing: "And please, I need a cassette where His Divine Grace is singing the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra," '*

Another one came from a boy who wrote from prison, where he had been confined because several years ago he had been involved with intoxication: "It is miserable here, no doubt. But by reading *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* I have noticed the more I read it the stronger its spiritual light grows. That is my only shelter. I read the *Gītā* to others who have an ear for it and chant together with them."

That same evening a phone call from one of the most famous German actors, seventy-two-year-old Georg Thomalla, came. "I am reading in the midst *of Nectar of Devotion,* when I realized that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is what I have been searching for all of my life. Later that morning I went to a park to chant, when the feeling overwhelmed me how much I have been gifted by Śrīla Prabhupāda. I could not help but cry."

You are the true representative of Nityānanda-Rāma by digging a channel to the conditioned souls through which Kṛṣṇa consciousness comes to them. By connecting as many souls as possible to you, our lives get meaning.

This year some of us had the opportunity to take you on pilgrimage throughout the holy places of Vṛndāvana on an oxcart—just like you desired. We remembered how you had asked us during one Māyāpur / Vṛndāvana Festival: "So, how do you like Vṛndāvana?" All the devotees responded: "Very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda!" Then you said: "Now it is your duty to establish Vṛndāvana in the West."

When we returned to the West and reached our temple after going through the cultural shock of Frankfurt Airport, we realized that in fact you have established transcendental *tīrthas* in the West. In the *śāstras* it is said: "Saints of your caliber are moving *tīrthas.*"

If devotees and guests are properly guided to you, who are eternally residing in these *tīrthas,* they will be introduced to the wonderful world of Kṛṣṇa consciousness through your mercy. How? By your *vāṇī!* But there is no doubt, we are also missing you very much. You single-handedly wove a network of Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world and expertly protected it against the vicious attacks of *māyā.* Now you expect us to fight and be steadfast ourselves. "Do as I have done." We are determined to prove ourselves worthy recipients of your mercy, but we continually pray for your powerful blessings to gain the necessary understanding and strength.

Great things could be done: the time is ripe. The conditioned souls are feeling the heat of Kali-yuga increasing. Please bless us with your mercy so that we may be able to distribute more and more books, attract more conditioned souls, increase the preaching of *nāma-haṭṭa,* and take care of your transcendental institution and projects in all ways that please you.

*Your children and grandchildren of the Heidelberg temple,*

## Helsinki

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances, bowing down at the dust of your lotus feet again and again. Being situated in one of your most northern ISKCON temples, wherein at least two thirds of the year the outside temperature is minus zero degrees, we do get our fair share of fresh air. But as is mostly the case in loving devotional service to the Supreme Lord, the devotee does not take into consideration his material circumstances, but rather the main consideration is how to always be spiritually well situated.

Since your departure we have gone through many changes, and *māyā* has been the cause of many casualties in her attempt to destroy the only hope you gave us that would save the world from total suffocation. That only hope is to serve Your Divine Grace by following your instructions and carrying out your orders, working cooperatively in spreading Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement, and thus being always happy in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Canto Three, Chapter Eighteen, it is stated that the Lord in the form of a boar fulfilled the prayers of the demigods by lifting the planet earth from its unnatural position. The Lord did so, regardless of all the harassment and abusive words of Hiraṇyākṣa's claims that the earth was not the property of the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the same way, having heard the prayers offered by your sincere followers, you have reestablished your rightful claim as the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, regardless of insistent claims of others. It was certainly a great insult to Your Divine Grace to see your international society suffering in this way, and we pray to you from the core of our hearts to please guide us in the manner in which we will always remain spiritually fresh and never suffocate, individually or collectively. Reuniting our hearts is like a breath of fresh air, for we now feel that there is great hope again to carry out our desire. Just as the demigods rain flowers on the Lord for His wonderful activities, we in the same way, on this, your most auspicious appearance day, incessantly offer flowers to you out of appreciation for everything you have done for us.

*Your humble servants in ISKCON Helsinki, Finland,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dhīraśānta dāsa

## Hillsborough, N.C.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are our only shelter, and we, despite our weaknesses, are your eternal servants. So on this your glorious appearance day, we thankfully approach you with our humble offerings.

You are the greatest servant of God any of us has ever known. You deserve to be so honored because of your uncompromised conviction that the pure strength of *guru, śāstra,* and *sādhu* will overcome all allurements and corruption of this world.

You always trusted completely in your Guru Mahārāja, that he would protect you anywhere, against whatever danger, and that he would be pleased with your honest attempts.

You made the glorification of the *Bhāgavatam* your only mission. Every thought you spoke came out of its pages. The *saṅkīrtana of* its distribution made you happy as did nothing else.

You taught by your example and constantly requested the indispensibility of living cooperatively in the society of Lord Kṛṣṇa's devotees. Independence, in your eyes, was a delusion to be lamented.

Let this, then, be our declaration *of dependence* on you, our ever well-wishing guide, who has planted in our hearts seeds of faith in the spiritual sound and in Vaiṣṇava association.

*Your servants of New Goloka,*

## Houston

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our prayer all year to you  
Was "What would you have us do?"  
Our annual Māyāpur meeting convened  
To address the question, "What did Prabhupāda mean?"  
What you did and wrote and what you said  
Separates Mahāprabhu's living branches from the dead.  
What you demand and what you desire  
Sacrifices false pride in its fire.  
By striving together to understand your will,  
Your request to cooperate we shall fulfill.  
We may become many, but the mission is still one,  
And it can only spread the way you would have it done.  
Apart from you and your books there is a disaster;  
We worship you as our spiritual master.  
We congregate as a family around your vyāsāsana today.  
Love and trust is your house in which the whole world may stay.

(Written by Gopāla Ācārya dāsa)

## Indonesia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.  
Near-total darkness filled the world  
And Kali held full sway.  
From the East you rose just like the sun  
To herald Kṛṣṇa's day.  
You took the name to every town  
And village in the world,  
Caitanya's prophecy fulfilled,  
His victory flag unfurled.  
Though millions sought religion true,  
Just cheating could they find.  
You and your books showed all the true path  
To sanctify the mind.  
Each meal we gobbled tons of sins;  
Our future: eons in hell.  
With tasty prasādam you saved us all,  
Gave taste for Kṛṣṇa as well.  
The voidists and impersonalists  
Taught zero, and the world's a dream.  
You smash, proving Personality  
Of Kṛṣṇa is supreme.  
"All life has come from matter!"  
So centuries' scientists cry.  
"No! Life's from life!" proves Prabhupāda;  
Not one now can deny.  
We thought that sensuous pleasure  
Was life's goal from birth to grave,  
But you taught that Kṛṣṇa's sweet service  
Is what we should always crave.

You taught us everything we know,  
Through your every word and deed.  
If we follow your instructions  
From birth and death we're freed.  
To deliver we poor fallen souls  
You travelled o'er the earth.  
To try to pay our debt to you  
Let us serve you our every birth.  
Our dull eyes cannot see you  
But we know you're with us still.  
We pray to be pure instruments  
To carry out your will.  
Divided we are worthless  
But united ISKCON's you.  
May we always work together  
So our love for you is true.  
You want us to preach in Indonesia,  
The world's fifth largest nation,  
Nearly all of them Mohammedans  
And the Army's the administration.  
We translate and print thousands of your books  
And sell throughout this land.  
Some local caste Hindu snakes lie to the government:  
Since three years your books are banned.

Some devotees are tortured and jailed,  
Just like in the USSR.  
These bodies are yours, we'll die if we must,  
But we'll never stop Kṛṣṇa-kathā.  
We know that all this is just Kṛṣṇa's test  
To see if we love you or māyā.  
Some may leave and return, some will stay and be strong,  
Like steel being tempered in fire.  
As you gave your whole life just to try to save us,  
We must give ours to try to save others.  
We are all spirit souls, we are all Kṛṣṇa's servants;  
This is how the whole world can be brothers.

We're not fit to serve Kṛṣṇa, or even Lord Gaura;  
We're unqualified, foolish, and low.  
Don't kick us away, our dear Prabhupāda,  
For your service is all that we know.  
O greatest devotee, our dear Prabhupāda,  
Let us serve you here, heaven, or hell,  
Chant your infinite glories 'til our very last breath,  
May we serve in the next life as well.

*The unworthy servants of the Indonesian Yātrā,*

## Ireland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. You never visited Ireland in your short stay in this world, but we know that you are always here in sound and in the loyalty of your servants. On this special occasion of remembering your kind descent to this world, we beg the intelligence to hold you always in the center of our Society, and to push on, as you did, to carry further your vision for a house in which everyone can live. Please accept the following as an appreciation of your insurpassable work, especially in this small, segregated, and narrow-minded land.

The fools are fighting still in Northern Ireland,  
—a judge bomb-blasted to death a day ago  
in another false-ego expression of power—  
and, if they had it,  
would a thousand years bring change?  
They have talked of short-range solutions,  
pursued them like paupers,  
picking up patched clothes from bins,  
and even the fanatic religionists have failed  
despite their fervor.  
Along with pumped-up politicians  
they have become the butt  
of every Irishman's jokes.  
The children will not listen anymore,  
nor swallow the spoon-fed doctrines  
that they teach—  
they see the hypocrisy in those  
who drink their beer and eat their meat  
before they raise their blood-stained hands to preach.  
Amid all this there is a plan  
to bring humanity back to man.  
Your books are spreading fast to every home  
as you so potently desired;  
like secret weapons  
they are waiting to be fired at the  
heart of diseased society.  
People are turning to them, in their room,  
or school, university, or some backstreet bookshop,  
when in utter confusion they open up a page  
and see truth explained in clarity.  
The grave tone of transcendence  
uncompromisingly presented  
wins the hearts of sincere souls  
and draws them from their many holes  
to partake in this great spiritual siege.

The Irishman O'Grady talked with you—  
we read it in the SSR—  
and you gave such simple but solid answers  
that any honest man could see—  
one who is transcendentally situated  
is "equally disposed to everyone,"  
you quoted, and we see its relevance  
here and how, when practically applied,  
it provides the only means  
for many kinds to mix together  
and see beyond diversity.  
"Unity in diversity," you called it.

Now, as ISKCON is growing worldwide,  
the need is greater to hear from you  
so we may set example for the world.  
We have seen internal strife  
that like a wave would wash away our  
good intentions by causing schisms  
and duality and by so doing  
blemish the blossoming flower  
that you so carefully cultivate.  
As you said to O'Grady,  
a disciple who speaks the message unchanged  
is bona fide.  
And by your kindness and good training  
there are many such souls  
who hold the reins of our racing chariot,  
steering it on course,  
lest we fly off from the  
laid-out path  
to a spiritual death–  
reminiscent of the major Christian church.  
These stalwart souls,  
true spiritual masters,  
save us from confusion  
set upon us by others who dare  
to wear your crown  
without your stature or spiritual greatness.  
They cannot fill your fathomless feet,  
which have walked ever and only for Kṛṣṇa.  
They beg us bray their glories  
as if it were the same  
as serving you.  
As you said (again to O'Grady),  
God, by definition, is one who need  
not take lessons from others,  
but we must work together  
and show willingness to hear and  
accept a higher argument when it comes.

We pray that we can carry on,  
in all insignificance,  
and bring people to your feet  
by allowing you to channel your  
unmotivated love through us.

Please stay and guide our projects  
so that when people come to our restaurants  
they will be affected positively,  
and when they visit our island  
they will be convinced  
we are carrying on as you desired,  
that they may be inspired to somehow  
lend a hand and help push on  
this needed knowledge in this vacant world.  
At the end of the day, all arguments past,  
the mature souls will stay  
growing together,  
retracting, reforming, trying to improve,  
and see beyond it all to unity  
when the Deity doors open  
and every head bows to the one  
Supreme Lord and chants His name  
as you desire,  
and your smile will mean success.

*(Written by Prabhupāda dāsa)*

*Your servants in the Irish Yātrā.*

## Israel

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to your divine appearance on this earth!

At the darkest time, when Kali is spreading his influence at full speed and is conquering the hearts of all, you arrived from the East like the sun to illuminate the world. You came to the West, where Kṛṣṇa was completely unknown, and awakened the hopeless forgetful souls. No one before had done what you did: delivering the most transcendental knowledge about God to the most fallen.

In one of your purports *(Bhāg.* 4.8.80) you wrote in connection with Dhruva Mahārāja: "This example of Dhruva Mahārāja . . . clearly indicates that a devotee, by his personal devotional service, can influence the people of the whole world to become devotees of the Lord. If there is only one pure devotee in pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, he can change the total consciousness of the world into Kṛṣṇa consciousness."

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are such a pure devotee, and this international movement is the fruit of your pure love of Kṛṣṇa. Single-handedly you started it, and you alone maintain it. Like the kings in Vedic times, you give shelter and hope to all the innocent creatures in the world and force the nondevotees to doubt their own convictions.

You are the embodiment of the four pillars of this movement—preaching, books, purity, and utility. Your preaching through lectures, letters, books, etc., is the essence of our preaching. Your books are the only basis—philosophically and economically. Your purity is the transcendental force that pushes and protects us. And utility means acting according to your instructions.

You created this movement in order to give us the association of a pure devotee, and by your divine association you created us—you constructed within us a new heart, new intelligence, new desires, new goals, and new faith. We always appreciated you like that, but now, taking more direct shelter at your lotus feet, our gratitude increases even more. We are now granted entrance into the treasure house of your lectures, which was somehow less accessible for us before. And we are struck by the simple yet demanding call folded within each word of yours:

"Please surrender to Kṛṣṇa."

So on this most auspicious day, we are begging for your mercy. Please help us to dedicate ourselves completely to your mission, devoid of the desire for profit, adoration, and distinction, for as long as we maintain such an egotistic mentality, we are in danger. You built the foundation of the house, so help us to strengthen the walls by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa sincerely, sixteen rounds daily, following all the principles enthusiastically, and depending on Kṛṣṇa in all circumstances.

*Begging to always remain your servants in Israel,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Guṇa Avatāra dāsa

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Nitya Siddha dāsa / Nitya līlā dāsa / Īśvara Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Dhīra Govinda dāsa / Śuddha-sattva dāsa / Bhakta Yigal / Bhakta Yonathan / Śacī-devī dāsī / Devahutī-devī dāsī / Rādhā-Mādhava-devī dāsī / Mahā Lakṣmī-devī dāsī / Vaiṣṇavī-devī dāsī / Śāntasya-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Noga / Bhaktin Dalit / Bhaktin Efrat / Bhaktin Sigi / Bhaktin Lilach

## Japan

I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who, on August 13, 1965, set sail from Calcutta for the United States to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world.

I am writing this Vyāsa-pūjā offering on behalf of the devotees here in Japan, praying that I may one day become your sincere servant and give up my foolish notions ("I am the enjoyer," etc).

The devotees here go out for *saṅkīrtana harināma* usually six days a week, distributing your books (mainly *Bhagavad-gītā)* in places like Ikebukuro, Shinjuku, Ueno, etc. These places are very crowded and full of passion and ignorance, people marching like ants, thousands of them, from early in the morning until late at night—28,000,000 conditioned souls in Tokyo and the surrounding area. There is so much to do, and *māyā* seems very strong. Our only hope (and our only desire) is to faithfully carry out your instructions. By doing this we are seeing that devotees are coming and surrendering to Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is the supreme controller, and you are His dearmost servant. So we have this connection, but still we are always on our guard against our "eternal enemy."

Please keep us always as your servants, and please let us engage life after life in Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement. We pray for no other desire.

(Written by Kavicandra Swami)

*From your tiny servants in Japan,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Kavicandra Swami / Jagad-vīra dāsa

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Johannesburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This occasion for celebrating your appearance gives us great joy. We can eulogize this great event in countless volumes of books, but what would please you more is that we commemorate this Vyāsa-pūjā homage to a rededication of our lives to strictly following all of your instructions exactly as you have given them to us. We have witnessed so many changes since your disappearance from this material world.

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around!  
It is described in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā,* 7.11-12:

*kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*  
*tāhā pravartāilā tumi—ei ta 'pramāṇa'*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti dhara tumi,—ithe nāhi āna*

**"The fundamental religious system in the age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement. You have spread the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore it is evident that You have been empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa. There is no question about it."**

This description is perfectly stating the obvious to all of us, for you have introduced the *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī has explained that unless one is directly empowered by the causeless mercy of Kṛṣṇa, one cannot become the spiritual master of the entire world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the real *ācārya* who is presenting Kṛṣṇa to everyone by distributing the holy name of the Lord. When the conditioned souls become purified by chanting the names of God, the blazing fire of material existence becomes extinguished.

Now, by your mercy, we are here in Johannesburg, South Africa. We have a good field for preaching here, and our fervent desire is that you will bless us so that we may cooperate amongst ourselves to fulfill your desire to make everyone in this land Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Please help us to weed our devotional creepers so that our plants of devotion will grow strong and true. *Yow* presence is being felt more and more as we put your desires and instructions foremost in our lives. Sometimes the young child will make mistakes, and if the child learns from these mistakes, then this is intelligent. Please help us to exercise our intelligence to always serve you and never deviate from the righteous path.

We are begging to remain your servants birth after birth.

*Your servants,*

## Kaduna, Nigeria

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

What do we know to say about you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

Who are we to talk about you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

What qualification do we possess to associate with your name, pastimes, qualities, and paraphernalia?

On behalf of the devotees in this remote part of the world, we only beg His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda to please shed a drop of his causeless divine mercy upon us through the medium of his present bona fide representatives to keep us in devotional service and push us to preach the glories of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Kṛṣṇa constantly.

It's quite natural that the grandfather is more merciful and kind to the grandson than the father. Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, ocean of mercy, on this special divine appearance day of yours, we beg to remain your menial, insignificant servants life after life after life.

Jai Śrīla Prabhupāda!

(Written by Citraketu dāsa)

*Your servants in Kaduna, Nigeria,*

Citraketu dāsa / Abhimanyu dāsa / Devarṣi dāsa / Balarāma dāsa / Ādi-Keśava dāsa / Viṣṇu dāsa / Śaunaka Ṛṣi dāsa / Premavatī-devī dāsī / Visvakāma-devī dāsī / Bhakta Cletus

## Kitwe, Zambia

**I had a dream . . .**

The President of the United States today welcomed the Prime Minister of India and a large group of ISKCON devotees at the White House amidst growing reports that a new Constitution and Bill of Rights will be formulated using the Bhaktivedanta purports and texts of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* as the basis. Both leaders had audience with Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Americaīśvara at the U.S. Congressional temple with most congressmen in attendance wearing *tilaka* and *dhotis.*

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

Leading diplomats from Europe and Canada have vowed to support the United States' decision to ban all cow slaughter and limit the amount of slaughter of lesser animals with 1995 as the deadline for shutdown of all abattoirs in the U.S.A.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

The Supreme Court, reversing its former ruling, has now banned abortion in all fifty states and opened up the public schools to regular religious instruction using guidelines set up by ISKCON's Ministry of Education in conjunction with other religious representatives around the country. All public teachers will be required to swear an oath to abstain from all intoxicants, gambling habits, and illicit connections with the opposite sex, and to submit to governmental tests yearly.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

The first of a series of newly minted gold coins will go into circulation in the United States and Canada today. Government Treasury spokesmen have announced that as of midnight on Monday all paper currencies will not be acceptable for any transactions. On a lighter note, reports have come in from the campus of UCLA, where a new fad of dollar-bill wallpaper has spread amongst the student population.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

Again from our Washington counter, the U.S. Congress has just passed a bill legalizing polygamy. Despite a strong protest from women's liberation groups, the bill was passed in both houses by a strong majority. The President, who supported the bill, will sign it into law this evening. The call to remove the divorce laws from the federal statutes, however, is not expected to be ruled upon until the next congressional session.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

*A* new Washington monument with a fifteen-foot statue of Swami A.C. Bhaktivedanta Prabhupāda and bronze-plated copies of all his books and translations of Vedic scriptures will be unveiled today in the grounds next to the Lincoln Memorial. The Chief Justice of the United States will give the keynote address, to be followed by a banquet of ten tons of strawberry *halava* and blueberry sweet rice donated for the occasion by the two U.S. corporate giants Ford Motor Company and General Motors.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

The United Nations has formally adopted a new charter today uniting all the former world nations under the rule of one General Council. Ninety-eight percent of the world's nations have accepted the charter and have now made it imperative that the abstaining nations must accept the world authority of the General Council, which is composed of the most spiritually evolved men of our new age.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

Muslim and Christian leaders, after meeting with a council of ISKCON chairmen for three days in Istanbul, have agreed to adopt a joint plan of united *saṅkīrtana.* Twelve locations of selected cities around the globe have been designated where massive glorification of the Lord's holy names is to be performed. The world's most famed symphony orchestras are scheduled to back up the spectaculars, for which twelve ten-acre stadiums have been designed by the best Indian and Japanese architects.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

The recent filming of "Lord Caitanya in Ecstasy" netted the largest profits of any three-dimensional laser holographic film to date. The realism of the film forced many cinema-hall managers to employ extra ushers to deal with numerous incidents of fainting spells and uncontrolled emotions. There are plans for a sequel to the movie, "Lord Caitanya's *Bhāva,"* which will be due for release in March. Profits from the two films will go towards founding Vaiṣṇava academies of art and music around the world.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

Finally, our planet earth has received a wonderful communication via radio-telescopic transmitters in the language of Sanskrit that leading demigods from the higher planets, being pleased with the renovations of our earth, are planning to visit us on the eve of Lord Caitanya's appearance day celebrations in Māyāpur. Lord Viṣṇu has disclosed in a separate transmission that Lord Śrī Caitanya may also come if more than ten million are in attendance at the festival.

*(hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare,*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare)*

*. .* . So I suppose a dream has some reality, but it is manifest if Lord Caitanya has the same dream, and if we dream, certainly the Lord must dream perfectly. And who knows what fanciful creations are crossing the Lord's mind? We do hope and pray that one day our preaching may be effective enough to realize even one divine aspiration.

*Your servants in Kitwe.*

## Korsnasgard

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Your lotus feet are a secure shelter for all those who surrender unto them. The bona fide spiritual master is the captain of the ship that will bring us over the ocean of material nescience, the roaring ocean of repeated birth and death. In the shade of your lotus feet we are safe, safe from *māyā.*

Here we are in Sweden, a materially advanced but spiritually retarded society. You visited Sweden in 1973. Out of your mercy you preached to the professors as well as the drunkards. By your mercy they all got a chance to progress at the Uppsala University, and in your public lecture in Stockholm you addressed the drunkards. You said that even a drunkard would ultimately become Kṛṣṇa conscious if he thought that the taste of the wine was Kṛṣṇa. In the audience were sitting some of those drunkards we meet on the streets of Stockholm. By your grace you even blessed them with an instruction that could immediately help them in their spiritual progress. Of course, you gave many more instructions on that occasion.

You also left all your books for us to live with. They have all been translated throughout the years. And they are being distributed now, and we pray that we will be able to carry on distributing them in the future. Thus your transcendental mercy will continue to shower over this country, and we will also be showered by your mercy. Your mercy is that we are engaged in the transcendental activity of distributing your literature. To do that we have to surrender, and that surrender means that we actually do it. That means to actually sacrifice ourselves in this *saṅkīrtana-yajña.* Thus by your grace we are being purified in our heart and our activities. Our offering to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will be this *saṅkīrtana-yajña,* distribution of your literature. By that everything will become, and is already becoming, very auspicious in this dark age. Your books are the tickets to this ship of yours, ISKCON, which will bring us over this ocean of calamities of birth and death. The tickets are distributed freely. It is just a matter of opening the book and reading the instructions. Those who are fortunate take advantage, and those who are not so fortunate still get purified by the very touch of your books. That is your glory.

We all fall in the dust of your lotus feet and beg to remain your humble servants for eternal time.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the savior of the three worlds.

*The devotees at Korsnasgard and the Hare Krishna Center in Stockholm, Sweden,*

## Lagos, Nigeria

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We are begging to offer our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. You have come into this world and performed your beautiful pastimes until you returned back home to Kṛṣṇa before we had the good fortune to hear of these glorious activities. Nevertheless, we do not feel in any way disadvantaged, because we can still see you through your dear servants.

We have heard about your unique life in India before you went to America. We have heard of the perilous sea journey that you undertook aboard the *Jaladuta* at your blessed age. We have heard about all the trouble you took to establish ISKCON and expand the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya all over the world. We read your books, and we marvel at your unfathomable knowledge. We marvel at your unalloyed devotion to your own spiritual master and your intense love for Kṛṣṇa. Thus we understand you to be a specially empowered emissary of Lord Kṛṣṇa sent to continue the work of Śrī Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī and to save the world.

You have explained in your own purports that this whole material civilization is simply advancing fast towards hell. The living beings who have taken birth here under the influence of the laws of nature have become victims of this dangerous system. But we are fortunate because Your Divine Grace has intervened and pulled us out of the jaws of death, which have taken the form of this evil civilization. You are our real father and the well-wisher of all living entities. You accepted so much hazard just to save the conditioned souls of this world. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are not at all surprised about your great compassion for us, because we can understand you to be the pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. We have heard of similar levels of compassion in Prahlāda Mahārāja and in Vāsudeva Datta.

We belong to the lowest *mleccha* race. Therefore we are completely unfit for any spiritual activity, and we would not normally be accepted in the *āśrama* of the *guru* for any training according to the Vedic tradition. Of course the scriptures do say that even the untouchable is purified by chanting the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa; but we know you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the *ācārya* who came straight to us in spite of our disqualification. You gave the holy names and taught *saṅkīrtana,* and thus you elevated those who were formerly untouchables to the position of *brāhmaṇas* and Vaiṣṇavas. You did this following in the footsteps of your own spiritual master and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, now that you have saved us, it is our duty to take your order and save others and take the name of Lord Caitanya to every town and village. This is your desire. But we remember your prayers when you went to America: "Although my Guru Mahārāja ordered me to accomplish this mission, I am not worthy or fit to do it. I am very fallen and insignificant . . ." So considering this, we do not see any chance whatever for our humble selves. But in the *Markine Bhāgavata-Dharma* we have seen the cue in your own words: "O spiritual master of all the worlds! I can simply repeat Your message. So if You like, You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding." This is our hope. We surrender at your lotus feet, and we beg for your blessings that we may be able to continue your mission. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

*Your children in Lagos,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

## Laguna Beach

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine feet.

You've changed the future of this entire universe for the next ten thousand years, just by coming to the West. *You* came like a timeless traveler, unfamiliar with material contamination. You brought timeless wisdom, boundless knowledge, and unlimited truths for the benefit of a world devoid of self-realization and human values. Since your departure, the responsibility for carrying on your mission has fallen to your followers.

In the history of the world there has never been such an empowered spiritual preceptor as you. You alone have changed the *karma* of this planet. Steeped in sin and ignorance, blinded by envy and lust, the whole world races at breakneck speeds to enjoy, exploit, and consume, gradually suffocating all human qualities. But you have the knowledge, compassion, and the ability to stop it all.

By your powerful divine love of God you descend to deliver the unfortunate. Persons who don't know of your presence on earth or of your movement still receive your mercy by your mystic genius. We pray that we may become your worthy followers and selflessly give that priceless gift, the treasure of divine love, to all. We pray to understand your glories and appreciate what you are, what you want us to be, what pleases you, and thus be empowered by your potency to carry on.

On this occasion of your beloved Vyāsa-pūjā, let all of us recommit our lives to your lotus feet and with new vigor purely follow you with all our hearts, minds, and bodies to create a spiritual revolution in the misdirected lives of a misguided civilization.

*Your aspiring servants in Laguna Beach.*

## Los Angeles

**Invocation**

*so 'lankrsista bhagavan vacamsi me*

**"May the Supreme Personality of Godhead be pleased to decorate our statements."** ***(Bhāg.*** **2.4.23)**

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories unto you, the divine embodiment of Śrī Kṛṣṇa's perfect devotee.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*  
*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

For the ability to properly honor you on this supremely divine occasion, we have first invoked the blessings of the Supreme Lord Himself, as it is His special pleasure to see the worship of His loving confidant, and it is His special mercy that we, ordinary human beings, may be able to execute it adequately. We therefore pray to Him who is glorified by choice poetry, so that our words may similarly be embellished to praise you.

Prabhupāda, as we think of you and remember your innumerable and unfathomable qualities, we are especially struck by your quality of kindness. Your coming to the Western world out of your concern for us was the supreme manifestation of a most extraordinary degree of kindness.

Kirāta, Hūṇa, Āndhra, Pulinda, Pulkaśa, Abhira, Śumbha, Yavana, members of the Khasa races and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, due to His being the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him.

**Purport**: . . . According to the cult of devotion, generally known as the Vaiṣṇava cult, there is no bar against anyone's advancing in the matter of God realization. A Vaiṣṇava is powerful enough to turn into a Vaiṣṇava even the Kirata, etc., as above mentioned. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.32) it is said by the Lord that there is no bar to becoming a devotee of the Lord (even for those who are lowborn, or women, *śūdras,* or *vaisyas),* and by becoming a devotee everyone is eligible to return home, back to Godhead. The only qualification is that one take shelter of a pure devotee of the Lord who has thorough knowledge in the transcendental science of Kṛṣṇa *(Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam). (Bhāg.* 2.4.18)

Just a few years back we had no idea about any of these things. Kirata, Huna, Andhra, Pulinda, Pulkasa . . ., words we couldn't have even pronounced properly, much the less understand. Now we know they mean the Joneses, the Schmidts, the Garcias . . . "we, the people," the lowborn, the addicted to sinful acts. What were our chances, Prabhupāda? Even if somehow or other we would have come across the statements of the *śāstras* or the *ācāryas,* like yours above, "the only qualification is that one take shelter of a pure devotee of the Lord," where would we have turned to?

Apparently, everyone else had dismissed us as hopeless cases. Everyone except for you. Therefore you launched an astounding one-man rescue mission. In your extreme humbleness, feeling always incapable, you would continuously ask for help. But Kṛṣṇa wanted to show the great character and superhuman feats of His pure devotee. You alone had to do it.

It is said that great personalities almost always accept voluntary suffering because of the suffering of people in general. This is considered the highest method of worshiping the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is present in everyone's heart." *(Bhāg.* 8.7.44)

From the external point of view, what were the chances of your success?

Was it a realistic mission, at the age of seventy, with only forty rupees and a trunk made in India full of books printed in India? Was it realistic to travel across the ocean to establish the totally unknown *bhagavat-dharma* as a world religion?

What actually moved you to set out?

Was there any indication that it was a feasible mission?

Was anyone forcing you to do it?

Would Kṛṣṇa or Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta blame you for not trying it at such a ripe age?

Would they have neglected you if not?

As you always pointed out, no one expects such an old man to embark on such an enterprise. At that age everyone retires. Kṛṣṇa and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta could have been sufficiently pleased by the path you had already chosen: renouncing family and social life altogether and taking the order of *sannyāsa* for the exclusive absorption in the service of the Lord. The perfection of such a life could have been very well accomplished by you anywhere in India, even if you would have selected a most comfortable and peaceful environment for spiritual expression. Who could possibly reproach you for this? But just the slightest thought of it terrifies us. What would have been of us?! Hare Kṛṣṇa!!!

To our great fortune, you felt for us; in your absolute freedom, you chose to be supremely kind and make us fortunate. *Guru kṛṣṇa prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bijā.*

... \[A\] full-fledged free soul like Nārada, always engaged in chanting the Lord's glory, is free to move not only on earth but also in any pan of the universe, as well as in any part of the spiritual sky. We can just imagine the extent and unlimitedness of his freedom, which is as good as that of the Supreme Lord. There is no reason or obligation for his traveling, and no one can stop him from his free movement. Similarly, the transcendental system of devotional service is also free. It may or may not develop in a particular person even after he undergoes all the detailed formulas. Similarly, the association of the devotee is also free. One may be fortunate to have it, or one may not have it even after thousands of endeavors. (Your purport to *Bhāg.* 1.6.37)

Such a baffling statement provides us with your conclusive answer: it was only by your sweet will that we were fortunate.

In our ignorance we tend to take it for granted, but when we are hit by flashes of understanding—and, again, these being brought about by your words—we get a glimpse of the magnitude of your kindness. Nothing else brought you to us.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, here in New Dvārakā we feel extremely grateful. You have always been so merciful to this community, for so many years considered by you as your world headquarters. By your personal presence you turned this section of Watseka Avenue into a holy *tīrtha,* establishing a focal point from which your kindness could perpetually be distributed to everyone in Southern, or, rather, sinful, California, by your causeless mercy turned into fortunate California. Now you are here eternally: In your deity. In your room. In your garden. In your temple room. In the Deities you personally installed. In the alley from which we saw you so many times go to your morning walks. And, especially, in your direct instructions to us.

In the summer of 1976 you wrote to us in reply to our Vyāsa-pūjā offering of that year. This letter shows another of your gemlike qualities: all that you say becomes *śāstra.* It also shows how your own words are the best decorations of yourself.

My Dear Sons and Daughters,  
Please accept my blessings. I am in due receipt of your letter dated August 9, 1976 and the enclosed daksina and pictures of the murties in New Dvārakā which I am having framed and put on my wall here in Bombay.  
If you feel at all indebted to me then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me. Of course no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple. In the Bhagavad-gītā it is said: *vyavasayat-mika buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana,* "Those who are on this path are resolute in purpose, and their aim is one." Our only business is to be fixed up in devotional service by pleasing the spiritual master. Those who are not fixed up, they have various lines of action. (Eka means 'one' and bahu means 'many').  
The real ocean of mercy is Kṛṣṇa, and it is the duty of the spiritual master to tell his disciple to come to the ocean and be happy. The spiritual master's duty is to lead the disciple to this ocean. I am trying my best and if you try to follow, surely you will benefit.  
Bhaktivinode Ṭhākura has sung, *kṛṣṇa sei tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite para, tomāra śakati āche. Ami ta 'kaṅgala, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa boli, dhai tava pāche pāche.* "Kṛṣṇa is yours and you have the power to give Him to anyone you wish. I am poor and wretched and running behind you shouting Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa!"  
Kṛṣṇa is unlimited, no one can catch Him, but if someone follows the paramparā. He agrees to be captured. Everyone is afraid of Kṛṣṇa, but Kṛṣṇa is afraid of Mother Yaśodā. That is Kṛṣṇa's special mercy.  
I hope this meets all of you in good health.  
Your ever well-wisher,  
A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami

For us in New Dvārakā, may these instructions remain as the umbilical cord that will always nurture our spiritual life and keep us connected to the source of all spiritual energies, now that we need more strength than ever to be able to continue your mission. O Prabhupāda, we want to become preachers for your pleasure. With your blessings everything is possible. On this joyous day we fall at your feet, praying that you may accept our humble service. Kṛṣṇa is yours, and you have the power to give Him to anyone you wish. We are poor and wretched. We are just running behind you, shouting Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!

(Written by Vīrabāhu dāsa)

*Your servants in New Dvārakā,*
