## State College

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Diversions leading to divisions . . . the strength of *māyā’s* attacks ever forceful . . .the degree of difficulty of Kṛṣṇa's tests bringing the sincere disciples ever more to the "honor roll" of your Society. As the years go on, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we find more and more that the basic ingredient for remaining on our path toward pure love of God is our pure desire to remain fully under the protective shelter of your most merciful lotus feet. And the ever-occurring realization is that this shelter means, in a very practical way, that your most basic instructions must be followed without deviation. On our own we are weak and useless. Some soldiers will fall, but we must come to the understanding that those soldiers who have implicit faith in the instructions of the spiritual master need never fear. For those devotees who are sincerely endeavoring for expanding this *saṅkīrtana* movement, Kṛṣṇa's protection is ever present.

Here on the front lines of college preaching we are simply and humbly praying to your lotus feet that you may give us the ever-increasing strength and intelligence to become more and more intense in our efforts to push on Lord Caitanya's golden era. Please give us the ability to sidestep obstacles and to avoid time-consuming politics and just become more and more expert at cutting through *māyā's* forces. Please give us the pure desire to distribute profusely this Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please give us the full understanding that in order to please you, we must cooperate and follow your order, and that by pleasing you, we please Kṛṣṇa, and by pleasing Kṛṣṇa, the whole universe becomes pleased.

*We beg simply to remain in your humble service,*

Nitāi dāsa / Śacī-devī dāsī / Mohinīmūrti-devī dāsī / Jaya Śacīnandana dāsa / Giridhārī dāsa / Bhakta Steve / Bhakta Andrew / Bhakta Gary / Bhakta Joseph / Bhakta Sunil / Bhaktin Denise / Bhaktin Michelle / Bhaktin Anna / Other congregational members

## State College Gurukula

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Prabhupāda,  
a special gift from Kṛṣṇa  
to save the human race,  
fruit-bearing seed  
to decorate the earth  
with a real desire tree.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you walked amongst us  
free from desire,  
no need for personal glory;  
you have Kṛṣṇa;  
you can save the world

Like a vigilant lifeguard  
perched on a high seat  
to better see  
those in danger  
and rescue them,  
often against their will.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you train your children  
with laving care  
and create an army,  
an able general,  
commander for Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
can we know our fortune  
and become free  
from desire to control,  
working together,  
a bundle unbreakable?

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
like Kṛṣṇa you watch us  
and guide if we ask  
and follow your instruction.  
Otherwise you silently sit  
and watch us struggle to learn.

If we could bury our pride  
and work as a family,  
giving the world Kṛṣṇa,  
you could rest in peace,  
and your work would be done—  
the world would have a house to live in.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
on your order we must,  
with purity—free from pride and envy—  
spread Kṛṣṇa's glory  
to every town and village a  
and make you happy.

*Your humble servants at the State College Vaiṣṇava Academy Gurukula,*

Nirmala-candra dāsa / Lakṣmīmoṇi-devī dāsī / Akūti-devī dāsī / Hari-prasādā-devī dāsī / Annapūrṇā-devī dāsī

**Gurukula**

## Switzerland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

We who are drying up in the desert of material existence look to you for relief. You are the rain cloud of mercy sent by Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

We who are drowning in the ocean of material desires look to you for relief. You are the life preserver thrown to us by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī.

We who are bewildered by our own speculations and by the demoniac influences of Kali-yuga cry out to you—the most perfect representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva—that you empower us to perfectly understand your instructions, to accept them in our hearts, and to bring your most glorious books into the hands of the conditioned souls. Only by the mercy of the devotee can one obtain Kṛṣṇa, and only by your continuous mercy can the fallen souls receive your wonderful books. These books are the torchlight for the misguided men of Kali-yuga. *They* are the rain cloud of mercy; *they* are the life preserver—the life*-giver.* Your books are perfect because they are actually Kṛṣṇa; but they are *most* perfect because they have appeared through your lotus mouth.

Thank you for appearing amongst us. Thank you for *remaining* amongst us. Thank you for walking on this planet for the next ten thousand years. Thank you for being our Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants in the Swiss Yātrā,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Rohiṇīsuta dāsa / Sudarśana dāsa / Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi dāsa / Puruṣottama dāsa

## Sydney

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, let us once again dedicate our lives to your mission. At this time every year, hundreds of your disciples and granddisciples meditate on the instructions you have imparted in their hearts, reflecting how we have fallen short in the past year.

It is to our greatest blessing that you are the foremost follower of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In Atlanta in your arrival address when you saw the Deities of Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Nityānanda Prabhu, you requested us with tears in your eyes to follow Caitanya Mahāprabhu—because He is so kind. Kṛṣṇa, you said, demanded *sarva-dharmān parityajya . . .,* but Lord Caitanya did not ask anything: "He is so kind."

So even though we may have made mistakes in the past year, we are nonetheless very enthusiastic because we know that as long as we cling tightly to your lotus feet and strictly follow your instructions, then only can success be the result.

As the Kali-yuga progresses and the atmosphere becomes more and more surcharged with sinful activities, even the devotees sometimes fall prey to the material energy, succumbing to fault-finding and increased sense gratification, but we are beginning to appreciate your instructions more and more, realizing how you have left us a foolproof system of going back home, back to Godhead. In the beginning we followed your rules and regulations because "Prabhupāda said so," but now, as we are maturing in our devotional service, we are realizing in a more scientific way how the morning program, for example, encompasses the five limbs of devotional service, how *vaiṣṇava-sevā* is so important, and how criticizing our Godbrothers and Godsisters will destroy this movement, etc.

We have learnt many things since your departure, and many the hard way, but one thing that stands out amongst everything is how you are the PERFECT spiritual master. Because you embody the Absolute Truth, your instructions will remain unchanged eternally. If we uncompromisingly follow them even in the midst of great difficulty, then success is guaranteed. In the Tenth Canto, Fourteenth Chapter, verse 8, of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* it is said, "My dear Lord, any person who is constantly awaiting Your causeless mercy to be bestowed upon him, and who goes on suffering the resultant actions of his past misdeeds, offering You respectful obeisances from the core of his heart, is surely eligible to become liberated, for it has become his rightful claim."

Offering our humble obeisances at your lotus feet, we are praying to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to help us also become followers of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, making His *saṅkīrtana* mission our only mission. When we are tasting that *ānandāmbudhi-vardhanam,* then all our "problems" will fade away, and we will all be dancing back home, back to Godhead.

*Your servants in Sydney,*

## Taiwan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet again and again.

Having taken birth in this part of the world at the farthest point west from the most sacred land of Gauḍadeśa, we, the descendants of the Khasa race, hardly had the opportunity to meet Your Divine Grace. Whatever we came to know about you was through your beloved disciples, your books, and lectures. However, you are the embodiment of all the wonderful qualities of the Lord's pure devotee, so the only bar to glorifying a personality like Your Divine Grace is our inability to appreciate those qualities. Nonetheless, we will still try our best to glorify you.

On several occasions you had spoken in regard to the Chinese people. This has made us all feel very intimately connected with you. We remember and worship you in these divine words of yours; and as we share it with others, somehow their heart is touched, and just as we were, they are invariably being brought closer to you.

On one such occasion, your first Chinese disciple recruited from Hong Kong had managed to translate a paragraph from *Bhagavad-gītā* upon your request at Māyāpur. He was then asked to read it out loud in front of you in his Cantonese dialect. After listening to the reading with rapt attention, you lovingly exclaimed, "Isn't it sweet!" We can understand from this incident that you are the great *paramahaṁsa,* expert at extracting only the good element even if it is mingled with filth and foul. It is not at all difficult to recognize the Cantonese dialect to be most unbearable to the ear, yet you considered it sweet. This transcendental perception of yours brought about a soul's complete surrender, dedicating his life to render all your books into Chinese, through which the Chinese race, numbered in billions, will bow down at your lotus feet one day. Śrīla Prabhupāda, how sweet you are!

On another occasion, you remarked that the Chinese will be the last to join, but will be the best. We would like to immediately accept this as a fact, but we know too well ourselves how fallen we are. And this makes it difficult to accept your statement at face value. On the other hand, since we have come to accept whatever you said as absolute, then it must be true. We guess that perhaps it is the most difficult thing to try to convert a Chinese person to theism, so once he is converted, he will remain as such for his whole life. Or for that matter, it is true that generally it is most difficult to ask a Chinese to change his way of thinking, his life, or anything, but once he adopts a new way, it is again very difficult to get him to change over to another one. And for this reason, we may be the best. Or perhaps because Lord Caitanya is *patita-pāvana,* since we are most degraded, low-born, and fallen, we are then the only qualified candidates to receive the Lord's and His pure devotees' special mercy. So, being the recipient of this special mercy by dint of our fallen position, we may become the best. But, these are all relative calculations. Behind and beyond all these is your causeless love and compassion. Yet, simply by these well-wishing words of yours, you have bound us to fulfill your desire. We simply want to be your good sons and daughters, because in this way, you will be ever more glorified for making the impossible possible, namely, the deliverance of the most fallen human beings. Sr3a Prabhupāda, how glorious you are!

Another time, you were asked why it was that everything about the Chinese was just the opposite of the Vedic way of thinking. For instance, the way the Chinese write and the uneatable food they eat. Your answer was that our ancestor had moved from a plutonic planet to the earth planet. Those who are the staunch and conceited upholders of their five-thousand-year-old culture should for their own ultimate benefit accept your absolute statement. Fortunate are those who immediately recognize this truth, for in this way, they will be at once reinstated in the one and only culture—the culture of the soul, the Vedic culture. Thank you, Sr3a Prabhupāda, for your sharply cutting off all our petty attachment to our so-called ancient culture in just one single statement. Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to your bold statement!

Your foremost instruction in regard to preaching in China is, "Let the books first enter, and the preaching field will develop." So, your beloved son Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami has been strictly following and enthusing others to follow your instruction. Here in this land of Buddhaloka, where every step is mushroom and soybean milk, and the *mantra* they chant is *nama amita buddha,* we offer to you our mediocre attempt to glorify you by distributing your transcendental literature. And as a result, we have been able to make some devotees.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are so fallen that we do not even have the syllables or pronunciation for the word "Kṛṣṇa." But we do find the Chinese characters for your name indicating the sound *Pra-bhu-pāda. So,* you are more easily approachable to the Chinese than the Lord Himself. And we have an incessant desire to glorify Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, these are but a few sketches of memory of you which the Chinese devotees particularly relish. And these very much betray our meager position, for whatever bit we have to offer in glorification of you on this day, it also comes from Your Divine Grace. Lastly, we would like to relate to you a *saṅkīrtana* story, written by your granddaughter, as follows, which is an extract from an offering she made to her spiritual master:

"I was trying to convince a man to buy a book. He was not interested very much. He was not interested in me either. But I said, 'My *guru* will give a lecture. Would you mind coming on Sunday?' He could not understand English very well. After the lecture, he pointed at a picture and asked, 'Who is this old man?' 'He is Śrīla Prabhupāda, our founder-teacher, my spiritual master's spiritual master.' 'How many disciples did he have? I mean, like your spiritual master, did he do any preaching work?' 'He has ten thousand disciples. They are all preaching the message of Kṛṣṇa.' 'Ten thousand!' He was astonished. 'How much should I pay if I buy all your books?' " Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to your eternal *saṅkīrtana-līlā.*

*Your servants of Taiwan Yātrā,*  
Yaśomatīsuta dāsa / Hanumān dāsa / Bhakta Shiau-Kuen-Wang / Bhakta Yang Chi-man / Kīrtanāmṛta-devī dāsī / Śrīmatī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Leu yi-hwa / Bhaktin Anna Lo

## Takoradi

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our most prostrate obeisances unto your lotus feet and pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that at any hour of the day or night we may be found gathering up the dust of Śrīla Prabhupāda's lotus feet in a mood of loving devotion.

At every moment, we bow down to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, whose lotus feet are our only refuge. With great joy he reveals to us the teachings of Lord Gaurāṅga, and as he is always engaged in spiritual welfare activities for the benefit of all living entities, he is our ever well-wisher. Prabhupāda, you are the revealer of the truth of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the Western world, the humble servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Ṭhākura, and your eyes are anointed with ecstatic love of God. You have been empowered by Lord Nityānanda to propagate the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the *Bhāgavata Purāṇa.* You are a brilliant moon among the Gaudiya Vaiṣṇavas and the very life and soul of devotees throughout the world. You are very dear to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and are our divine lord and master. You are our ever well-wishing friend, grand-spiritual master and spiritual father. You are the end-all and be-all of our life and are the exalted spiritual master of the entire world.

The places where you walk become holy sites of pilgrimage, and your lotus feet are to be taken shelter of by those who desire to attain the treasure of love of Godhead. By your infinite kindness, the divine name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa has been bestowed upon us, although we are truly wretched and sinful living beings. As all the rivers of the world flow toward the great ocean, so your compassion and mercy flow toward all fallen souls.

We pray unto your lotus feet just as Sanātana Gosvāmī prays unto the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.

*Your insignificant servants in Takoradi, Ghana,*

## Toronto

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious day we sit before you and recollect the precious treasures you have so mercifully given to us. Who can adequately express the indebtedness we all feel to you? You gave soothing spiritual shelter to the confused, the intoxicated, and the hopeless. By your causeless mercy and kindness, the blind were given vision. Not only did you give us vision—you gave us your *darśana.* We were fortunate enough to observe a pure, unalloyed servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa in action.

Through your unceasing selfless devotion to your spiritual master and to Lord Kṛṣṇa, and through your potent Kṛṣṇa conscious discourses and books, you captured our hearts. We became convinced that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead and that we are His eternal parts and parcels.

During your brief but memorable visits with us here, in Remuṇā Dhāma, we personally bathed in your transcendental association and relished every moment of it.

One afternoon we recall trying to plan your preaching schedule. There were professors to meet, newspaper reporters to meet, initiations to perform, and life members to visit. We asked what order would be the most convenient for you. You told us to decide and added that "Preaching means to voluntarily accept any inconvenience necessary for the sake of pushing on Lord Caitanya's mission." This particular remark penetrated deep into our hearts. You never seemed to tire—despite your physical ailings—when preaching opportunities came along. Rather, you drew strength and energy from such selfless engagements. In fact, you once said that the debt one owes to his spiritual master can never be repaid, but one could begin the attempt "by preaching vigorously as I am doing."

We remember one refreshing morning walk with you through a park here in Toronto. One guest asked, "What will happen when you are gone, Śrīla Prabhupāda?"

"I will always be present here with you in my books," you replied.

You remarked to another devotee elsewhere, "If you want to know me, read my books." The first occasion I personally heard you give a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, the only instruction I can remember hearing is, "These books are not just meant for distributing. They are meant for *you* to read."

Your desires are an open secret: Be Kṛṣṇa conscious; remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him; read the books carefully and distribute them profusely; cooperate together to push on and expand the movement, which you struggled to establish; uproot personal ambition; chant Hare Kṛṣṇa offenselessly.

We feel that you have definitely given us more than enough service for one lifetime. We know that we are highly unqualified, yet we beg you to kindly overlook all our inadequacies and continue to keep us at a corner of your lotus feet. Please do not kick us away. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants in New Remuṇā Dhāma, ISKCON Toronto.*

## Trinidad

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, please allow fallen souls like us to glorify Your Divine Grace. You came to us although you appeared old and sick, passing through many difficulties. You brought what no one ever brought before—pure love of Kṛṣṇa.

Without Kṛṣṇa consciousness there is no hope for anyone but to remain, birth after birth, in this material world and suffer. Therefore, you are the most wonderful and glorious person in the entire universe.

Please allow us to remain serving your lotus feet.

(Written by Baladeva dāsa)

*Your servants,*

## Uitvlugt and Essequibo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

As the days roll by,  
Your resuscitation of Kṛṣṇa 's message  
Becomes the time of awakening  
For the introspective man.  
The practical results  
Of Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
Impel a conscientious soul  
To be part of it (ISKCON).

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Your compassion for the world,  
Demonstrated by your intense struggle,  
Was the force that moved our dīkṣājīs and śikṣājīs  
To become branches of the Caitanya tree.  
These exalted personalities  
Are your torch-bearers  
To show the need  
For one to become compassionate.

The man of distress,  
The man of need,  
The man of questions,  
The man of wisdom—  
All can fulfill their desires  
And achieve the quest for perpetual happiness.

Not by a millions dollars nor a mansion  
Can we repay you,  
But by the love  
That can be proven  
By how much we cooperate.

So we pray  
To understand  
How we conjoin hands  
And act as your eternal torch-bearers,  
That the world be guided  
Into the house  
You have built.

(Written by Trivikrama dāsa)

*Your devotees of New Godruma and Essequibo, Guyana,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Vancouver

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The world was dark, and in that place  
chaos had broken loose;  
instead of kaṇṭhi-mālā around their necks  
there hung a noose.  
Bad was good and good was bad,  
Sin taken as dharma  
"And (thought they), we will enjoy!"  
Their prema transformed to kāma.

While fools clutched out for dogs' tails  
and for jewels on top of snakes,  
Human life was being wasted  
through unpardonable mistakes.  
Two-legged camels chewed thorny twigs  
and lived like cats and dogs;  
Kṛṣṇa 's instructions to them  
were like pearls before the hogs.

And so within the soul of Him  
compassion was His mood;  
"Jīvas suffering for want of Me;  
how shall I do them good ?  
I know! I'II send My dearest there,  
and souls he will reclaim;  
besides, in Kali-yuga  
there is nothing but My name."  
And so in line with history  
that saint thus did appear  
who could kill demoniac spirit  
by his kīrtana through the ear.  
The Vaiṣṇavas dance and cry aloud  
for now that time has come  
when Brahmā sings with all four heads  
and Śiva plays his drum.

The devas and apsarās chant,  
their praises fill each ear,  
"Śrīla Prabhupāda's, the greatest soul's,  
appearance day is here!"  
That one who is of vices free  
personified ISKCON,  
on Kṛṣṇa who alone depends,  
like lotus thrives on sun.

Whose enemy was never born,  
a real Vaikuṇṭha man,  
leader of the demons (us!),  
your wish is our command."  
Whose smile kills anxiety,  
the only ray of hope,  
the crest jewel of all Vaiṣṇavas;  
in our dark well, a rope.

Who is hard like a thunderbolt  
and yet soft like a rose,  
He built a shelter for them  
who take shelter at His toes.  
He came "to make gṛhasthas" and  
taught paśus how to live,  
then when śiṣyas caused offenses  
would forgive, forgive, forgive.

Form of beauty, lotus feet and lotus eyes and hands;  
who makes gosvāmīs from godāsa,  
like glass is made from sand.  
A nitya-siddha kṛṣṇa-bhakta  
destroys smārta logic,  
he changed the earth by changing hearts  
while "avatāras" did magic.

A lover of Śrī Kṛṣṇa,  
cutter of Māyādevī's net;  
"Purity's the power"; catch the  
big fish, don't get wet!  
Celibate father of thousands  
at whose feet all masters sit;  
turning duḥkha to ānanda  
and our tamaḥ into cit.  
Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta's only hope  
and of himself he gave  
our movement at three score and ten  
while others dug their grave.  
Servant of the Six Gosāis,  
one with flawless behavior,  
the ungrateful world will one day see  
Our Prabhupāda, their savior!

Teaching us humility  
he moves among those  
who take water rather than milk;  
like a swan amongst the crows.  
He who can never be replaced,  
whose saṅga is wildly sought  
by intoxicated lovers  
of the kīrtana that you brought.

O Prabhupāda, please come to me,  
please come out of my heart  
and appear before your bhakta;  
from my vision don't depart.  
Please fill my minutes, days, and life  
with your sweet lotus face.  
May my vipralambha-sevā  
bring me to Your Divine Grace,

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your holy appearance, we, together with all of your followers throughout the world, beg to fall in the sacred dust of your lotus feet to offer you our most humble obeisances from our heart of hearts. What to speak of ourselves, we think that on this most holy day all created beings in this universe, including the demigods and great sages, must be offering respects unto your exalted personality for the great kindness you have shown to all suffering souls by spreading the wonderfully soothing benediction of Śrī Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement throughout the world. Alas! We can conceive of no festival grand enough to honor you properly on this most sacred occasion.

You left Vṛndāvana. To carry out the order of your spiritual master, you left Vṛndāvana. To preach the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world, you left Vṛndāvana. To save souls uselessly rotting in this material world, wasting their lives in the dark ignorance of sense gratification, you left Vṛndāvana. Although others make vows to never leave even for a day, you left Vṛndāvana. To give Vṛndāvana to the whole world, you left Vṛndāvana.

"You could have easily stayed in Vṛndāvana. After all, it is the most sacred place in the entire universe. Everywhere there are peacocks dancing and crying, green parrots chattering, birds singing their own particular songs, chipmunks scolding, and monkeys creating mischief. But you left this peaceful place where, walking through the streets, one hears the tinkle of *arotik* bells, first from this side, a little further on from that side, a *kīrtana* here, *bhajana* over there, from another place the chanting of ancient hymns. Conches sounding, temple bells ringing out, Vṛndāvana is the town of five thousand temples, where all the residents you see are devotees, with *śikhā, kaṇṭhi-mālā,* and around their hand a small white bag with *tulasī-mālā.* Passing one another as they hurry from one temple to the next, they call out "Jaya Rādhe! Jaya Śrī Kṛṣṇa! Hare Kṛṣṇa! Rādhe Śyāma!" A perfect place to retire at the end of your life to live in quiet devotion. You were seventy years old, "the fag end of my life," you would tell us. It was easy to remain without any bother to go to some foreign land, but you left Vṛndāvana.

Within your breast burned a compassion and love the world has never known. You left your peaceful Vṛndāvana behind. At seventy ("You're too old, Swāmijī") you sailed across the ocean to a land unknown, so completely opposite from your Vṛndāvana you could not possibly imagine. From this holy village of Vṛndāvana you went to New York, the veritable capital of Kali-yuga. In Vṛndāvana beautiful white cows with big brown timid eyes and questioning ears wander here and there through crooked little lanes. Pushing their moist noses in every doorway, no one will refuse a *capātī* or two, a bunch of greens. Here they are worshiped. In New York, they slash their throats and spill their blood upon the floor, chop them up, and serve their warmed-over cadavers on every corner and in between. You left that peaceful Vṛndāvana where Kṛṣṇa used to play with the cows, and went to that horrible place where she is slaughtered and butchered mercilessly.

You left that Vṛndāvana, where the only intoxication is the holy name. Pilgrims wander down the road, swaying back and forth, singing, "Rādhe Rādhe! Rādhe Rādhe!" And not a liquor shop to be seen. And there in New York you also saw people swaying down the sidewalk, but not immersed in *Kṛṣṇa-prema:* an alcoholic stupor dulls their brain. You stepped across them sleeping in the doorway; one had taken so much he passed away. Upstairs in your room you confronted young people, minds racing beyond control, berserk with drug-induced madness. And no one knew the nectar of the holy name.

You left that peaceful Vṛndāvana where every woman is called *mātājī,* mother, out of respect. Draped in *sārīs* covering even their heads, with a brass tray with incense, *kuṇkuma,* coconut, and bananas, they scurry to the temple to worship their Lord Kṛṣṇa. But in New York you saw them hardly dressed at all, commanding no respect but only whistles and catcalls. And there was one street with only naked cinema shows stretching as far as the eye can see.

And there are no peacocks to wail to announce the rising of the sun, but only shrieking sirens shattering still nights. No birds were singing, no temple bells pealing, but only the roaring of engines, the grinding of gears. No conch shell sounding, but car horns, offending the ears. And no one calling out the names of the Lord, but angry cabbies shouting unprintable words. You could have easily avoided all this and stayed in your peaceful Vṛndāvana, leaving us to rot in this hell, but by your causeless mercy you took so much trouble to bring us Vṛndāvana—to bring us to Vṛndāvana.

You brought us to Vṛndāvana, and we wonder how it is you left. We ourselves never want to leave Vṛndāvana. Your mercy will forever remain unfathomable to us—how you left this peaceful, wonderful place just to save such miserably degraded people as ourselves. We can only fall in the dust of your lotus feet in great awe and reverence and pray that one day we will come to appreciate this great mercy, the immense trouble that you undertook to save such undeserving rascals. You left Vṛndāvana to give Vṛndāvana to the world, and you made so many Vṛndāvanas all over the planet.

Then one day you left us in Vṛndāvana. And you left us Vṛndāvana. You left us the entire spiritual kingdom. But until now we haven't given you a palace in that kingdom. O Prabhupāda, we fall at your feet and beg for your mercy and forgiveness! Please give us the strength to join together to push on your mission and worship you as you deserve.

*The residents of ISKCON Vṛndāvana,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Washington, D. C.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Five thousand years ago, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, spoke the *Bhagavad-gītā.* The *Bhagavad-gītā* is Lord Kṛṣṇa's transcendental "position paper." As your disciples, we should all study it daily and thus worship Kṛṣṇa by our intelligence. In Chapter Sixteen Lord Kṛṣṇa very perfectly describes my position before I met you.

"Pride, arrogance, conceit, anger, harshness, and ignorance—these qualities belong to those of demoniac nature, O son of Pṛthā . . . Those who are demoniac do not know what is to be done and what is not to be done. Neither cleanliness nor proper behavior nor truth is found in them. They say that this world is unreal, with no foundation, no God in control. They say it is produced of sex desire and has no cause other than lust. Following such conclusions, the demoniac, who are lost to themselves and who have no intelligence, engage in unbeneficial, horrible works meant to destroy the world. Taking shelter of insatiable lust and absorbed in the conceit of pride and false prestige, the demoniac, thus illusioned, are always sworn to unclean work, attracted by the impermanent. They believe that to gratify the senses is the prime necessity of human civilization. Thus until the end of life their anxiety is immeasurable. Bound by a network of hundreds of thousands of desires and absorbed in lust and anger, they secure money by illegal means for sense gratification. The demoniac person thinks, 'So much wealth do I have today, and I will gain more according to my schemes. So much is mine now, and it will increase in the future, more and more. He is my enemy, and I have killed him, and my other enemies will also be killed. I am the lord of everything. I am the enjoyer. I am perfect, powerful, and happy. I am the richest man, surrounded by aristocratic relatives. There is none so powerful and happy as I am. I shall perform sacrifices, I shall give some charity, and thus I shall rejoice.' In this way, such persons are deluded by ignorance. Thus perplexed by various anxieties and bound by a network of illusions, they become too strongly attached to sense enjoyment and fall down into hell. Self-complacent and always impudent, deluded by wealth and false prestige, they sometimes proudly perform sacrifices in name only, without following any rules or regulations. Bewildered by false ego, strength, pride, lust, and anger, the demons become envious of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is situated in their own bodies and in the bodies of others, and blaspheme against the real religion. Those who are envious and mischievous, who are the lowest among men, I perpetually cast into the ocean of material existence, into various demoniac species of life. Attaining repeated birth amongst the species of demoniac life, O son of Kuntī, such persons can never approach Me. Gradually they sink down to the most abominable type of existence." *(Bhagavad-gītā,* 16.4, 16.7-20)

So this was my fate, but somehow or other I came in contact with you and was able to hear from you. Because you were *nivrtta-tarṣaiḥ,* your words were *bhavāuṣadhi,* the right medicine for us. Thus you personally came to America (and went all around the world) to save us.

*anye tv evam ajānantaḥ*  
*śrutvānyebhya upāsate*  
*te ’pi cātitaranty eva*  
*mṛtyuṁ śruti-parāyaṇāḥ*

**"Again there are those who, although not conversant in spiritual knowledge, begin to worship the Supreme Person upon hearing about Him from others. Because of their tendency to hear from authorities, they also transcend the path of birth and death."** ***(Bhagavad-gītā*** **13.26)**

You told us that if we at all feel indebted to you, then we should preach vigorously, as you did. For so many years together I have been praying to actively remain preaching your mission as an attempt to repay you. But now I am thinking that I must also become *nivrtta-tarṣaiḥ* to become perfect in book distribution. Only by living your books purely can I move conditioned souls to study your books seriously. If I am plagued by *kāma, krodha, lobha, mada, matsarya,* and *moha,* how will they be deeply moved to read these books with great attention? They will see me as just another fanatical evangelist. It is stated in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (2.2.37),

*pibanti ye bhagavata ātmanaḥ satāṁ*  
*kathāmṛtaṁ śravaṇa-puṭeṣu sambhṛtam*  
*punanti te viṣaya-vidūṣitāśayaṁ*  
*vrajanti tac-caraṇa-saroruhāntikam*

**"Those who drink through aural reception, fully filled with the nectarean message of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the beloved of the devotees, purify the polluted aim of life known as material enjoyment and thus go back to Godhead, to the lotus feet of Him, the Personality of Godhead."**

So for me to enliven others to *śravaṇa-puṭeṣu sambhṛtam,* to fill themselves up with the nectarean messages of Kṛṣṇa, I must do it myself.

So I am approaching you like a beggar on this all-auspicious day of your appearance to pray that you, and all the holy Vaiṣṇavas who may be reading this book, will bless me with pure devotional service so that I may perfect my distribution of your books. This is my humble way to repay you. Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura prays, *Kṛṣṇa pitā, Kṛṣṇa mātā, Kṛṣṇa prāṇa-dān:* "Kṛṣṇa is my father, Kṛṣṇa is my mother, Kṛṣṇa is my life and soul." So I think that it is safe to say that since the Supreme Lord is always situated in acts of sacrifice, and you are always with Kṛṣṇa, therefore reading your books and distributing your books and chanting the holy name is my father, mother, and life and soul. Please protect me from *māyā.*

(Written by Vijitātmā dāsa)

*Aspiring to always remain with you,*

## Williamsburg

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear ŚrīIa Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā we, the devotees of New Kulīnagrāma, offer our obeisances and our very selves at your worshipable feet. All glories to you! All glories to your dedicated disciples who have brought to us the unadulterated message of the cult of Lord Caitanya in the form of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement!

Your appearance here on this planet is irrefutably the only redeeming factor in this age of Kali. As we surrender ourselves to you instructions, we realize how Kṛṣṇa meant to glorify His devotee—you, our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. How well He has used you!

You are always transcendental, and your ISKCON movement is completely spiritual.

*Golokera prema dhana harināma-saṅkīrtana—*you have declared that we shall have our ISKCON in the spiritual world. This cannot be understood by mundane thinkers, only by those who have surrendered to your instructions, only by those who have unreservedly dedicated their lives to propagating Lord Caitanya's message of *saṅkīrtana.*

You have exemplified that the *Vedas* are eternal. You have made the import of the *Vedas* applicable in today's degraded society by giving us the holy name, accompanied by the transcendental sounds of *mṛdaṅgas* and *karatālas.* You gave everyone the benediction of the holy name and encouraged the people in general that rules and regulations would come later. You have given us transcendental literatures to inspire future generations, *varṇāśrama* communities to present God-conscious morality and self-sufficiency, *gurukulas* to train our children along the perfect path of going back to Godhead, and, even more essentially, you have revived the ability in us to glorify Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa through transcendental festivals. You have taught us to approach and worship Him through His manifestation of the *arcā-vigraha.* As Kṛṣṇa is limitless, we, being His pans and parcels, can aspire to unlimitedly serve Him and His pure devotees.

All this and more is possible through you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the master preacher. We have full faith that when we have truly surrendered, your mercy will bounce us back to Kṛṣṇa's abode. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

(Written by Candrāvalī-devī dāsī)

*We beg to remain your servants at New Kulīnagrāma, Guyana,*

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## Australasian BBT

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Let us offer our most respectful obeisances.

It is because of you and you only that we have any hope of purifying ourselves from the ugly afflictions of material conditioning.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by remembering you we know what to do, how to act, and how to deal with adversities.  
By remembering you we know how to behave like *vaiṣṇavas* and relate to each other as *vaiṣṇavas.*  
By remembering you we know how to make devotees, not drive them away. And we know how to offer encouragement when our Godbrothers need help.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, by remembering you we can defeat the insidious infiltration of impersonalism, which you fought so hard to destroy.  
By remembering you we can govern ourselves.  
By remembering you we become inspired to go forth and break new ground in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.  
By remembering you we have strength, we have purpose, we remain humble.  
By remembering you we can develop all good qualities.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, by remembering you we can remain fixed and not be swayed by flowery verses or Utopian ideas of how we think things should be.  
By remembering you we have a perfect example to follow.  
By remembering you we ask ourselves, "How did Prabhupāda deal with this situation? How did Prabhupāda deal with this devotee? What did Prabhupāda want?"  
By remembering you we become masters, not servants, of the material energy, and servants, not self-asserting masters, of the spiritual energy.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, by remembering you we remember your instructions. By remembering your instructions we remember that our primary duty is to distribute *YOUR* books.  
By remembering you we know we *must* remit to your BBT. And that always gives a fair indication of how much you are remembered.  
By remembering you we will learn how to read your books. Learning how to read means that when you state something, it is to be taken *As It Is.*  
By remembering you we should want to fulfill your dream to build a city in Māyāpur.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your instruction is to read your books. By reading your books we will remember you and see things in perspective. You are perfect, and we are not. We can only try our best, according to our own frail abilities, to spread your message and invite everyone—regardless of cast, creed, color, social, political, or any material identification or idiosyncrasy—into the house you labored so hard to build.

(Written by Bala dāsa)

*We beg to remain humble servants of your BBT,*  
Nareśvara dāsa / Jyotirmaya dāsa / Gopamātā-devī dāsī

## North American BBT

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**"We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being."**

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Of all conceivable offerings that we can hope to present to you, the greatest would be that we fulfill your order that we cooperate together, so that the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness can proceed on a massive scale. This is clearly your dearest hope, the fulfillment of which would be the basis of any real value that might come about as results of any of our other undertakings in this lifetime.

But this cooperative spirit for preaching seems to be a rarity among rarities. "Cooperation" by definition requires sacrifice or the offering of some degree of personal determination for the furtherance of a broader, extra-personal cause; similarly, long-term and consistent preaching work demands significant ongoing personal sacrifice (*yajña*). A daunting spectre indeed is this historically renowned and now unavoidably manifest requirement that individual and familial self-interests be subordinated to the will of God, and thus we are finding increasing difficulty in enlisting soldiers and officers into your preaching army.

For ourselves, we must follow in your footsteps to the furthest extent attainable at each moment, according to our current situation and in harmony with the standards of *guru-sadhu-śāstra.* This course will be our personal salvation, and we will certainly preach, directly and indirectly, and display the glories of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to those whom we encounter along the way.

However, your footsteps lead us farther still: you have fanned within us the burning urgency of Śrī Caitanya's limitless compassion for the innumerable innocent souls, who are incessantly and unwittingly suffering and causing suffering within this dark age of confusion. They are misled and cheated by the "pimps" of materialistic civilization, namely, hocus-pocus *sahajiya* religion, mundane science, careerist education, industrial economics, and morally-bankrupt special-interest political institutions. This is made possible by the lack of transcendental knowledge and, therefore, by their lack of knowledge of followers of Śrī Caitanya. Indeed, our very *raison d'etre* is to be found in the mission of preaching for the benefit of such unfortunate souls. And so we find that this sense of preaching urgency with which you have so mercifully cursed us begets impatience with the snail's pace of personal salvation and incidental preaching. Is this impatience, this inner conflict, the lot of each generation of preaching followers of Śrī Caitanya? If we reject this impatience as merely a symptom of our less-advanced spiritual condition, do we run the risk that we are in fact thereby dropping back from the realm of transcendent aspirations into the less-discomforting province of mundane religiosity, albeit of a rarified species? We believe so ...

Yet, upon accepting as appropriate this gnawing impatience and thereupon becoming determined ourselves to revive the preaching spirit and the preaching mission, we find that we are confronted by numerous questions to which we should, but do not, know the answers . . .

—How shall the Kṛṣṇa consciousness society be conceived and ordered so as to encourage and enthuse its members and well-wishers to engage in the preaching *yajña*?

—How are we to go about institutionalizing within the Kṛṣṇa consciousness society the fact that spiritual leadership in this Kali age is entirely a matter of direct involvement in preaching? And, collaterally, without unduly offending those who think otherwise, how shall we establish that even significant accomplishments in any other fields (for instance, temple management, publishing management, political, legalistic, artistic, or literary endeavors, etc.) are not sufficient to transform a man into a spiritual leader of all other men?

—Considering, on the one hand, your indisputable stressing of book distribution as the cornerstone of our preaching mission, and, on the other hand, the fact that many of our centers' book distribution programs are currently either nonexistent or are severely debilitated by the high manpower costs of maintaining the physical buildings and their residents, should we now give serious thought to give up the more expensive facilities in favor of more directly fulfilling your order that we must be, first and foremost, preachers and distributors of transcendental literature? Are we correct in concluding that a world saturated with millions of transcendental literatures distributed by us is a far greater bequest to leave for the future generations of preachers than a bunch of buildings inhabited by dried-out old codgers with lofty reminiscences?

In the coming months, we must consider, debate, and decide numerous complex questions such as those above, and, most importantly, we will have to translate those decisions into actions. We cannot neglect any of this if we are to have any hope of successfully manifesting the cooperative preaching mission that you have demanded of us. This Kali age does not favor even rational, analytical thinking, what to speak of the further necessary elements—careful and dispassionate discussion / debate, mature and comprehensive decision-making, and the many modes of action that issue therefrom. At all of these stages, the forces arrayed against us are formidable and seemingly insurmountable. Our decisions and actions in the coming months in the face of the barriers that we now confront will most likely determine our own and ISKCON's fate, in terms of effectiveness on behalf of Śrī Caitanya's mission, for the duration of our lifetimes.

But, alas, at this crucial crossroad, at this threshold where we now stand, we realize ourselves to be very small, very weak, and incapable of sustaining the necessary determination through the struggle that is before us. We are entirely dependent upon our relationship with you and so can only turn to you for help. We pray that somehow we will always be enabled to know your will in all situations and that we will always be reminded of the specifics of our eternal indebtedness to you. Your Divine Grace has awakened us from amongst the living dead, and you lent us your eternal eyes for just an instant. Now we will have no peace until we know that you are pleased with us. Please tolerate our nitwitted follies and help us to be of use to your mission and to the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya.

(Written by Mahendra dāsa)

*Your faithful servants of The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)*

## Portuguese BBT

Quietly listening to your words  
during class,  
watching your exquisite glasses  
resting inside  
your Gītā.  
Inside your purports  
the whole philosophy  
of Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
magnified a million times  
by your simple and deep  
surrender to your Gurudeva.

So many sweet pastimes  
from your heart  
entering the earholes of the fortunate.  
Your dearmost Kṛṣṇa  
is wherever you are  
softly moving the camara  
for His pleasure.

Here we are again,  
humbled by the stength of our own misdeeds.  
Foolish children ...  
When are we going to join you  
in fact?  
When are we going to stop  
this whole childish affair?

Eighteen minutes,  
eighteen days,  
or eighteen lives ...  
May we all just sit down  
and read your instructions.  
The real nectar of life  
is pouring down  
from this one full moment  
of love for you.

Only you gave us real love,  
pure love of Godhead.  
May we all just go out  
and preach your name,  
your holy name around,  
and give real love  
to each other  
and trust each other  
as we trusted you,  
and see Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
blossom everywhere.

(Written by Mahākāla dāsa)

## Spain BBT

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. On this day we are once again fixing our minds on you to recall your greatness, compassion, wisdom, humility and love and rededicating ourselves as your followers and disciples. In writing some words of glorification, we have to sift through and decide which of your noble attributes it would be most appropriate to briefly praise and describe.

At this time, I am recalling the qualities that most impressed me when I first saw you. There was your cutting honesty, your complete lack of all pretension, your sharp sense of humor, and, above all, the revolutionary zeal that came through so strongly in your spoken and written words. Immediately it was apparent: here is a man who will never compromise with a corrupt and hypocritical society; here is someone actually capable of sweeping away the bitter competitive endeavor that is the overriding characteristic of Western culture.

As a young man, I easily empathised with such ideals; but, somehow, with the onset of middle age, it seems a little harder to maintain that revolutionary, missionary spirit. The iniquities of modern society have not gone away—governments in both East and West are still dominated by wicked men, racial hatred still produces cruelty and oppression, the larger nations still threaten and bully the weaker, the poor still starve while the rich seek only to enjoy—and we know that your teachings are the only solution. With your uncompromising philosophical instructions, you have shown the only solution. Economic theories you described as "worthless," teaching us that only a change in the heart is a meaningful change.

Therefore, on this special day, we are praying to you to give us strength. The insidious idea that this Western society has some value is a threat to our firm resolve. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the strength to resist the temptation to compromise. Please give us the ardent desire to continue the endeavor to bring about a revolution in the impious lives of the people in general. Please help us to remain convinced that this Western culture, with all of its adornments, must be rejected as utterly useless. Let us thereby unhesitatingly immerse ourselves in the eternal beauty of Śrī Kṛṣṇa's culture, as brought by Lord Gaurāṅga and Your Divine Grace.

(Written by Naimiṣāraṇya dāsa)

*Your servants at Spanish BBT and C.P.S.,*

Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Swami / Vīrabāhu dāsa / Amṛta-devī dāsī / Caitanya-Nṛsiṁha dāsa / Govinda-bhāsya dāsa / Kāmāri dāsa / Naimiṣāraṇya dāsa / Niścintya-devī dāsī / Ṛṣabhadeva dāsa / Vaikuṇṭha-guṇa dāsa

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead Magazine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We would like to begin this Vyāsa-pūjā offering by recognizing the inspiration and sense of dedication that you have infused into *Back to Godhead* magazine. On the order of your *guru Mahārāja* you created the perfect vehicle for stimulating thoughtful persons to consider the necessity of spiritual life and to keep the members of the greater ISKCON community in contact with the transcendental message of Lord Caitanya, even when they live hundreds or thousands of miles away from the nearest ISKCON temple. Because of your example, we are determined to assist you in producing *Back to Godhead* month after month, year after year, without interruption.

Because you have given such clear instructions as to what you want *Back to Godhead* to be, we do not suffer from a lack of identity, as some might claim. Instead we suffer largely from insensitivity and incompetence in following your lead as we strive to make *Back to Godhead* the best-known spiritual journal in the world. Somehow you are able to speak to every audience in every context, and we must also master this art. In an effort to perform this service more perfectly, we will strive to concentrate on the following objectives:

1\. That all members of the *Back to Godhead* staff will strive to abide by the spiritual principles that you have set down, not the least of which are the following of the four regulative principles, the chanting of sixteen rounds of the *mahā-mantra* daily, and the endeavor to live continuously in the association of devotees.

2\. That *Back to Godhead* is understood to have been created by you so that all of your disciples and followers can preach through the written word, illustrations, and photographs. As such, every attempt will be made to encourage a maximum number of devotees to contribute well-written articles and nicely executed illustrations to the magazine for publication.

3\. That *Back to Godhead* shows the real activities of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Regular readers of the magazine should feel such intimate contact with the *saṅkīrtana* movement that they will not be swayed by any false propaganda emanating from the mundane media.

4\. That *Back to Godhead* should become a truly vital communication link within the larger devotee population as we all struggle to apply the philosophy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to the problems of modern life.

5\. That the primary objectives of *Back to Godhead* magazine will be to encourage readers to read your books, attend ISKCON temples, and dedicate their lives to achieving the purposes listed inside the front cover of every *Back to Godhead* magazine.

6\. That *Back to Godhead* never becomes the voice of any faction and never promotes separatism within ISKCON. We will speak strongly in our fight against materialism, but never without carefully weighing the validity of our argument in terms *of guru, sādhu, and śāstra.*

7\. That every effort be made by the members of the staff to hear and communicate with devotees throughout the society to understand their desires for the production and distribution *of Back to Godhead.*

8\. That every devotee is encouraged to distribute *Back to Godhead* in whatever way he or she can and that *Back to Godhead* be made readily available to the people of every country in the world, first in the English language, and then in every other language.

We are aware that these are towering objectives. But, as our founder-*ācārya*, you have never encouraged us to be faint-hearted. Please give us the strength to fulfill your mission.

Your unworthy servants at *Back to Godhead* magazine.

## ITV

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Our humble obeisances to you, and our eternal gratitude for your causeless mercy towards us. We aspire to be your menial servants and sincere devotees. When we read about your activities and qualities, when we study your books, and when we remember your association, we become inspired to preach, to assist in the work of Lord Caitanya, and to take shelter of the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Certainly at the present time, the devotees and indeed the whole world is in need of the guidance of such a *paramahaṁsa* as yourself. We feel bereft of leadership, drifting further and further away from the mood of pure devotion and surrender to Kṛṣṇa, which will deliver us from this hellish existence in the material world.

On this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, especially, we pray to you that we may always remain in the shelter of your mission, and that our humble efforts to serve you may find your favor.

*Your servants at ITV,*

Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa / Siddhānta dāsa / Ranjit dāsa / Ādi-puruṣa dāsa / Padmalocana dāsa / Bhakta John / Bhakta Jorge.
